Where it reaches you: people's thoughts (the fortnight's news pulls by its freshness and size, a big story the first three days like you standing close by; about one thought in ten in all, one in five of a quiet day's idle ones; the everyday talk only half the time; a person who's said all they have about a story moves on; their own country's news counts double: a ranch hand thinks of Wilcox, a Saint Denis dock man of the Brazos and the hurricane); Talk's "What's the news?" (seven times in ten, nine in ten when something big broke in the last three days, ahead of the town's talk of you) and the typed news question; the talks between two people; somebody reading the paper aloud on the street; a clipping of the week's paper in the reader every seven days (the last four kept); a word on the ticker, "In the paper", the morning something talked of breaks; and the town paper's own item for the biggest. All behind `[World] HistoricalAccuracy` as well. Testing row "The almanac: the day's news and a paper to read"; F7 `TEST almanac:`. Not yet seen in game.

## 0.7.21 (2026-11-04)

- **A broken wagon wheel** (`[Encounters] WagonWheel`, on, `WagonWheelChance` 20; settings version 245; roadmap 32, built on the probe's answer). By day on the roads, once a day, a wagon ahead of you and out of view loses a wheel and stops, the driver down beside it. Within 25 m he calls to you. Stop on foot within 3 m of the wagon for three seconds and you set the wheel with him: his thanks, his liking for you up, and he climbs back on and drives on. Ride past and once you're 40 m gone he calls after you to send word; reach any town within thirty game minutes and the word is sent (a notice; the smith goes out). Nothing is spawned: the wagon and its driver are the game's own, handed back; a hint on the bar says what to do when he calls. Testing row "A wagon ahead loses a wheel (the scene)". Not yet seen in game.
- **Three from Lex's first session with the build** (2026-11-04). The Around town box now stays up the whole time you're in a town, with the regard line alone when there's nothing new. A lawman at the top of the bar is a friend: he wears the dot and comes to your aid against anyone but the law. A lawman who already had a body in his arms when RealNPCs sent him for it no longer loses the carry (he stood with it in his arms); the carry is RealNPCs' from there, and whenever a carry ends early the body is set down the game's way instead of a bare clear.
- **The town's regard of you, in the Around town box** (`[Display] TownRegard`, on; settings version 244; Lex, 2026-11-04). While you're in a town, a line under the box's title says what it makes of you: a killer here, a peacemaker, or nothing held against you, with the killings of yours it knows of this week and your kindnesses. When you come into a town, and whenever that changes, an item says more: the town's mood, how many of the people you know here are afraid of you, fond of you, or holding something against you, and the latest talk about you that has reached the town. The box stays up while you're in a town with the whole of it, wrapped; the item also says how many people you've met whose town it is, and counts the ones about town (within 120 m) for who is afraid or fond. F7 `TEST regard:`.
- **A full bar makes a friend, afraid or not** (Lex, 2026-11-04: Margaret greeted to the top of the bar wore no dot on the map, because the town knew him for a killer and the friend rule gave nothing to anyone afraid of you). Now someone whose bar is at the line (`[Encounters] AlliesLiking`, 90) or above is a friend, and wears the dot and comes to your aid, even if they're afraid of you; a grudge or hate still stands in the way. The log says once a body why a full bar made no friend (`FRIENDS <name>: the bar at 100 and no friend: ...`).
- **The brave stand up** (`[World] BraveStand`, on; settings version 243; Lex, 2026-11-04, the Valentine saloon: after a man was beaten to death, "several NPCs freeze up or get stuck because they seem to be too scared"). The bravery setting trades the game's running for the game's cowering, and a cower beside a body looked like freezing with fear. Now a brave townsperson found cowering is stood up by RealNPCs: they hold their ground on their feet for twenty seconds, then walk off, then are the game's own again (a minute before any second stand-up); never someone sat or at a spot, never someone another feature has. Said plainly: a brave one stood up doesn't report the killing while they're ours; the others near do.
- **Review fixes, items 20-27** (gunpoint, group acts, returning neighbours, the epilogue, memory, the waking, the wagon probe, side by side). The beaten man now walks off with his friend after the close round, and no close round is tried on a man knocked out and held down; a neighbour back off the train gets a body of their own sex (their own model when one is free; a changed look is said when not), the ledger counts the return, and what the dead held against you is kept for RealNPCs' own residents too; the epilogue's carried people are dated on John's clock (they aren't first in line to be forgotten) and the moved left eight years ago ("eight years after leaving it" when they come back); someone who defied you or backed away at gunpoint isn't grateful when the gun comes down, and the game's own aimed-at response can't retask them mid-step; the wagon probe reads the wheel at its readings, not in the native's frame, and runs under its own guard; the waking's F7 counters count the act, not the look; a partner taken away from a fight is busy for the walk.
- **Side by side with other mods** (`[World] YieldToDying`, on; settings version 242; roadmaps 29 and 34). Nothing of RealNPCs' goes on anyone the game has dying: no line of ours over their dying sounds, no shove, fall or knock-down of ours, no move or clip of a street scene's on a dying man, and a scene's end never clears a dying man's tasks, so a death animations and sounds mod's deaths play as they are; RealNPCs still reads every death (the ledger, the grudges, the kin). Arthur's own voice has his gunfight calls now (the game's own recordings, by name) when someone comes at him with a gun, never over a line already playing on him, so the Cut Dialogue mod's lines are never talked over. Both readmes say the two run alongside. The in-game check with the mods installed is Lex's.
- **A wagon's wheel off, the probe** (roadmap 32's first step; the Testing row "A wagon's wheel off"). The row takes an ambient wagon with a driver out of view within 150 m, breaks one wheel with the game's own draft-wheel native, reads the game's answers every two seconds for forty (does the wheel read broken, does the wagon stop, does the driver get down or run, do the vehicles behind it stand), mends it and reads twenty seconds more, then says in the log, a notice and F7 `TEST wagon:` what it found and what the scene can be built on. The scene itself (the driver down beside the wheel, the family on the bench, the passer-by's choices) waits on those answers; its words are written. The first run showed the draft-wheel native alone does nothing, so the row now tries one way a press, round five: the draft-wheel native with a heavy force after the can-break flags, the plain break-off-wheel native, the wagon taken as a mission entity first, damage at the wheel's own bone, and the body health as a check that natives take at all; a way that reads nothing broken in twelve seconds ends there.
- **The waking's strange acts by nature** (`[WestWorld] WakeByNature`, on; settings version 241; roadmap 41's leftover). Which strange thing someone waking does follows their temper (a hothead gets in a face, the proud preach, a coward keeps behind or tests from arm's length, the cold stare and shadow) and what they feel most (fear keeps its distance, anger confronts, grief turns outward), the roll only breaking the tie; and the one they bother is whoever they feel most about if they're near, else the person they have the strongest tie with, else the nearest. Their friends laugh it off, their enemies curse them. F7 `TEST wake hold:` says how many acts came by nature. Not yet seen in game.
- **Memory that behaves like memory** (`[Mind] PlacesRemember`, on; settings version 240; roadmap 4). Places hold memories: someone who knew one you killed (a tie, or word of the death reached them) thinks of it near the spot, once each; a friend's ground pulls for good, an acquaintance's for a month; whether they can say it was you follows whether the killing was seen. People know you by your horse: anyone who has seen you on it up close knows the horse before the face as you ride up from 18 m out, once a day each. And the town notices your habits: the same place at about the same hour on three different days is a habit, and a known person there thinks of it once. F7 `TEST places remember:`. Not yet seen in game.
- **Review fixes, items 15-19** (the nights after, the criminal class, the saloon's two, the undertaker, the stalkers). The saloon's price is taken once per offer and given back when no night comes of it (called off before the dark, a mission, her gone, the offer lapsed); CasualHookups' own night is written down too (the husband may hear, the price kept); a sweetheart's word isn't spent out of earshot. A theft stays on the tie when the two make small talk since; the "thief" over a head is for thefts the town saw (a pickpocket caught, a mugging), never a lift nobody noticed. The drunk outside: whoever won mocks, whoever lost comes to and asks who won; the tie is noted; a brawl the barkeep stopped is hard words, not blows; the barkeep goes back behind his bar. The carry to the undertaker's gets the time its walk needs; a scene never takes the mourner. The stalker's dismount isn't overridden by his next order and his horse is never deleted under him; a revenant at camp is counted when he appears. A kin's walk called off by a mission comes again tomorrow.
- **The epilogue remembers** (`[World] EpilogueCarries`, on; settings version 239; roadmap 25). The first time John's save is made with Arthur's beside it (john.json next to arthur.json), Arthur's people come over eight years older: most as they were, some married, widowed, moved on (they may come back by train) or dead, by their age and nature; the ties between them come too (never Arthur's own doings); and those who met Arthur think of a man called Morgan when they see John, once each. F7 `TEST epilogue:`. Not yet seen in game.
- **Neighbours who come back, and the dead who remember** (`[World] ReturningNeighbours`, on; settings version 238; roadmaps 36 and 35). Someone who left the town on the train a week ago or more may step off a later one (one in three, by the day), one of the town's own again, with a notice ("...twelve days after leaving it. The west didn't take.") and a friend near doing a double take. And a man you kill keeps what he held against you from before (the latest wrong of yours on him in the month: a threat, a theft, a beating...): the revenant who comes back says so ("First you robbed me. Then you killed me. I've had time to think about the order of it.") and rides out on horseback twice as often. Not yet seen in game.
- **Group acts** (`[Mind] GroupActs`, on, with MindLive; settings version 237; roadmap 39). Acts for several people at once that the mind, and the model when it's on, choose: after a street fight, when two friends of the man who went down are near, the first friend's mind picks between tending him alone, fetching the other and closing round him (a scene of three: the two see him up and walk him off) and leaving it; and an onlooker whose mind says leave takes their spouse or sweetheart within 6 m along, side by side, with a word ("Come away, Martha. It's none of ours."). A crowd gathering was already the Director's. Not yet seen in game.
- **Reactions at gunpoint, in stages** (`[World] GunpointStages`, on, with Pleading; settings version 236; roadmap 38). The plea and the game's own hands-up stay the first answer; keep the gun on someone six seconds and they take a second step by who they are: beg for their life, defy you to your face, back away a step at a time, break and run, or freeze with their hands up (the mind's table by temper, drink, what they think of you and the town's bravery; the model's pick when it's on and the mind knows them); at fourteen seconds a third: most who begged or froze break and run, the steady hold, the defiant stand. Someone who ran gets no "spared" when the gun comes off them. Not yet seen in game.
- **Stalkers on horseback, and revenants at your campfire** (`[Stalkers] StalkerHorse` 35, `RevenantsAtCamp` on; settings version 235; roadmap 13). About a third of stalkers now appear mounted: he sits his horse where he turned up, watching, and gets down the first time he moves (the horse is given back out of view). And with West World on, sit at a fire of a night out of the towns and someone you killed who is due back (or due within two days) comes to the edge of the firelight instead of the road behind you, 13-18 m off, with a word from the dark; the stalker's ways after (freezes when looked at, slips away). F7 `TEST stalker:` says both. Not yet seen in game.
- **The dead carried to the undertaker's or the jail, a mourner following** (`[World] BodyToUndertaker`, on, with LawClearsBodies; settings version 234; roadmap 33). When the lawman lifts a body and the undertaker's counter or the sheriff's door you last came out of is within 80 m, that is where he carries it (the short spots off the street stay as the fallback if the walk fails); and someone who knew the dead, within 25 m as it's lifted, walks behind him with a thought over their head and stands over the body where it's set down. Not yet seen in game.
- **The barkeep steps in, and the drunk takes it outside** (`[Scenes] BarkeepSteps`, `DrunkOutside`, on; settings version 233; roadmap 7). In a saloon brawl or rough of two the barkeep comes round the bar as they square up and shouts them down: the hot and the drunk heed him three times in ten, the proud half the time, the rest three in four, and take it outside with no blows; the rest shove him off, he swears to fetch the sheriff, and the fight goes on. A drunk's challenge taken up is really taken outside: the two walk side by side to the saloon's door you last came out of (within 40 m) and the fight is in the street, where the drunk goes down and comes round asking who won. F7 `TEST saloon:`. Not yet seen in game.
- **The criminal class** (`[Scenes] CriminalClass`, on; settings version 232; roadmap 28). People who take to crime keep to it: anyone with a pocket picked, a lift or a mugging to their name in the last thirty days is of the bent, likelier to go for a pocket again whatever their nature or their money (the street's thief reason takes them first), and wears the thief's name over their head the whole month, not the week; a drunk at the bar is rolled with words of its own (a helping hand that isn't; he comes round to it one time in four); after thirty clean days a thief has gone straight and thinks of it once. F7 `TEST criminals:`. Not yet seen in game.
- **Nights together, the next steps** (`[Romance] TrystPrice` 2, `TrystsAfter` on; settings version 231; roadmap 26). Where you go is in her yes: the saloon's own stairs, her home in her own town, the hotel's back stairs (a married woman never her home), or a quiet place past the last lamp where there's no hotel. One of the saloon's women asks her price before her yes (two dollars as it comes; without it, no night). The morning after, if you're still about, she thinks of it. A single woman after two nights expects you back: a word to your face when you've been gone three days, and if it's a fortnight she cools (affection down). A married woman's husband hears of it one time in three (two in three when the saloon saw) and comes to town two to five days on to say it to your face, as the kin do; never violent. F7 `TEST trysts:`. Not yet seen in game.
- **The kin who take up a wrong** (`[Scenes] KinGrudges`, on; settings version 230; roadmap 20). A wrong of yours on a living person RealNPCs knows (a threat, a beating, a theft, a trouble turned the wrong way on purpose) reaches a man of their family, a brother or a father you never met, who comes to the town five to nine days on: he walks up and says it to your face by his nature (the hot threaten, the proud wanted a look, the cold say little, the plain only tell you), the street sees someone face you, a notice names him, and he stays a resident of his town. Once for each person wronged; never violent from our side; a killing is the avengers' business, not his. Testing row "kingrudge" (the nearest known resident's kin, due now); F7 `TEST kingrudges:`. Not yet seen in game.
- **Moods that carry** (`[Mind] MoodsCarry`, on, with MindLive; settings version 229; roadmap 18). The weather wears on the mood of everyone out in it: rain, snow, fog and dust toward sore and flat, a storm keyed up, a quarter of it under a roof, the unsteady feeling it more; so the street's chance meetings lean sourer in the rain and the thoughts read it. And a feeling shown on one face in a crowd is caught by those near who felt less of it themselves, at a share set by who they are (the agreeable and the open catch more; never a feeling kept off the face, never more than half): a fright at a standoff runs through the onlookers, a grief at a graveside spreads. F7 `TEST moods:`. Not yet seen in game.
- **Vices and kindnesses, what was left** (roadmaps 19 and 21; under the existing switches, no settings change). The gambler who lost the rent comes to you for his marker as the debtor and the toothache do (the favour asked: five dollars, said plainly or hinted at, by his nature; Talk lists the mend once he's told you). The Devout and the churchgoing don't drink away bad news on a Sunday (grief for a friend, or a death in the family, is mourned with a glass any day), and a Devout within 10 m of a man drinking on a Sunday judges him in a thought (log `VICE sabbath:`). A friend of someone drinking away bad news stands the house a round with him first, ranked above a glad mood (`cheer`; the man thanks him; the round's tie as before). Not yet seen in game.
- **Despair in the river** (`[WestWorld] DespairWater`, on; settings version 228; roadmap 3). One more way a fully awake person who can't bear it ends it: with the water's edge within 14 m (the game's own water height, about at their feet, the player not on the line) and neither a gun nor poison chosen, 45% walk into the river and keep walking; six seconds in, they go under; never reached, they go limp where they stand, as at the wall. The fall from a height is not built: no safe drop is known. Log `WAKE despair: ... walked into the water / went under`. Not yet seen in game.
- **A line that wakes, and the revived dead seen** (`[WestWorld] WakeLines`, on; settings version 227; roadmap 3). Two more ways the Awakening spreads. Each person at the fourth stage holds one line out of the public domain, fixed by who they are (The Tempest, Poe, Dickinson, Ecclesiastes), and says it as a talk closes with someone tied to them or waking too ("There's a thing I read once, in a play..."); a listener who already suspects (awareness 40 and over) and hears the same line from two different people within three days wakes a stage, at most one a town a day. And someone tied to a man they knew for dead who sees him alive again after a save loaded from before, in view and within 15 m, looks, steps back and thinks of it once ("Buried Abner Tuesday. That's Abner at the rail."), awareness +10 and a stage at 60; the town chews on it for a day. Log `WAKE line said`, `WAKE line`, `WAKE seen-dead`; F7 `TEST wakelines:`. Not yet seen in game.
- **The reload noticed** (`[WestWorld] Ghosts`, on; settings version 226; roadmap 3). Load a save from before, and what the rewind undoes (a killing, a death, a deed, a tie, a link) is kept a week as ghosts, a hundred at most, never rewound themselves; a resident who died after the cut is alive again whether or not the ledger had them (`Resident.DiedAt`). With West World on: a victim of a rewound killing who walks the town again gets one sharp deja vu the first time you come within 12 m ("I've been shot here. I've never been shot.", an uneasy line in their own voice, awareness +10), both sides of a rewound tie think of the quarrel or the kiss that didn't happen for days, the waking open their talk with the morning that happened twice for two days, and the ticker says how many times the clock turned back. Log `GHOST rewound`, `GHOST felt`; F7 `TEST ghosts:`. Not yet seen in game.
- **The voice, and breaking the loop** (`[WestWorld] LoopVoice`, on; settings version 225; roadmap 3). A waking regular's habits are their script; from the second stage of waking they hear it as a voice giving orders at the hour, on the ticker, once a day ("Seven o'clock. The saloon. You'll be at the saloon."; "Ten o'clock. Bed. The lamp out."); from the third they answer it back a breath later ("Not tonight."); at the fourth they refuse it, and now and then break the loop where the habit forbids: a broom on an empty street in the small hours, the church wall at noon, the river's edge no deeper than the knee (the church steps and the wharf from the spot table), one a town a day, with their own thought over it. Once, three thoughts end with them knowing the voice as their own: the centre. After it an ending of theirs may be a reset: the loop closes over them, back to the start, cheerful and blank, and their friends notice for two days. Log `VOICE`, `LOOP broken`, `WAKE reset`; F7 `TEST loop:` lists each near waking regular's routine and whether they're keeping it. Not yet seen in game.
- **Review fixes** (two independent reviews of the week's items, 32 findings): a letter's meet waits a fortnight of days rather than one, is refused from towns you can't ride to, and closes when nobody came; a grieving woman is a woman; a rewound letter never lands its effects twice; one body per writer at a meet; the pad can answer and write letters (X and Y over the reader, A and B in the menus); a loan is kept on the person, not only in the capped log; the drink mirror is paid for; the election is posted anywhere in its week and the town's sides follow the votes cast; a rewind drops events posted after the cut and your part in the ones it undoes; a gun merely pointed at a hanging sends the man inside instead of freeing him; the trial's, the gallows' and the show's key is a setting (`[Keys] EventKey`, J).
- **The town ledger: towns that grow or shrink** (`[World] TownLedger`, on; `ThinStreets`, off; settings version 223; roadmap 11). Each town's ledger is read off the records every day: 50 to start; a killing of yours -3, another death -1, a hanging -4, someone leaving on the train -2, someone arriving +2, a good turn of yours (peace, mercy, a rescue, someone spared, charity) +1, the Wild West show +2, a dance you went to +1. The last month sets the mood: fearful (two or more killings of yours), grieving (three or more deaths), prospering (arrivals and good turns three ahead), else steady; a grieving town's people think in mourning, a fearful one's bar their doors early, a prospering one's complain of prices and new faces. The regulars a town keeps are RegularsPerTown scaled by the index, never below 10 (or the setting). A monthly mention in the paper for towns you've been to ("Valentine has lost twelve souls since March. Two took the train."). A town you never go to has no records and never changes; a rewind needs nothing, the ledger is derived. ThinStreets (off as it comes): a town under 30 has its ambient people thinned each frame, never below 0.6, that town only, never in missions, the count within 60 m logged once a minute so the game's answer can be read. Log `TOWN`; F7 `TEST towns:`. Not yet seen in game.
- **Train time** (`[World] TrainTime`, on; settings version 222; roadmap 11). With a platform spot for the town (the spot key, or the station you came out of), a train standing at it is noticed: up to four people on the platform turn to look, the station's keeper calls it in his own voice, one person steps down and becomes a resident of the town (kin of someone here one time in two, with the family name, else a newcomer from a far city), letters waiting for the town come off it, and a resident whose trouble just ended in leaving (the ticket saved for and bought; the house gone to the bank, half; a son gone and the father after him, a third) says goodbye to a friend if one is near, walks to the train and is gone for good. Away, they go all the same, at the next day's tick, and the town says so. Never the train, its crew or its passengers; nothing spawned; nothing in missions. Log `TRAIN`; F7 `TEST trains:`. Not yet seen in game.
- **A spot table, and a key to fill it** (`[Keys] SpotKey`, K; settings version 221; roadmap 11). Each town's named outdoor places (platform, wharf, pithead, stockyards, cemetery, bandstand, church steps, corral, main street, saloon porch, bank door, jail yard, show ground) live in spots.txt beside RealNPCs.ini, one a line (town|kind|x|y|z|heading|room), read at the start with bad lines skipped and logged. Stand on a place and press the key: it's recorded under the current kind and written to the file; Shift and the key change the kind (the news line says which); refused indoors. A station you come out of stands for the town's platform for the session when none is recorded. F7 `TEST spots:`. The trains, the hangings and the shows stand at these next. Safe to ignore.
- **The Wild West show** (`[World] EventShows`, on; settings version 220; roadmap 10). The fourth town event. Once your most-told deed (a killing or a robbery) is talked of in four towns, one of them three hops from where it happened, a touring show books a large town you've been to (Saint Denis, Valentine, Rhodes; Blackwater in 1907), one a month at most: posters a week ahead (a notice, the paper, a poster among your papers), the act billed under the name the rumour gave you (THE DEVIL OF THE HEARTLANDS, THE TERROR OF THE RAILS, THE GHOST IN THE GRAY HAT...). Away: the paper reviews it, and the town's own version of the legend bends further and spreads from there. There (two till four, within 150 m of the town's heart): the ground just outside town, a barker, two riders in the ring, a crowd; the opening, the bill, the act told in four captions from the most bent version, the closing with "the real one". **J** while the act runs: heckle (the barker answers, the crowd jeers, the regulars think the less of you), stand up and say who you are (a deed everyone there saw: the crowd stares, some leave, the paper has it, the town remembers), rob the takings ($40-$90, a robbery deed, the show breaks up), or just watch. Testing row "show" (key None): a show posted here for tomorrow from your most-told deed (a test legend without one); again: two minutes. Not yet seen in game.
- **The circuit judge and gallows day** (`[World] EventTrials`, on; settings version 219; roadmap 10). The third town event, on the same framework. A resident who kills a man in a fight (the death ledger names him) is held at the jail; the circuit judge sits three to seven days on, ten till two. The verdict is weighed from what there is: who saw it, the dead man's friends in the town against the accused's, the accused's temper in the dock (a hothead convicts himself, a coward is pitied), your word if you give it (a known killer's word counts half), and a frightened town, which hangs more readily. Guilty: a hanging two days on at the jail yard, eleven o'clock, in a stockade with the sheriff's special deputies inside and the crowd outside; the drop is never shown. Not guilty: the town splits for a week, the dead man's friends souring toward him. Posted and reported in the paper at every turn. There: the trial outside the saloon (the judge, the accused in his own body between two deputies, a witness, the plea, the verdict read); the hanging at the jail door (the sentence read, his last words, a hymn, a silence, the sound from inside). Your part, on **J** (keyboard): testify when the judge asks (true or false: a lie two witnesses know for one is remembered), be sworn in as a special deputy before the hour and stand inside (his last words are to you; his friends think the less of you), break him out of the jail at night with no lawman near (a deed as heavy as a robbery; the dead man's friends sour, his warm), or stop the hanging with shots (he gets away in the uproar). Someone else's shots: the deputies finish it inside. A hogtied man of ours carried into a sheriff's office is brought in (a killing on his record: the judge; a known thief: a fine in the paper). A held man is not about the town and cannot stand for election. Testing row "trial" (key None): a regular held for the death of a made-up man, the judge tomorrow; the row again moves the trial, then the hanging, to two minutes from now. Not yet seen in game.
- **Election day** (`[World] EventElections`, on; settings version 218; roadmap 10). The second town event: the first Tuesday after the first Monday of November (the 7th in 1899), in every town with five living regulars, two grown men of the town stand for mayor or town marshal (the proud and the loyal first, the better-tied before the rest; never the game's own sheriff or a story character). Posted a week ahead: a notice, a line in the paper, an election poster among your papers. The voters are the town's regulars: each leans by their ties to the two men and by temper, the drifting undecided. Talk to a candidate: your word for him (worth what the town makes of you: a peacemaker's word moves the undecided, a known killer's counts against him), five dollars of whiskey for his voters (the thirsty are bought, the town thinks the less of it, a Loyal man refuses the money), or a word to step aside (a threat: the proud dig in, a coward loses heart). Polls ten till four; the tally at the close whether you're there or not, the result as a toast and in the paper; the winner's voters warm toward him, the loser's sour, and each remembers. There in the hours: a porch speech by the candidate whose half of the day it is, before a listening crowd: hecklers, an old man grumbling about the new secret ballot, a party man pressing a ticket on you, two supporters falling to words. Shots break it up as the dance's. Not yet seen in game.
- **Town events on one framework, and the Saturday dance** (`[World] TownEvents`, `EventDances`, both on; settings version 217; roadmap 10). A town's days: things posted ahead (a notice, a line in the paper) that happen whether you're there or not. The first: a dance at the saloon every second Saturday, seven till eleven, in a town with a saloon you've been to, posted on Thursday. Away, it resolves among the town's regulars (two to four come away warmer toward each other, a man and a woman now and then sweet on each other, sweethearts have words one dance in three; each remembers who they danced with) and word reaches you. There (within 150 m of the town's heart in the hours), it's staged outside the saloon you last came out of (else in the street behind you): a fiddler, three women dancing, a few watching, the caller's shouts, the people near turning to look. The wedding's rails: never in a mission, nothing spawned in view, a gun pointed or shots break it up and the town remembers who. Stay a while and the regulars think the better of you. Election day, the circuit judge and the Wild West show come next on the same framework. F7 `TEST events:`. Not yet seen in game.
- **Every worry mendable, and every one with a dark mirror** (`[Encounters] TalkWorries`, on; settings version 216; roadmap 9). Once someone has told you their trouble, Talk offers its mend: a dollar for the telegraph (the letter), toward the water cart ($5, the drought), the fare to the mine ($5, the son), a word put in (the courting), a word with the neighbour (the feud), toward the note ($10, the bank), coffee instead of whiskey (the drink, in a saloon), the marker paid ($5, the cards), a secret kept, the stage out of town ($5, the law); the debt, the doctor, the dentist and the fare keep their row. Taken, the trouble reads as ended well from there and they thank you later as for a debt settled; the hostile take nothing of yours, the proud won't be paid for but take a word. And every worry's **dark mirror**, a word that turns it the wrong way: the creditor told where to find them, the fever called a chill, the letter given up on, the farm sold up, the mine's bad news, a word in the wrong ear, the neighbour's insult carried, the bank's doom, another whiskey, a game pointed out, a sure thing for the savings, the tooth pulled by hand, the secret already known, the sheriff asking their name. The frightened believe the worst at once, the proud and the cold half the time want proof, the rest three times in four; believed, the trouble reads as ended badly from there (unless you mend it after), they think the less of you, and it counts as a wrong (no thanks till you've apologised). One or the other, once per run of the trouble. Not yet seen in game.
- **Answering letters, and writing your own** (`[World] LetterReplies`, `WriteLetters`, both on; settings version 215; roadmap 8). In the reader, **R on a letter** answers it: kindly, coldly, with a threat, with money ($5 or $20), or asking to meet; one answer per letter, none to the dead. The answer travels one to three game days by the post (by the towns' distance) and changes what the writer does next: a grieving relative riding west may **stand down** (a kind word, money, or frightened off by a threat) or **ride sooner** (a threat); a sweetheart warms or cools; the scorned soften or sink lower; a frightened host feels believed or mocked; the thankful stay grateful; the wedding expects you or doesn't. Most **write back**, a short letter delivered like any other. Asked to meet, some **wait at their town's station** two days on, from ten till four: a grey mark on your map with their name, and when you come up to them they say their two lines (a grieving relative only after standing down: "I came to look you in the eye. That's all I came for."). **L in the reader, in a post town, writes first** to anyone you've met (the eight most recently seen): a warning, an apology, a love note (the romance's rules: the courted warm, the rest half scorned), a threat or money ($5), carried and taken the same way; a love note or a threat may be shown around the town. Keyboard only for the two menus. F7 `TEST letters:`. Not yet seen in game.
- **Seeing what happened: Overheard, and raised voices behind you** (`[Display] Overheard`, `[Ticker] SceneCues`, both on; settings version 214; roadmap 24). The papers end with an **Overheard** page: what went on near you in the last game day, one plain sentence each, newest first ("Simon Walsh and Ezra Whitlow came to blows in the saloon, two hours ago."; a fight, a quarrel, a courting, a pocket picked, a killing between others, within 22 m of you), read from what the add-on already keeps and never saved; the Who's who page follows it. And a **cue on the ticker** when a scene starts out of your view within 22 m: "Raised voices behind you.", "A scuffle behind you.", "Laughter behind you.", "Soft words behind you.", "Someone weeping behind you.", "Talk behind you." (nothing for a pocket picked), at most once a minute. Not yet seen in game.
- **Three more verbs in Talk** (`[Encounters] TalkVerbs`, on; settings version 213; roadmap 6). **Ask about someone they know:** "What do you know of <X>?" for up to two people they've had dealings with lately (a quarrel, a kindness, sweethearts, an acquaintance), answered by how they stand with X and their own nature (the warm praise by what passed between them, the hostile grieve, an acquaintance states a fact, sweethearts go shy), with a warning when the town's log knows X for a killer, a thief or a brawler; the afraid and the hostile carry no tales. **Lend money:** "Could you use a loan? ($10)", taken by most (the proud won't be lent to, the hostile want nothing of yours; the greedy take it gladly and mean to forget), owed three game days, then "About that ten dollars I lent you..." first on the list: paid back by those who can and will, put off by those who can't (three days more), denied now and then by the greedy and the drifting ("What ten dollars?", and off they walk; the mind remembers); asked too early, a sting. One loan out per person at a time; they remember the lending. **Introduce two people:** "Have you met <X>?" when someone the mind knows stands within 8 m whom they don't: a word each ("Pleased to know you." / "Likewise." in their own voices), a hat tipped or a nod, and from then on they're acquainted: a tie of no warmth, so they nod to each other in passing and the street's reasons can pair them; both think the better of you. With the AI model on, every answer is worded in its own words. F7 `TEST talk:` counts them. Not yet seen in game.
- **The street reacts to other people's fights** (`[Scenes] StreetReacts`, on; settings version 212; roadmap 17). The moments of a scene between other people (the fists flying, two men squaring up, a gun out, a man killed or knocked out, the body after) are raised as the game's own shocking events, so its crowd does what it does for your fights: looking, backing off, running, calling the law, by its own rules. Once per scene per moment; the look-at the onlookers already gave stays beside it. Never on a mission, with the law after you, or beside enRAGE's own fighters. A guard watches ten seconds after each event for the law in combat, a gun out of someone else's holster or people fleeing beside you, and holds the town for thirty game minutes when it sees one. Three seconds after each event the log says what the street did (`STREET event ...: N within 20 m; 3 s on: L looking, F fleeing, C fighting`), since the natives' trailing arguments are unnamed and this is the first time the events are raised from a script: the in-game check decides whether they take. Not yet seen in game.
- **Reputation in the street** (`[Scenes] StreetReputation`, on; settings version 212; roadmap 17). People react to who walks among them. An outlaw (one of enRAGE's gunmen, read from its own roles, or a resident the law wants: the wanted-poster worry) walking in a town is looked after by the free people near who can see him, by their natures: a look that follows, a step off his line and then on their way, the old and the timid turning away, the proud and the hot-headed staring him down; the doctor, a preacher, the sheriff and his deputies and a counter keeper out of doors get a hat tipped, a nod or a wave and, half the time, a word in the passer-by's own voice ("Morning, Doc."). One witness mutters it on the ticker ("The street went quiet when he came in. It knows."; about 70 lines). The player, lightly: a known killer in a town (two killings known here this week, or one with no kindness beside it) or a known peacemaker (three kindnesses and no killing) gets a thought from a witness who knows of him, never a task. One reaction per 20 s, each notable once a game hour. F7 `TEST street:`. Not yet seen in game.
- **The town keeps time** (`[World] RegularsRoutines`, on; settings version 211; roadmap 17's last slice and 2's routines). Each regular has a routine read off who they are (their trade, temper, devotion and age; nothing saved, so old saves have it too): the early up at dawn, work and errands by day, the saloon of an evening (the drinkers' most, nearly anyone's a little), church on Sunday morning for those who go, home at night but for the owls. Nobody walks across town for it: when the game puts an ordinary person in the street, the saloon or the store out of your sight, the regular they become is the one whose routine puts them at that kind of spot at that hour (the best fit among the free regulars of that build), and a new regular made for a spot is made to fit it (no devout churchgoer born at the bar at midnight). A regular caught out of their hour thinks so ("Never come in here on a Tuesday. Today I did."), and the others think of the routine they're in ("Seven o'clock. Saloon. Same stool."; about 120 lines by spot and hour). Nobody is cast within 30 m of one of enRAGE's habits or funerals due or running. F7 `TEST regulars:` says where each regular near you should be by their routine. Checked offline over a week of hours for a town of thirty: the evening saloon, the store and the street always have a regular whose routine fits, Sunday morning's church is a churchgoer's, the same few faces own the evening saloon. Not yet seen in game.
- **Talk in your own voice** (`[Mind] TalkVoice`, off as it comes; `TalkVoiceSeconds` 6; settings version 210; roadmap 37; Lex's brief: the microphone through the existing Talk prompt). With the voice server running and a microphone, Talk's row "Say it out loud..." (keyboard or pad) opens the server's ear: it listens until you've said something and fallen quiet (or Space, Enter or a pad's A say that's all; Backspace, Esc or B never mind; `TalkVoiceSeconds` at most), writes it down with faster-whisper (its small English model, on the CPU: none of the card; about half a second), and your words are said to them exactly as if typed, with the same answers (the AI model's own words with it on, else the written answer by what you seem to be asking). Nothing is kept and nothing leaves the PC. The row is greyed with the reason when the server isn't running, has no transcriber or finds no microphone; the words it makes out of silence are refused ("Didn't catch that"). `install-voice.cmd` now puts the transcriber in (about 150 MB more, once; run it again if the voices are already set up); the server's `--stt-model` (base.en; small.en hears better), `--stt-device` (cpu; cuda), `--no-stt`. A Testing row, "Hear you through the microphone", proves the ear without Talk (the log line `HEARD:`). Also fixed from 4a (unseen): the Enter or Space that chose the typed row no longer counts as its first key. Not yet seen in game.
- **Talk in your own words, typed** (`[Mind] TalkTyped`, on; settings version 209; roadmap 37; Lex's brief: talk through the existing Talk prompt). Talk's last row, "Say something else...", opens a line to type on (a keyboard in use; the game's controls are held off while it's open so the letters don't walk Arthur about): Enter says it, Backspace deletes, Esc leaves. What you type is said to them, and they answer from who they are and how they stand with you: with the AI model and `LanguageTalk` on, in their own words (told where they are, their nature and mood, how they feel about you, and the trouble they'd tell you of if you asked; what they'd not tell a stranger they keep to themselves; the usual checks on names and modern words, the written answer if it's slow); without the model, a written answer by what your words seem to be about: the news, their name (the friendly give it, the afraid don't), how they are, their trouble (told to the fond and to those you've been kind to, as Talk's news), the weather, the way, a drink, money, God, guns (a hostile man squares up), the law, thanks, sorry, goodbye (which ends it), and anything else met with a plain non-answer by their nature. Nothing is paid or promised by typing. The microphone comes next. Not yet seen in game.
- **The real history behind one strict switch, and the year as it was** (`[World] HistoricalAccuracy`, on; settings version 208; roadmap 23; Lex's brief: historical context "must be a strictly togglable setting"). On, as it comes: everything dated that RealNPCs says or thinks stands behind this one switch: the talk of the times from the papers of 1899 and 1907 (the election, the Philippines, the reservations, the trusts; in 1907 Roosevelt, the Panic, Oklahoma), the lines about the year they live in, and, new: **the real calendar**, keyed to the game's own date, which follows the true weekdays (Easter fell on 2 April 1899, the Sunday your clock read): New Year's, Mardi Gras and All Saints' in Lemoyne, Saint Patrick's and Labor Day in Saint Denis, Washington's Birthday, Good Friday and Easter, May Day, Decoration Day, the Fourth, Halloween, Election Day (7 November 1899), Thanksgiving (the last Thursday), Christmas Eve and Day, New Year's Eve ("Nineteen hundred tomorrow. Doesn't feel any different. Yet."), each with what the day, its eve and its morning after bring to mind, and a word of the day on the ticker in the morning; **the year's work by region**, two months at a time (calving and the roundup in ranch country, cane and cotton and the fever watch in Lemoyne, the thaw and the trapping lines in the high country); in 1907 the snake-oil man swears to the Food and Drugs Act and the Panic is in the paper from 22 October. Off: none of it, and the timeless texture of the days (bath night, Sunday collars, wash day) stays as it was. F7 `TEST calendar:`. Not yet seen in game.
- **The friend from the road rides in** (`[Encounters] AlliesRide`, on; settings version 207): the friend from a town's ledger who happens by when you're fighting out of the towns comes on horseback now, 45-70 m off (a saddle horse a townsman might own, made under them out of sight), shouting as they come, and is down off the horse and in on foot near you; if the horse won't take the order within eight seconds they're down and running as before. The horse is given back to the game. Not yet seen in game.
- **An ally who dies is grieved, at a cost to you** (`[Encounters] AlliesGrieved`, on; `AlliesGrievedLiking`, 15): someone who fell standing with you is mourned by those who knew them, who hear of it within an hour or two, remember whose quarrel it was ("Abner died standing with Morgan. Morgan's quarrel, Abner's blood."), and think the less of you: a friend of the fallen loses 15 of their fondness for you (a third of it for someone who merely knew them), the town's word has it by evening, and you're told as they fall. A death in someone else's quarrel is the oldest grievance there is, and it's what makes an ally's coming worth something. Not yet seen in game.
- **More ways to win people over** (`[Encounters] FondnessActs`, on; settings version 206; Lex, 2026-11-03: more NPC interactions that raise fondness). Talk (Q, locked on) gains three rows: **whiskey for the house** ($2, in a saloon with two or more others at the bar): the one you say it to answers for the room by their nature (someone who hates you won't drink it, the devout thank you for the house without a glass), the room looks over and cheers, and everyone treated thinks better of you, the drunk most, each with a drink of yours to remember and maybe thank you for later; **a gift** (forty cents: a pouch of tobacco for a man, a tin of peaches for a woman), which the proud take where they'd refuse money (a gift between equals carries no shame; alms do), the greedy would sooner the price of, and the poor are fondest of; and **condolences** to someone grieving a friend ("I'm sorry about Abner."), the kindness people most notice and least often get, thrown back by anyone who knows you killed them. Each is a happening the mind already understands (a round, charity, a comfort), so the thanks, the greetings, the trust with a worry and the allies' warmth follow as before. And two more, outside Talk's list: **a debt covered in the street**: when a creditor is pressing someone before you (the debt scene: "You had time. Fifteen dollars, Elwood."), lock on to the creditor and Talk offers the one thing to say to him, "I'll cover what he owes you ($N)."; he answers by his nature and walks off paid, the one pressed turns to you with their thanks and goes, and they're fonder of you than for anything but their life (a settled debt for the thanks that come later; their own debt worry, when that's what it was, ends well), the creditor a little fonder too, and everyone who saw it thinks better of you; and **a fire shared**: the men at a campfire you sit at on the road are the nearest town's own people from then on (regulars of it, when it has room), kept once you've sat, and remember the night when you meet them in its street. And **walking someone home**: after dark in a town, Talk on a woman, someone in drink or a timid man offers "Let me see you home." (a proud man knows the way to his own door; a woman who doesn't know you won't walk out with a stranger, half the time); say yes and they set off for their door, 60-100 m off, at a walk: keep up (fall 8 m behind and they stop and wait, looking back; wait twenty seconds and they go on alone, stung), and at the door they turn and thank you in their own voice, fonder of you by a lot, with the night to remember and a thanks that may come days later. The street after dark was a real fear in 1899, and company against it is the oldest ground of liking. Testing row "Walk someone home". **Carrying a word**, the first errand an NPC gives you: Talk on someone with a quarrel still sore (a tie gone cold with a named person of their town, or sweethearts fallen out) offers "Any word you'd have carried to Hiram?"; by their nature they give you one ("Tell him I was in the wrong. I'll not say it to his face."; the cold trust nothing to a stranger), and for two game days the other wears a small dot on the map while near you and Talk on them opens with the word ("Abner sent word. He says the fault was his."); by their nature they take it (the quarrel mended: a makeup tie, the memory for both, the town's talk) or send it back ("Let him say it to my face"), and either way both think better of you for carrying it. Pride can't apologise to a face; a go-between lets it. One errand at a time, kept until delivered or lapsed (a reload forgets it). **A coin for the music**: lock on to the piano player, the guitar or the banjo and Talk offers "For the music ($0.25)" alone; they look up and thank you in their voice, and, when they're one of the town's own, remember the face. Not yet seen in game.
- **A kindness seen** (`[Encounters] KindnessSeen`, on; `KindnessSeenLiking`, 5): the named people who see you do a kindness to someone else from within 12 m (a drink stood, money or a gift given, a debt paid, a hand up, a bully faced, a mercy shown) think better of you too, and remember seeing you do right ("Saw you do a decent thing. Didn't think he had it in him."). Not yet seen in game.

## 0.7.20 (2026-11-03)

- The green dot on friends and allies is smaller (`[Encounters] AlliesDotScale`, 0.6; settings version 205): the companion dot in that colour drew large (Lex, seen in game: "looks pretty good, can we make it the smaller version"). 1 is the game's own size; a change shows on the next dot made, and **`Insert`** remakes them all.

## 0.7.19 (2026-11-03)

The voices: the people near you speak RealNPCs' words in their own voices, cloned on your PC from your own game as you meet them. People talk to each other from what's on their minds and the times they live in, their thoughts follow the moment they're in, allies come to your aid, the VRAM gauge is back, and from the earlier 0.7.19 build (never released): grudges said to your face, names that fit the person, a rarer and eerier Awakening, and the held ring to try.

- **Inner thoughts follow the moment they're in** (Lex: a man fighting Arthur thought "Can't complain"). Someone fighting you (fists or guns), fighting someone else, running for their life, knocked down, hurt, or looking down your barrel thinks of that before anything, awake or not, with or without the AI model (the line is written, and turns over every few seconds as the fight goes on); so does anyone with shots, a dead body or a fight close by, or a wanted man with his gun out beside them. The quieter moments join their thoughts too: the saddle, a wagon, a drink, a meal, a smoke, their work, a seat or a lean, walking in the dark, running, standing about, drunk, a gun in hand, and how you look beside them (bloodied, drunk, filthy, soaked, undressed), above the timeless musings and below a worry or a fresh memory. About 430 new lines by temper (the bold, the fearful, the cold, the plain) in 26 kinds; the AI model is told what's happening to them ("Right now: ..."); someone fighting or fleeing is on the ticker now, first in turn, with a new line every six seconds. Only the waking doubt the world: someone still asleep never wonders whether any of it is real, whatever they lived through; the wondering starts at the first stage of the Awakening (`[Mind] WrittenWakes`, `ModelWakes`). F7 `TEST moments:`. Not yet seen in game.
- **A green dot on each friend** on the map whenever they're near you (within 80 m; the bar at `AlliesLiking` or more, someone you saved, a kindness remembered, a trouble mended; Lex greeted two people to the top of the bar and saw no dot), and on each ally while they're with you (`[Encounters] AlliesDot`, on; `AlliesDotColour`, a blip modifier's name: BLIP_MODIFIER_DEBUG_GREEN as built, BLIP_MODIFIER_FRIENDLY the game's companion blue, empty the plain companion dot; settings version 199). The game's own small companion dot, recoloured. Not yet seen in game: the shade may want tuning from the ini.
- **Greeting someone makes them fonder of you** (`[Encounters] GreetLiking`, 34; settings version 203): enRAGE's fondness (the bar under people with FeelingBars, what AlliesLiking reads) rises by a third of the bar on every greeting of someone; nothing from someone afraid of you or angry with you. Three hellos fill the bar and make a friend who'll stand with you: someone counts as a friend from 90 on the bar (`AlliesLiking` 90, from 40; settings version 202; Lex: "a friend when the fondness bar is 90% or higher", "3 greets will get to 100%", "for now until we add in more NPC interactions", which are the roadmap's next item). Seen in game at 20 a greeting (the bar half full after two: hence the rest).
- **The voices take less of your card** (Lex: the card full, frames lagging). The voice server runs its Llama backbone in half precision (a gigabyte less: about 2.3 GB on the card instead of 3.3, lines as quick as before; `--full` in `start-voice-server.cmd` puts it back if a voice sounds off), gives the card its working memory back after every line, and after a minute with nobody speaking moves the whole model to system memory, about 3 GB back to the game, returning for the next line in under a second (`--idle-seconds`, 60; 0 keeps it on the card). F7's `TEST voice:` line says what the voices hold, and the settings panel shows the voice server's state under the AI model's switch (ready in green, on its way in gold, not reachable in red; Lex), with the two voice settings you'd reach for under it: inner thoughts voiced on or off, and how far the voices are heard. The AI model (Ollama's llama3.1:8b, about 5 GB) is the other big tenant: a smaller model in `[Mind] LanguageModel` (llama3.2:3b, about 2 GB) is the next lever if the card is still full.
- **Someone you save is yours for good** (Lex): the man you pull a bully off, the one whose beater you put down in a brawl, the captive you save from the rope, the hogtied you cut loose who thank you. enRAGE's fondness goes to the top (the bar under them), they count as a friend who owes you for 30 game days whatever else they feel (so they come to your aid first), they trust you with a worry, and they remember it ("he stepped in for me"). F7 `TEST saved:`. Not yet seen in game.
- Fixed: the allies Testing row said "the model isn't loaded" and made nobody (Lex): the revolver man's body (his ped model, not the AI model) was asked for and spawned in the same instant. The row and the friend from the road wait for the body to stream in now (up to 15 s) and say so. Allies keep fists against fists; nobody new comes while you're wanted (the townsfolk answering your crime are in combat with you too), though those already with you fight on; the friend from the road and the test's man are handed back to the game after; an ally no longer stays deaf to gunfire for good.
- The VRAM gauge: after eight failed readings running it tries every 30 s instead of giving up for good, and the card's size comes from the registry when DXGI reads it smaller (a 16 GB card showed 15.6).
- Mind talks: the plate or dollar a kind neighbour gives is a "charity" kindness in the ledger (its note never landed before), and the talks use the street's own rumours.

- **Allies who come to your aid** (a player's request; `[Encounters] AlliesHelp`, on; `AlliesMetres` 40, `AlliesMost` 3, `AlliesLiking` 90 (40 until settings version 202), `AlliesRoadChance` 30; settings version 197). Attacked by anyone, the law too (Lex, 2026-11-03: a friend "would now protect the main character if fought by the law or anyone else"; before, never the law), within sight of people who think well of you, some of them come: someone who owes you (the bar at `AlliesLiking` or more, someone you saved) whatever the odds, the rest from how they stand with you (enRAGE's fondness, a kindness of yours they remember, a trouble you mended, talks and your name known; never the afraid or the aggrieved) and from their own nature (the brave and the hot first, the loyal for a friend, cowards never, the drunk readier, a woman only against fists), fewer the more are with you already. Against guns they're given a revolver and keep it. They shout as they come, go for the nearest of your attackers and the next as those fall, and when no foe is left they turn to you with a word, said in their own voice with the voice server. They remember standing with you (their thoughts say so for days) and enRAGE's townsfolk think better of you for it; one who falls is logged. Out of the towns, with nobody you know in sight and two or more armed against you, a friend from a town's ledger may happen by, made 30 to 45 m off in their own clothes, and run in (once in five minutes at most). Testing row "An armed man comes at you; a friend comes to your aid"; F7 `TEST allies:`; log `ALLIES`. Not yet seen in game.
- **People talk to each other from what's on their minds** (`[Mind] MindTalks`, on; settings version 196; no AI model needed). Among people near you who know each other, when no quarrel, debt or courtship has a claim on them, one brings up what their thoughts are made of: the worry they're living through, in the words their thoughts use; the times they live in (below); the stranger, as they remember him; a plan they carry (getting even, paying someone back, courting); the town's talk; what they're short of (a meal, a word, a dollar, sleep); or the day. The other answers from their own nature, kindly, coldly or sharply, and on the times from their own side of the question (the lawful and the well-off for the government, the reckless against it; the compassionate and the devout for people, the hard against them; the curious for the new, the orderly against it; nobody against their own people), and the first has a last word, or not. A kind answer to a trouble leaves them warmer, a cold one a brush-off, a sharp one a snap; a plate or a dollar offered is a kindness between them. Said in their own voices with the voice server, as subtitles when you can see them. With the AI model on (`[Mind] Language` and `LanguageTalk`), the answer is in the model's words, asked as the talk starts and taken as it's said (the written line when the model is late). Testing row "Two people talk of what's on their minds"; F7 `TEST mind talks:`; log `MINDTALK`.
- **The times they live in.** What people in 1899 were really living through and talking of, and in the epilogue's 1907: the election coming (McKinley, Bryan, Hanna, the Rough Rider for vice president), the war in the Philippines, Dewey's homecoming, Cuba and the fever, the Klondike and the gold at Nome, the Boers, the reservations and the allotments (and the Wapiti up north), Wounded Knee nine years on, Geronimo at Fort Sill, the trusts and the railroads, the horseless carriage, the telephone, the X-ray and the bicycle, the yellow papers, Jeffries the champion, the Maple Leaf Rag, the blizzard of February, the century turning, The Hague, Dreyfus, what happened in Georgia, the frontier closed, votes for women, prices and wages; and for 1907 Roosevelt and the canal, the Panic, Oklahoma, the white fleet, motorcars and flying machines, San Francisco, the Thaw trial, Monongah and Haywood, The Jungle, Taft against Bryan, the Cubs, Johnson and Cobb, Ellis Island, the old West gone, the suffrage women, Geronimo's book: 35 topics, about 440 lines, each with the month it was in the papers from. They come up in the talks above (the other answering from their own side) and when you ask someone the news.
- The voices are heard a little less far: `VoiceMetres` 14 (from 15; an ini on 15 moves, a value of your own is kept; settings version 195).
- **The VRAM gauge is back** (set aside for 0.7.12): while the AI model is on, a small `VRAM 11.3 / 15.6 GB` at the top left shows how much of your graphics card's memory is in use (the game, the model and everything else on it, as Task Manager counts it); amber over 85%, red over 95%. `[Display] VramGauge` (model; on shows it always, off never), on the settings panel's UI page.
- Fixed: Talk (Q in the lock-on menu) on someone walking by left them walking, and the list closed a few steps on, lock-on or not (Lex). They stop and turn to you now, out of a scenario's walk the game's own way, and someone who walks on anyway is asked again.
- With VoicedDialogue, the subtitle for a line waits for the cloned voice (a second or two to make) and shows as the voice starts, or after 3.5 s anyway (Lex: the voice came behind the words).
- Fixed: a street scene could call someone by one name while the card over their head and their mind used another (a brawl's captions said Elphege, the card over him Owen). enRAGE had taken the man up between the two under a name of its own, and RealNPCs only settled the name when his card next came round. It's settled the moment any part of RealNPCs names him now, so the mind, the scenes, the ticker and the card say the same name.
- No more repeated voice lines (Lex): the glitch where someone said the same greeting three times over is gone (someone who glitches freezes and stares, then says what that was like), and the questioning stage of the Awakening no longer says a thing twice to hear itself.
- **Voices recorded from your own game** (`[Mind] VoiceRecord`, on; settings version 194). Each person has a voice bank of their own for good (one of their model's, chosen by who they are, among the voices the game recorded for their people), and when it has no clip yet, the next time they're near in a quiet moment they say one of the game's own lines once and the voice server records it off your PC's sound. While it records, RealNPCs' own game lines are held; another voice near spoils the clip (kept under `captured\poor` to listen to), a voice farther off keeps it as the weaker sort until a clean one comes; a clearer recording replaces a weaker one. The Testing page has "Record the nearest person's voice clip" and "Record every voice still wanted on the nearest person" (about 90 minutes, pressed again it stops). The server writes `voice-server.log` beside its files.
- **Voiced thoughts and dialogue** (`[Mind] VoicedThoughts`, `VoicedDialogue`, on as they come; settings version 193, on from 198). A local voice server (the zip's `voice` folder: `install-voice.cmd` once, about 8 GB and ten minutes, then `start-voice-server.cmd` before you play; an NVIDIA card with about 4 GB free beside the game) clones each person's voice on your own PC with Chatterbox from the game's own recording of their voice, recorded from your own game (above), and speaks RealNPCs' words in it. A thought over someone within `VoiceMetres` (14) is murmured in their voice a moment after it shows (one at a time, each person once in 90 s); that thought isn't also said aloud by rule. What people say near you in RealNPCs' words (the street's scenes, their talks with each other, Talk's answers, the encounters) is said in their voice, the subtitle waiting for it. One voice plays at a time, in turn, louder near and panned to the side they stand on. `VoiceVolume` (100%), `VoiceUrl`. The server takes about 3.5 GB of the graphics card and stays silent while the card has less than 0.8 GB free; without it nothing changes. Nothing of Rockstar's ships. A row on the Testing page (Tools): "Voice the nearest person now"; F7 adds `TEST voice`. Seen in game 2026-11-03.
- Fixed: two people whose fight scene stopped (when one of them was in combat with you, or you aimed at one) and who fought on anyway were left to it, with no lawman coming. The law hears that fight too now: after a few seconds a free lawman within 60 m comes and breaks it up. The log says who stopped a scene and why.
- **The held ring** (`[Mind] HeldRing`, off while it's tried; `HeldRingMetres` 12, `HeldRingMinutes` 10). The people near you are held: when one of their acts ends the next begins at once (what their mind chose, else what it ranked next, else the nearest seat or spot, else a short stroll) instead of the game taking them back between acts. Let go 13 m farther out, after ten minutes, or the moment a gun, a fight, a scene, Talk, the law or enRAGE has a claim on them. The log's "MIND acting minute" line and F7's mind acts line say how many in the ring are held (settings 192).
- **The Awakening rarer and eerier.** Gatherings belong to the dark now: from nine at night to five in the morning, or any hour in fog, rain or a storm. By clear day they come a quarter as often, and only with nobody else in sight. A third of the rings that won't turn on you are gone if you look away and back. The waking say the game's lines in the wrong places far less often (the fully awake every 25 to 40 seconds, not 7 to 11; the first stage every few minutes). New values as they come: GatheringChance 10 (from 15), GatheringGapHours 24 (from 6), AwakeningDespair 50 (from 75); an ini on the old values is moved, a value of your own is kept (settings 191).
- The crying woman has nothing in her hands but her knife: some of the women's models came with a rifle.
- **Names that fit the person** (a player's report). The game's own voice recordings say who each kind of townsperson was recorded for, and RealNPCs now names people to match: Saint Denis's Chinatown and the railworkers get Chinese names, the Wapiti a mission name and their own ("Joseph Gray Elk"), Hispanic travellers and townsfolk Spanish names, Black townsfolk the names of the time's Black South, the Dusters Irish ones. Where the game mixes people in one model, the share of its own voices decides. A white townsman sometimes takes his town's people: French and Creole in Saint Denis and the bayou, Italian and Irish in Saint Denis, German and Scandinavian on the farms. People already met keep the names they have.
- The Testing row for Called out yourself always shows it now: with nobody near who holds something against you, the nearest named person stands in, sore over hard words.
- **Called out yourself** (`[Encounters] CalloutsArthur`, on). By day in a town, someone named near you who holds something against you comes over and says it to your face: you struck or robbed them, they saw you kill, word of your thieving in their town reached them, plain hatred, or you spoke to them badly (enRAGE's vengeful and murderous are left to enRAGE). Walk off past 12 m and they shout "coward" after you. Stand there seven seconds and most put up their fists (fighting them is self-defence) or say their piece and go. An armed hothead with blood between you may draw instead (`CalloutsArthurDraw`, 10%). Hold a gun on them and they back off. Once a day per person, at least five minutes between them, `CalloutsArthurChance` 30% a roll. There's a row for it on the Testing page. Settings version 190. Not yet seen in game.
- The hogtied: hold a gun on the men and bounty hunters fight back too (they used to be left to enRAGE's own reactions and never fought). And one in four you cut loose pulls a knife and wants your money instead of thanking you (`[Encounters] HogtiedTurnsOnYou`, 25; violence against Arthur, on as it comes; settings version 189). Not yet seen in game.
- **As many wake without the AI model as with it.** Someone below the second stage of waking who has cause (something seen that shouldn't have been, a death they woke from, a moment lived twice) now and then wonders at the world in a written thought, at most once a day, and it shakes them a little, as the model's own wondering does (`[Mind] WrittenWakes`, on; 30 lines; settings version 188). Not yet seen in game.
- The readmes say what runs without the AI model now (a thought with what people do, scenes they start themselves, thoughts aloud, Talk that varies, far fewer repeats), and "recommended" where they said "highly recommended"; thoughts aloud no longer say they need a voice list (it ships since 0.7.15). F7 adds TEST no model: what ran on RealNPCs' own rules this session.

## 0.7.18 (2026-11-01)

Hostile gatherings of the awake, the game's own event dot on the map, and eerier rings.

- A hostile gathering comes at you as one band: every one of them a red dot on the map, all of them against you together. The event dot, once you have reached it, stays gone until that happening is over.
- Fixed: a hostile gathering whose ring was kneeling or sitting never came at you (their act kept them); they get up the game's own way first now, and the same for scattering and for turning to you in the middle. The event dot goes once you are within 8 m of it.
- The game's own pulsing event dot on the map where one of RealNPCs' happenings is going on near you: a brawl, a gathering of the awake, a callout, a husband's temper, the hogtied, an execution (`[Encounters] EventDots`, on; settings version 187).
- A gathering's ring all do the same eerie thing together, whichever it is that night.
- Half the gatherings of the awake are hostile: come within 10 m and every one of them comes at you, bare-handed, all at once (`[Encounters] GatheringHostile`, 50; 0 for never). Fighting them off is self-defence (settings version 186).

## 0.7.17 (2026-11-01)

More happening on the street and less idle talk, the law that shoots your pickpocket, eerier and bigger gatherings, Talk that varies, and feelings that know their cause.

- The gatherings of the awake are eerier: each in the ring does something of their own, mixed round the circle: kneels, dances, stands stock-still and stares, sits or lies on the ground, mourns, crouches, holds up a lantern, or stands and makes a low sound now and then. Caught creeping closer, they still stop dead; a few seconds later each goes back to it.
- More happening on the street, less idle talk: street scenes come twice as often as they come (`[Scenes] DramaGapSeconds` 30, from 60; an ini on 60 moves), and scenes of nothing but talk (small talk, gossip, a song, a haggle, a tall tale, the paper read out, a round stood) are passed over, along with the quiet talks beside a scene (`[Scenes] EventfulOnly`, on). Quarrels, fights, thefts, cheating, debts, flirts and proposals, snapping and brush-offs, favours, warnings and the waking's questions all stay (settings version 185).
- The law shoots the one who picked your pocket: with a lawman near, half the time (`[Scenes] LawShootsThieves`, 50) he draws and shoots the thief instead of running him down, and your money comes back off him either way (settings version 184). Pickpockets among townsfolk lifting from each other were already there (`[Scenes] NpcThieves`).
- The gatherings of the awake are bigger: six to ten stand in the ring, where it was four to seven.
- More of the game's own recorded voices between people: asked how it's going, someone answers by how they really are (going well, going badly) and the other is glad or sorry to hear it; someone asks for the latest and gets the news; friends rib each other; fog and snow get their own grumbles; and in 1907 people talk of the times. Five line names the add-on tried that no voice has (a howdy, a hi, a greeting, a stare and a sorry) were swapped for ones the voices carry.
- **Talk that varies and remembers.** Every answer that had one way of putting it now has three or four: someone afraid of you, someone who hates you, and each temper as you offer a drink or money, threaten, ask the news, apologise or say goodbye (about 80 new answers; on average 3.6 answers a situation, from 1.7). Ask the same person the same thing again and they won't give you the very same words. Only the words vary: what the answer does (the money, the liking, whether they step back or square up) stays as it was.
- Feelings that know their cause: right after something happens, the thought says what it was most of the time, not just whom it's about. Anger at a shove is not anger at a theft, a slight, a beating, a debt called in before a crowd or the law's hand on a collar; fear after a killing is not fear of a brawl; and gratitude, grief, shame, pride and relief each have their cause (107 lines over 25 feelings and causes). Kept in memory only, nothing new in the save.
- The waking mutter far more: twenty lines for each stage of the Awakening where there were six (questioning, unravelling, awake), and twelve for the ones who wander off to see where the street ends.

## 0.7.16 (2026-11-01)

Far fewer repeated thoughts: about 650 new written lines and thoughts that vary over an hour of play, all without the AI model.

- The street's exchanges open five times as many ways: twenty hellos where there were four, and for each worry four ways to snap at its worst and to tell how it ended, good or bad, where there was one. With the lines said lately passed over, the street's talk repeats 9% of the time in an hour, from 15% before this version.
- Twice the weather in people's heads (twelve lines for each kind of weather, where there were six), more for a sore day and a glad one, and more of what each temper thinks watching someone it knows on the street (the proud, the loyal, the drifter, the cold, the hothead, the greedy, the coward, everyone else).
- Many more thoughts from the day itself: every meal and the small hours have three times the lines, every day of the week has its own (Tuesday to Thursday had none), and the year gives more of 1899's talk (the Boers, bicycles, McKinley, the phonograph) and 1907's (the bank panic, Oklahoma, the white fleet). A town thinking at the same hour no longer falls back on the one breakfast line: someone's own thought comes first (the townsfolk test's distinct thoughts 807 to 969).
- Twice the lines for every worry: each stage of the fourteen worries people live through (a debt, a sick child, a letter that doesn't come, the drought, the bank's note, the drink, a bad tooth...) has six lines where it had three, 210 more in all, so the same worry seen in a dozen faces in an afternoon doesn't read as one line passed round. In the ride test a worry or a thought about you never came round twice in the first hour (before: 13.6 and 23.3 an hour), and the second and third hours' repeats fell to 16% and 32%.
- More thoughts about you: what people think as they see you, ride by, wear a gun or meet you again goes from 44 lines to 145 (a regular who knows you, your gun, your horse, the rest of you). In the ride test the second hour's repeats fall from 27% to 19%. Written lines, no AI model needed.
- **Thoughts vary over an hour of play.** No two people near you think the same thought-shape within about an hour of play, where before it was the last few minutes: in the ride test the share of thoughts you had already seen fell from 22% to 0% in the first hour, 43% to 27% in the second and 50% to 40% in the third, with the same lines, and on a quiet day the plain filler gives way to the day, the times and what is going on. The street's scenes and chance meetings pass over lines said lately too (`[Mind] SaidLately`, on; their exact repeats in an hour 15% to 12%). No AI model needed (settings version 183).

## 0.7.15 (2026-11-01)

Brawls at the bar that grow, thoughts said aloud and scenes people start for their own reasons without the AI model, the game's voices known to every player, and Alt+F or Alt+R for the settings.

- The crowd that walked over to ring a fight and cheer is off (`[Scenes] FightCrowds`; an upgraded ini has it off too): some of them froze where they stopped. Brawls still draw people in to fight (settings version 182).
- One name per person, wherever it shows: the brawl's knock-outs, the ledger of the dead and the mind now use the name over their head (enRAGE's card when it gives one), as the scenes do.
- Fixed: onlookers who got up off a bench or the bar to watch a brawl could freeze where they stood and not move again. They now get up the game's own way, as everyone else does since 0.7.12.
- Brawls draw people in sooner: in a fight's first 10 seconds onlookers are readier still to join (`[Mind] BrawlOpeningBoost`, 20, on top of BrawlJoinBoost), so two at it grow into a brawl more often; someone may still go down early (settings version 181).
- Fixed: someone with a name card from enRAGE was called by that name in the scenes but by RealNPCs' own name in their thoughts and the brawl's log; the name over their head is used everywhere now.
- **People start scenes from their own reasons, without the AI model** (`[Mind] MindStartsScenes`, on). Someone near you with a reason of their own to go to someone else near (a debt to call in, a friend to comfort, a quarrel to pick up, good news to tell) may go and start it, with the written why over their head, on top of the street's own scenes: at most one a minute, never past MainScenesAtOnce. Their temper leans it (the assertive and the angry to a quarrel, the soft-hearted to comfort, the sociable to news), and so does a feeling that fits. With the model's picks on, the model still chooses among those reasons too. F7 TEST mind scenes counts "by their own minds". Not yet seen in game (settings version 179).
- **Brawls at the bar** (`[Mind] SeatedBrawls`, on): two people sat or leaning at a bar or a table may come to blows; they get up first, the game's own way. Until now their quarrel always stayed words, so a saloon full of drinkers never brawled (settings version 180). Seen in game in the Valentine saloon. The brawl test's refusal names a close pair's real reason now.
- **The settings panel opens on Alt+F, and Alt+R as well.** Alt+F11 is left to enRAGE's own settings menu (`[Output] ReplaceEnrageSettings` is off as it comes; true gives Alt+F11 to this panel again, on enRAGE 0.9.5.3). An ini still on the old keys moves by itself; a key you chose yourself is kept (settings versions 177 and 178).
- **The game's voices, known to every player.** RealNPCs now carries a table of the game's 592 voices: which of its lines each voice has and how many recordings of each (names and numbers only, no words, no audio). It used to need a voice list only one PC had. With it, 337 kinds of townsfolk are known for certain by their voice, the counter keepers' too (their voices were misread before), and remarks played by name take turns over a voice's first three fitting lines instead of always the first. With the AI model on, its pick of a remark for a thought said aloud now works for every player. A `voicesanks.txt` of your own still comes first.
