- Fixed: after an Insert reload (or a mission, or riding off mid-scene) the people in a street scene cut off halfway stood frozen in its last pose until you left the area. They now go on their way; anyone sat or leaning in the game's own spot is left there.
- **Thoughts said aloud, without the AI model** (`[Mind] ThoughtsAloud`, on). As a thought shows over someone within 12 m, what they feel most may come out aloud in their own recorded voice: a curse when they are angry (a disapproving sound with CrudeTalk off), "shame on you" at the one they despise close by, a frightened word, thanks, relief, things going well; drunk talk, or grumbling at the rain, when nothing stronger is on them. Grief, shame, guilt, pride, envy and pity stay inside, and so does anything they hide. The game picks a recording their voice has, so it needs no voice list; one every 20 s at most, each person once in 4 minutes, about half of thoughts never offered. With the AI model on and a voice list for that voice, the model picks as before. F7 TEST aloud counts "by rule". Not yet seen in game (settings version 176).

## 0.7.14 (2026-11-01)

Fights that grow into brawls, a red dot on whoever started it, and the law that hears a fight.

- Fights grow into brawls more readily: onlookers are readier to join (`[Mind] BrawlJoinBoost`, 20 as it comes; 0 as before). In Saint Denis two men fighting never drew anyone in: every onlooker fell short of the bar to join (settings version 175).
- The one who threw the first punch wears a small red dot on the map while the fight lasts, and stepping in against him is self-defence (`[Mind] BrawlStarterDot`, on). A fight is heard, too: if no lawman sees it, after a few seconds any free lawman within 60 m comes. Seen in game in Saint Denis.

## 0.7.13 (2026-11-01)

Thoughts that go with what people do, without the AI model.

- **A thought with what people choose to do** (`[Mind] ActThoughts`, on as it comes; works without the AI model). When someone near you and in sight gets up to go home, heads for a drink, sits down to rest, keeps clear of you or goes over to greet a friend by their own mind's choice, a thought over their head says what moves them to it: tired, thirsty, sore at someone, wanting company. Each turn of an encounter (a callout, a domestic, the hogtied, a favour asked, a warning) gets one too, the reason for the answer they chose. The lines are by temper (bold, fearful, cold or plain), never the same one twice in a day for one person, a person at most once every 90 s and never on top of another thought of theirs. With the AI model on and answering, its own thought comes first and these fill in only where it gave none. Loaded and running in game (settings upgraded, no errors); not yet seen on screen.
- The limits are settings: one person at most once every 90 s (`[Mind] ActThoughtEachSeconds`) and not within 15 s of another, different thought of theirs (`ActThoughtGapSeconds`; the same line again doesn't count). The callout and the husband's temper show what the quarrel is about first, then the reason for the answer (settings version 174).
- The settings panel's "The world and its people" page is called "World and people" now.

## 0.7.12 (2026-11-01)

People get up and go about what their minds choose, two street scenes play at once, a storyteller keeps the world lively, three encounters come from the game's own, more people deal with you, and violence against Arthur is on as it comes. None of it needs the AI model.

- **People get up and act on their minds** (`[Mind] MindActsScenarios`, on). Someone sat on a bench, leaning on a wall, at the bar or having a smoke whose mind wants a drink, a meal, a rest elsewhere, their work, home or someone to talk to gets up the way Rockstar's own scripts get people up, and goes to it. Anyone who doesn't get up is put back where they were, so nobody is left frozen in a pose.
- **Two street scenes at once** (`[Scenes] MainScenesAtOnce` 2, on the Essentials page). While one full scene plays, a second can start between two other people 20 m or more away. Fights, rough handling and thefts stay one at a time.
- **The storyteller** (`[World] Storyteller`: lively as it comes; calm, wild or off; on the Essentials page). When nothing worth stopping for has happened near you for a while, the next street scene, chance meeting and encounter come sooner; after something big, a little later. It only changes the waits and starts nothing itself.
- **Three encounters from the game's own:** someone calls another out before the street over something real between them (`[Encounters] PublicCallouts`), a husband loses his temper with his wife outside their home (`DomesticDisputes`), and one of a town's people lies hogtied off the road with two men over them (`Hogtied`). Each person in them answers by their own mind.
- **More people deal with you.** What someone feels about you shows for hours as you pass, a hard look or a tipped hat (`[Mind] MindPassingLasts`); someone in money trouble may come and ask you a favour, which Talk can then settle (`[Scenes] FavoursAsked`); a witness may tell you to your face what they think of what you did, and someone who thinks well of you may warn you of a man who's after you or the law asking about you (`[Scenes] WarnedYou`, words only).
- **Chance meetings chosen by the two minds** (`[Mind] MindChanceMeetings`, on): friends talk and sing, foes quarrel or turn their backs, the sore snap, someone with money worries trades or asks the other for a loan, drink tells tall tales. And enRAGE's townsfolk court each other too (`[Scenes] CourtFolkAges`).
- **Violence against Arthur is on as it comes:** the supper invitation (`[Encounters] SupperInvitation`) and the hogtied's men drawing on you unprovoked (`[Encounters] HogtiedMenDraw`). Set either false to turn it off. The supper invitation was off as it came, so an ini from 0.7.11 or before has it turned on as it upgrades: set it false again to keep it off.
- **The AI model is optional.** RealNPCs runs without it and without Ollama, with no extra graphics memory, and everything new here works without it: people's own minds choose, and with the model on it only picks among the same few things. The readmes now say which graphics cards have room for the model beside the game, and richer without the model is the next big piece of work (roadmap 41).
- Also: people look up from their seats, watch the street and make sure to be seen; a choice they couldn't act on is tried again; the street's scenes take turns (fewer sharp words); chance meetings get their turn, nearer you; the same faces less often; quarrels in a chair had out in words; fewer of the AI model's thoughts thrown away; Show minds and the wake stages over heads on the settings panel's UI page, which now shows in its short view too; the panel in 12 pages, not 16; and many fixes.
- Seen in game by Lex: it loads clean; people get up from benches and walls, and since the fix nobody is left frozen; the storyteller runs. Near Saint Denis he played the hogtied captive once, through its Testing row: the game's own hogtie took, the leader warned him off, the two men came at him and were killed, and she was cut loose with the game's prompt and thanked him with the game's own clip. Two people still can't come to blows while one of them is sat or leaning (Lex's brawl test is refused for it, and such quarrels are had out in words); getting them up to fight is next. In Lex's log but not yet watched on screen: second scenes played to the end, chance meetings chosen by the minds (one by the AI model), and the looks as you pass (a spit and hard looks among them). Not yet seen in game: the callout and the domestic; the hogtied met by chance, its price paid, its leader drawing unprovoked and its captive carried off; favours, warnings, courting townsfolk, the cold shoulder and the loan, people walking to a spot of the kind they want, and the supper invitation (in any version).

### People get up and go about what their minds choose (settings version 168)
- **People get up and go about what their minds choose** (`[Mind] MindActsScenarios`, on; settings version 168). Someone sat or leaning whose mind wants a rest elsewhere, a meal, a drink, the work, the saloon, the store, church, home, a stroll or someone to talk to now gets up the way the waking do (asked out of their scenario the way the game's own scripts do it, never pulled out of the chair while in view, the game's reactions held off while they get up; anyone not out in 9 seconds is put back in their scenario, never left as is; once up, they stand 3 seconds before going on) and goes to it. If a fight, a flight or a fall takes them on the way, they are let go with no more orders; someone in a fight's ring or a wake act is left be. Rests, meals, drinks and work go to a spot of the game's of that kind nearby (a bench, a chair or the steps; a wall, a post or a rail; the bar rail or a table with a glass; a table with a plate; a broom, a cloth, a hammer, the tally), where before the nearest spot of any kind was taken (work became a smoke), else to the bar, else something fitting where they stand. Someone who wants to talk joins two already talking first; someone who keeps watch near you turns to watch you. Being at it already counts only where the scenario really is what they chose (a smoke or a stare is no meal). One starts every 4 to 6 seconds near you, three at most at once (counting the walk to the bar, home or a talk), never two within 8 m; a spot never reached is skipped for 5 minutes. For everyone, with the switch on or off: anyone acting on their mind who is aimed at is let go at once, and someone sat who is walked up to only looks round (never turned out of the seat). The AI model may offer people sat or leaning "gets up and ..." choices, at most every 2 minutes each. F7 `TEST minds acting:` stage 1 counts (got up, walked to, took up by kind, joined, watched you, what failed), log `MIND gets up:`, `MIND took up:`, `MIND gave up:`. Testing row: "A seated person gets up and acts on their mind". Seen in game by Lex: people getting up from benches and walls, with the fix below. Not yet seen in game: the walk to a spot of the kind they want, and joining two who are talking.
- **Nobody left frozen when asked to get up** (Lex in Valentine: "some NPCs nearby do seem frozen"). Getting someone up out of a seat, a lean, the bar or a smoke used to be one bare "stop what you are doing", which the game mostly ignored or answered by stopping the scenario's animation and leaving them stood frozen in its pose (3 of 23 got up); smokers and drinkers standing were caught as well as the seated, and nobody put them right. Now it is done the way Rockstar's own scripts do it: the scenario is told to play its exit and the next task is given at once (asked once more, the game's guarded way, if they never began the exit); anyone not out in 9 seconds (12 if mid-exit) is put back on their own spot, or in their scenario where they stand, or sent on their way, then looked at 3 seconds on and taken out of it the hard way onto a walk if that didn't take. The same for the waking's get-up, the end of a wake act (someone still in a scenario is now left in it), a fall's sit on the ground, and the two men told to fight or a joiner getting up off the bar; when one is pulled out after 8 seconds it is now Rockstar's forced way (detached from the seat or bar, the next task the same moment), the likely cause of the T pose. Log `MIND up:` / `WAKE up:` with the exit taken and the scenario flags seen on the way, `MIND restore:` / `WAKE restore:` and `restore ok` / `restore failed`; F7 `TEST minds acting:` has "out of a scenario:" with the counts by path. Someone let go halfway through getting up (aimed at, taken by a fight, a flee or a scene, a mission starting) is looked at once that's over and put back if still stuck. The hard way out is only used out of sight (past 100 m or with the screen faded), as Rockstar's scripts do; in view it is the gentler exit, and the hard way waits until they're off camera. A scenario not in the names table is never restarted where they stand (it might be a chair). A waking get-up that was put back ends the hold there, so the next act no longer cuts into it. Seen in game by Lex: nobody left frozen.

### Two street scenes at once (settings version 168)
- **Two street scenes at once** (`[Scenes] MainScenesAtOnce`, 2; 1 is one at a time as before; settings version 168). While one full scene plays, a second can start between two other people at least 20 m from everyone in the first: a reason of their own, else a chance meeting, with its own walk-over and someone walking up, its words as subtitles when it is near you. Fights, rough handling, thefts, drunks and falls stay one at a time (the second scene never has them), and the quiet talks of `ScenesAtOnce` still come on top. F7 `TEST reasons:` "full scenes now N of 2", second scenes started and refused; log `SCENE (second)`. Offline, on a synthetic street, about twice the full scenes an hour. In Lex's log, second scenes start and play to the end; not yet watched on screen.

### The storyteller (settings version 167)
- **The storyteller** (`[World] Storyteller`, lively; calm, wild or off; on the Essentials page; settings version 167). It notices how long it has been since something worth stopping for happened within about 30 m of you (a fight, a theft, a standoff, a song or tall tale, a mourner, someone coming up to you, an encounter on the road; never the everyday sharp words, small talk or gossip) and, when it has been quiet too long (2 minutes in lively, 4 calm, 1 wild), shortens the waits the world already has: the next street scene and chance meeting, the quiet talks, the walk-ups' looks and odds, the rest between one person's scenes and the encounter rolls on the road; the chance meeting goes first more often and the main scene comes nearer. After something big it waits a little longer for 30 to 60 seconds; it never holds anything back and the hush stays off. It starts nothing itself. In a mission, a fight, while wanted, indoors (not a saloon) or with the settings panel open, everything is as built. Calm is a quieter world than before; off is exactly as before. F7 `TEST story:`, log `STORY` lines. Offline, a simulated hour on a busy street: about 4.1 things worth stopping for near you every 10 minutes as built, 3.5 calm, 5.4 lively, 8.1 wild, with as many of the people's own reason scenes as before (lively and wild); on the road 1.8, 1.8, 2.9 and 4.6 encounters an hour. Seen running in Lex's game (its `STORY` lines move between quiet, even, busy and held); what it does to the pace isn't measured in game yet.

### Three encounters from the game's own (settings version 169)
- **Called out before the street** (`[Encounters] PublicCallouts`, on; `PublicCalloutChance` 35; settings version 169). Re-staged from one of the game's own random encounters (a townsman hails a man across the street and has it out with him in front of everyone), with the town's own people: by day in a town, away from the noon callout's hour, one of the people about with something real against another (a quarrel gone sour, a beating, a theft, a woman, or a debt he's owed) calls him over and says it out loud. Each then answers by their own mind, or the AI model picks among the same few things (with `[Mind] LanguagePicks`): the one called out lets it go by, owns up, gives it back or squares up; the caller takes it, plays it to the onlookers, mocks him, warns him or walks off; up to two people near watch, jeer, step in or walk off. They use the game's own clips for that encounter (with the street's gestures when a clip won't play; the log says which played). Two who square up go to the street's quarrel, fists as before. Walk right up and it ends there; draw on the caller and his hands go up. F7 `TEST callouts:`, log `ENCOUNTER callout:` and `ENCOUNTER pick`, `ENCOUNTER clips:`. Testing row: "Someone calls out another before the street". Not yet seen in game.
- **A husband's temper** (`[Encounters] DomesticDisputes`, on; `DomesticChance` 30). Re-staged from the game's own random encounter of a man berating his wife outside their door: in a town from eight in the morning to nine at night, one of the town's men starts in on his wife in the street over what is really on his mind (money, his drinking, another man, the life he thought he'd have). His sweetheart, if she's standing near, plays her; else his wife walks up the street to him. Turn by turn, each by their own mind or the AI model among the same few things: he rants (the game's own clips for it), storms off, lets it drop when he sees you or the law watching, or now and then strikes her (rare: about one in a hundred first turns for a plain sober man, one in seven for a hot, hard man in his cups; never twice, never more than a blow that knocks her down, never a sober coward or a loyal man); she gives it back, pleads, sobs, shrinks back or walks off (runs, if he struck her); a neighbour watches, steps in, fetches a lawman near or walks on. Walk up before he's struck her and he backs off or has words for you, and it's over. Once he has struck her, dealing with him is no crime while it lasts (put back after, as with the stalker); go for him and the fight is the game's, and she thanks you. F7 `TEST domestic:`, log `ENCOUNTER domestic:`. Testing row: "A husband loses his temper outside his home". Not yet seen in game.
- **Hogtied by the road** (`[Encounters] Hogtied`, on; `HogtiedChance` 20). Re-staged from the game's own random encounter of a woman tied up off the road by a kidnapper: away from the towns from six in the morning to eight at night, once a day, one of a nearby town's regulars (a man or a woman, as the town knows them) lies hogtied out of sight off the road, a mark on the map, with two men standing by: bounty hunters if they're a known thief (a pickpocket, a lifter, a mugger), a creditor's men over a debt, else the region's outlaws. The captive calls for help, bargains, curses or keeps quiet by their nature (the first call can be the AI model's pick); as you come near, the men's leader warns you off, names a price (greet him to pay it; a test takes nothing), packs up and goes, or (with `[Encounters] HogtiedMenDraw`, on as it comes since settings version 172) draws on you unprovoked; with it off the men fight only once you step in (fire on them, rush outlaws, hold a gun on them, or cut the captive loose unpaid), as the execution's men. Outlaws are fair game from the start; the others once they fight; bounty hunters never start one, and shooting them first is a crime like any other. Cut the captive loose with the game's own prompt, and they thank you and go home, or run (a thief cut from a bounty mostly runs); ride on and the men carry them off. If the game's hogtie doesn't take, they kneel bound instead (the log says which). F7 `TEST hogtied:`, log `ENCOUNTER hogtied:`. Testing row: "Stage a hogtied captive nearby". Played once by Lex through that row, near Saint Denis: the game's own hogtie took (`is hogtied (the game's own, TASK_KNOCKED_OUT_AND_HOGTIED: seen to take ...)`), the leader chose to warn, the two men came at him and were killed (outside the encounter's own step-in: no `men fight you` line), she was cut loose with the game's prompt (`is loose (you cut them free)`) and thanked him, `script_re@kidnap_victim/thank_you_a_victim` seen playing. Not yet seen in game: one met by chance, a price paid, the men fighting you by the encounter's own rule, the leader drawing unprovoked, and a captive carried off or running.

### What they feel about you, a favour, warnings, and townsfolk courting (settings version 170)
- **What they feel about you lasts as you pass** (`[Mind] MindPassingLasts`, on; settings version 170). Someone you roughed up, scared, shamed or helped shows it when you pass them later (a hard look, a spit, a sullen look away, a glance, eyes down, or a hat tip and a greeting), for hours after, not just the half hour it was; from 12 m, not 9. None had been seen in three and a half hours of play. F7 `TEST nods you:` counts who was passed over and why; Testing row "Someone near shows what they feel about you as you pass". In Lex's log (a spit and hard looks among them), not yet watched on screen.
- **A favour asked** (`[Scenes] FavoursAsked`, on, with `[Mind] MindLive`). In town, someone at the worst of a money trouble a sum of yours could mend (a debt of $11, a tooth that wants a $3 dentist, $5 short of a fare) may come up to you when you've stopped, and by their nature ask outright, tell you the trouble and leave the asking unsaid, ask only for a dollar, or lose their nerve and bid you good day (with the AI model on, it may choose among the same). Once they've said it, Talk on them lists the fix ("Let me settle that debt ($11)"), and settling it is a kindness they may thank you for later. Walk on and they leave you be for the rest of that trouble. The proud and the cold come only to someone they think well of or who was kind to them; nobody afraid of you, sore at you or mourning one you killed. F7 `TEST favours:`; Testing row "Someone in trouble comes to ask a favour". Not yet seen in game.
- **Witnessed, or warned** (`[Scenes] WarnedYou`, on, with `[Mind] MindLive`). In town, when you've stopped, someone who saw you do violence today and isn't afraid of you may stare you down, or (a hothead or a proud man) walk up and tell you to your face what they think of it, in the game's own voice; someone who thinks well of you (fond of you, grateful, or a loyal soul you've met) may come and warn you quietly that a man near is after you (one still angry at you, or mourning one you killed), or that the law is asking about something you did here, with a deputy close; or just catch your eye and look toward the danger. Each by their own mind (the AI model may choose among the same). Words, looks and turning away only. F7 `TEST warned:`; Testing row "Someone warns you, or tells you to your face what they saw". Not yet seen in game.
- **enRAGE's townsfolk court too** (`[Scenes] CourtFolkAges`, on). enRAGE keeps no age for its townsfolk, so the street's courtships never took one of them in (none had been seen); now an age is guessed from who they are (the same guess their worries and ties already use), for courting only. F7 `TEST reasons:` ends with the courtships made and why the rest were passed over. Not yet seen in game.

### Chance meetings chosen by the two minds (settings version 171)
- **Chance meetings chosen by the two minds** (`[Mind] MindChanceMeetings`, on, with `[Mind] MindLive`). What passes between two people who meet by chance near you (a song, sharp words, a trade, a tall tale) was rolled from the place and the two people's sexes and ages alone. Now the kinds the place allows are leaned by who the two are: what lies between them (friends pass the time and sing; foes come to words, square up or turn their backs), their day (the sore snap; the glad talk and, in a saloon, sing), their worry (money sends them to a trade or to asking for a loan, a feud to an argument), drink (tall tales and songs) and their nature. Two small scenes are new, and only the minds choose them: a cold shoulder between two people already on bad terms, and someone with a money worry asking the other for a loan, given or refused by the other's nature (a refusal leaves the asker sore). With `[Mind] LanguagePicks` the AI model may pick among the three likeliest: the two stand still for up to 2.5 s while it picks, and a late or empty answer leaves it to their own minds. Off, the old roll exactly. F7 `TEST chance meetings:`, log `CHANCE`. In Lex's log, one of them chosen by the AI model; the cold shoulder and the loan not yet seen in game.

### Violence against Arthur on as it comes (settings version 172)
- Lex: "violence against Arthur, the player, should be switched on by default." Anything where people turn on Arthur himself is on as it comes, each with its own switch to turn it off. On now: `[Encounters] SupperInvitation` (at dusk on the roads a too-friendly man asks you to supper; follow him and you wake in a ditch at first light with most of your cash gone, $250 at most) and `[Encounters] HogtiedMenDraw` (the hogtied's leader may draw on you unprovoked as you come within 25 m: outlaws about 2 times in 9, nearly half the time with a hothead leading; a creditor's men seldom; bounty hunters never). The supper invitation was off as it came, so an ini from before this version has it turned on as it upgrades; the upgrade can't tell the old default from an off of your own, so set it false again if you want it off. `Settings: violence against Arthur is on as it comes (Lex): ...` is logged first, then each change (`Settings: brought up to date: SupperInvitation off -> on (violence against Arthur on by default).`); the upgrade's line for settings version 169 still calls HogtiedMenDraw off, and the line for 172 after it is the one that holds. Neither has been seen in game (the one time Lex played the hogtied, its leader chose to warn), and the supper invitation never has, in any version: whether it takes the right sum, the jump to first light and how you wake are unproven.

### More of what people's minds choose reaches the screen (settings version 166)
- **Quarrels in a chair are had out in words.** When two people with a reason to come to blows can't, because one of them is sat or leaning, they have it out in words over the same matter instead of nothing happening. A pair that can't be played at all is passed over for a while, so others nearby get their turn. In Lex's game such quarrels have gone this way, and the brawl test is refused for the same reason: two still can't come to blows while one of them is sat or leaning. Getting them up to fight is next.
- **People watch, look up and make sure to be seen** (`[Mind] MindActsSeatedLooks`, on; settings version 166). Someone whose mind says to keep watch now looks at you when you're close and in view, or at whoever is about, or stands and watches the street; before, only those who had seen you violent did anything. People sat or leaning look up from their seat at you, a friend or someone to talk to, without being made to get up. Someone who wants to be seen turns to the street and smokes or stands there. Someone already at what they chose (a drink at the bar, a rest on a bench) counts as doing it. Testing row: "Someone sat or leaning looks up".
- **A choice they couldn't act on is tried again** (`[Mind] MindRetries`, on). Someone whose mind chose something while they were sat, on a horse, running or busy tries again a minute later (up to three times), the AI model asked first; a choice that has been acted on is never repeated. The AI model is now asked for people sat or leaning too, offered only what they can do from where they sit (a look, staying put, or a reason of their own with someone near), and the outer ring's half-and-half coin is dropped while the model is idle.
- **The street's scenes take turns** (`[Scenes] ReasonVariety`, on). Someone at the worst of a worry says so once while you're about, not to everyone they meet, and the same kind of scene three times running is less likely. Sharp words had been more than half of all street scenes; in a simulated street they fall from about 80% to 25%, and gossip, thefts, good and bad news, courting and fights get their turns.
- **The same faces less often.** A high `TroubleWeight` still makes fights likelier, but now shortens the rest between one person's scenes by half at most (it was a fifth at 5: 24 seconds, and one man was in seven scenes in three minutes). A widow's or a posse's scene is followed by 3-5 minutes before the next one, and a town mourns one of your dead on screen once a game day.
- **Chance meetings get their turn, nearer you** (`[Scenes] ChanceFirst`, on). When nobody near has a strong reason, the chance meeting goes first, so songs, tall tales, haggling, standoffs and card cheats show up again (a reason had taken every turn: one chance meeting in an evening). With six or more people free, the street's main scene leans harder to the ones close to you, and two people sat or leaning a few steps apart are no longer picked for a scene they never walk to. A high `TroubleWeight` now brings more fights rather than a street of arguments (at 5, arguments were half of all chance meetings; now about one in six, with fists more common).
- **Fewer of the AI model's thoughts thrown away.** With a pick, the model is now told the same limits and shown the same examples as for any other thought, and answers with the number first, so a cut-off answer keeps its choice. A thought a little too long is cut back at its last comma or full stop instead of being dropped. "He", "His" and "Thy" for God, "Pa'd", "Ma'll", Main Street, the widow, the livery, the station, the states and Baton Rouge no longer count as names it wasn't given; names it wasn't given (Abigail among them) still do. The waking are told which openings of their last thoughts not to repeat.
- F7 counts more: `TEST reasons:` adds courtships, blows played as words, pairs passed over, the street lately, main scenes by kind and distance, chance-first turns, far pairs, "full scenes now N of 2" and second scenes; `TEST minds acting:` adds why choices didn't act, retries, the get-up's counts and "out of a scenario:"; `TEST model:` and `TEST picks:` are split into everyday, waking and Talk, with seated asks and "reached the screen N of M". What these changes do to the street hasn't been measured in game yet.

### Fixes and smaller changes
- A pick the AI model made for someone now stands until their own mind changes its choice. A street scene that fails to start no longer makes its people wait out a rest. The model is no longer offered a scene for two people far apart who are both held in chairs.
- Someone going over to another for a word goes only to a grown person on foot, never a lawman, and never to someone another is already walking to. A line said between other people is no longer replaced by another speaker's within 2.5 s, unless that speaker is nearer you.
- When someone is turned down after asking for help (a coin in the street, or a loan), a friend who takes it up now goes after the one who refused; before, it went after the one who had asked.
- The stalker never takes anyone RealNPCs is already using. A lawman handed back while he's fighting you, or a stalker left to enRAGE, is now properly released to the game (before, the game could never clear them away). A gun on any lawman waiting to be handed back (the domestic's, or a brawl's after the rope) gives him straight back to the game.
- The new encounters and walk-ups keep out of each other's way: a street encounter starts at least 12 m from a full scene's people, and the second scene keeps 12 m from an encounter; a gun on a callout's onlooker, or on anyone in a domestic but a man who has struck her, ends it (the two in a callout answer a gun through its step-in); favours and warnings pass over anyone aimed at in the last 10 s; the same two have a callout at most once a game day, and the same man a domestic once a day (forgotten on a reload); the domestic's wife comes from 12-25 m off, out of sight, and you count as watching it from up to 20 m; the lawman fetched to a domestic is borrowed as the other law jobs borrow theirs and handed back with a wander about 20 s later.
- Things people do where they stand last 12-25 s; a worker with no broom, cloth or hammer near does nothing rather than crouch over nothing in the street; someone who sat down this way isn't got up again for 4 minutes.
- Frame cost: the new features' costliest moments trimmed, and the per-frame allocations gone. Not measured in game.

### The settings panel: Show minds on the UI page, the UI page in the short view, fewer pages
- Show minds (the blue-edged card over people near you) and "Wake stages over heads (with West World)" are on the panel's UI page now, beside the names over heads, not on the Testing page. The ini keeps both in `[Testing]`, and their session keys stay on the Testing page under Tools.
- The UI page (names over heads, bars, the meter, Show minds, the wake stages, the ticker, subtitles and the panel's own ways) now shows in the panel's short view too, the way it opens as it comes (`[Output] PanelShowsAll` off): Essentials, UI, Hotkeys, Mod list. It is the same page in both views, so its "Show every setting" row keeps you on it.
- The panel has 12 pages, not 16 (Lex: "consolidate as many settings as possible"): "West World and stalkers", "The world and its people" (the world, then People) and "Pacing, files and the log" (pacing, Quiet (enRAGE), files and the log) each hold their sections under their own headings, where the first of them stood. The ini keeps every section and setting, and a master switch still greys only its own section's rows. The Essentials page no longer has its "On the screen" group, since the UI page beside it holds all of those, and its pickpocket, self-defence and memory-reset rows are one group, "You", where they were three groups of one row each. Encounters, Street scenes, Romance, The mind, UI, Hotkeys, Testing, enRAGE and the Mod list are as they were.

### The readmes: the AI model is optional
- The readmes say plainly that the free local AI model is optional, highly recommended, and not needed to play. "You don't need it" comes first: RealNPCs runs without the model and without Ollama, with no extra graphics memory. A table gives the graphics card memory it takes beside RDR2 on medium settings (12 GB or more recommended; 10 GB workable at 1080p; 8 GB or less, play without it), and a slower card still runs it, only later: an answer that comes too late gives way to RealNPCs' own line or choice (after 2 s for a thought, 2.5 s for a choice, 2.8 s for a Talk answer, `[Mind] TalkModelWaitSeconds`). The list of what the model adds now says that thoughts said aloud also need a voice list RealNPCs doesn't ship, so they're silent for nearly everyone. Roadmap 41, richer without the model, is the next big piece of work.

## 0.7.11 (2026-10-31)

Talk to people, brawls that grow, a braver town, the stalker in black, and the free AI model that turns itself on.

- **Talk** (Q, locked on to someone RealNPCs knows; rebind the game's lock-on "target info" control in Settings > Controls for another key). A short list of things to say: the news, a drink, money, a warning, your name, asking after their worry, settling a debt or paying for the doctor, the dentist or a ticket, an apology, goodbye. They answer from who they are and how they stand with you, in their own voice, and what you did is remembered: a settled debt changes their story, someone you told your name says it, someone you helped may come and thank you days later, and a lonely regular may come over hoping for a word. With the AI model on, they answer in their own words. **Talk is basic for now**: a fixed list of lines. The aim is real-time talk by microphone, and typed text, with the model, where your hardware allows.
- **Brawls that grow.** A fistfight draws people in a wave at a time, from both sides, up to 12 fighting (`[Mind] BrawlMost`), friends and grudges first, most of all in a saloon with drink in it. A crowd of up to 16 gathers round and cheers. A man going down no longer ends it: another of his side leads, and it runs until a side is all down. The law runs over and breaks it up: those who stop are let off with a word, those who don't get his fists and are hogtied. Every man beaten down is knocked out (up again in 20-40 s) or killed.
- **No more hushed streets.** A killing no longer holds back a town's own fights, thefts and callouts (`[Mind] HushHours` and `HushHoursSaintDenis`, 0 as it comes; set 2 and 1 for a short quiet after the latest killing), and brawls never hush it. Before, a day of deaths and brawls kept Saint Denis quiet for up to two days.
- **A braver town** (`[World] Bravery` 85, on the Essentials page). Brave townsfolk stand their ground where the game would send them running, and a fight draws a crowd instead of scattering it. Whoever you aim at or lock on to reacts as the game has them, so hold-ups and robberies work as before.
- **The stalker in black, and more often** (`[Stalkers] StalkerBlack`, on): a stranger in black who turns up out of sight behind you, about once in 45 minutes of play (it was about once in three hours). He keeps low, crouched until he runs, shows as a red dot on the map, and dealing with him is no crime.
- **The free local AI model** (Ollama with llama3.1:8b) is now highly recommended, and RealNPCs finds it and turns it on by itself. **AI MODEL (OLLAMA)** is the switch at the top of the settings panel (Alt+R), with whether it's answering beside it.
- Also: thoughts that vary (the weather in 10% of model thoughts, 23% before); word of a killing travels slowly (2-5 game days) and only from witnesses; gatherings of the awake at any hour; lawmen carry bodies off the street; name plates stay up in crowds and after a saloon; bold thieves who try it with the law looking on; whoever you aim at is let go to the game's own reaction; Hook up on H after her yes (nothing of RealNPCs' on G); and many fixes.
- Seen in game by Lex in Saint Denis: Talk, the stalker in black (Lex picked his outfit; crouched, a red dot), the AI MODEL (OLLAMA) switch, a brawl growing to six with nine more getting up to join and the law getting involved (before its reach and crowd were raised), a lawman lifting a body, carrying it 12 m at a walk and setting it down off the street, and the names staying up after a saloon. Fixed here but not yet seen fixed: townsfolk who didn't react when aimed at, and Hook up with CasualHookups showing no prompt. Not yet seen in game: word of a killing reaching kin, what Bravery does to witnesses and robbery victims, fists that kill or knock out, the brawl's wider reach, crowd and 45 s, and the lawman jogging with the body (move speed 2).

### The street hushes only after a killing, and briefly (settings version 165)
- From Lex in Saint Denis: "I would've expected to see some brawls, or some events but not much is happening." F7 had the town at fear 1.00 and the street hushed. Every death and brawl of the day added to one fear that took two game days to fade, and the street hushed while it stayed high, so the brawls themselves kept the brawls away.
- Now only a killing hushes a street, only the latest one in that town counts, and only for `[Mind] HushHours` game hours, `HushHoursSaintDenis` in Saint Denis (new; 0-12, 0 never). Both are 0 as they come, so the street never hushes (Lex: a livelier world); 2 and 1 give a short quiet after the latest killing. Someone beaten to death in a brawl (or by anyone's bare hands but yours), a Testing row's own trouble, and brawls, roughs, shoves and standoffs never hush it. A Testing row's own trouble is what happens to the test cast, or to a test scene's people in its first five real minutes (about two and a half game hours), and any other killing in those five minutes that isn't yours: your own killing just after a test hushes the street as any other.
- A brawler shot or cut while still in the fight (by a lawman, a stray bullet, a drawn gun or you) is a killing like any other: it hushes the street and is booked with the gun or the blade, never "a beating" (the first build booked it "a beating, by your hand").
- The pickpocket asks whether the street is hushed at the moment he'd go. He used to read the street scenes' last look, which an encounter or the gap after a scene could leave a minute out of date.
- The town's fear (the safety need, people going home, the model's words) fades faster: it halves every three game hours (six before). A brawl, a ring or an assault weighs 0.1 (0.25 before), a rough or a shove 0.05 (0.15), someone beaten to death half a killing. The model is told "someone beaten to death", not "a man": women fight too.
- F7 `TEST town mood:` says how long the hush has left and which killing began it (`the street is hushed for 47 more game min ..., since death ... 13 game min ago`), or `not hushed (2 game h after a killing; the latest 190 game min ago)`, and how many killings there never hush. Log `TOWN hushed: <town> for N more game min ..., since ...` once a killing and `TOWN hush over: <town>`.

### Nobody about after a saloon: the list of people no longer comes back empty
- From Lex's test in Saint Denis: after going in and out of the saloon every name plate went for minutes, and F7 showed nobody near with people standing about. The list of everyone in the world, which every part of RealNPCs reads, came back empty: the API's own call gives an empty list, without a word, whenever anything goes wrong underneath.
- RealNPCs now reads the list once a frame, and every part reads that one list. If it fails, or comes back with nobody (never true: Arthur is on it himself), the last good list is used instead, cut to the people still there (and still people: a handle that went can come back as a wagon or a prop), for as long as the trouble lasts; nobody new shows until the list comes back. The log says so once a minute (`PEDS list: empty (indoors <id>; N kept from the last good list, N s old)`, or `failed: <why>`) and once when it is over (`PEDS list: back, N peds after N s of trouble`), and F7 has a `TEST peds:` line: how many now, how many failures and empty lists, how often the last good list stood in. (Calling Script Hook's own worldGetAllPeds directly was tried and crashed the game when it faulted, which the API's call catches; it is left unused.)

### Hook up with CasualHookups: never stuck without its prompt
- From Lex's test: Opal Ackerman, a saloon woman, said yes to the flirting and to Hook up, and the hint said to press CasualHookups' Flirt (B) next, but CasualHookups showed no B prompt at all. It shows none for a kind of woman it won't take, so nothing happened.
- Now Hook up (H) on a willing woman goes by what is known of her kind (her model):
  - CasualHookups is known to take her kind: the hint says press B for its scene. A kind is learned as taken (`hookups takes:<model>` in the save) when its scene follows a B press on a willing woman, seen from your side (your controls off or Arthur undressing). The two of you standing close never counts as its scene with a willing woman: she stands that close at the ask anyway.
  - Known not to take her kind: RealNPCs' own night at once (the fade).
  - Not known yet: the hint says press B, and if it shows no prompt for her, to wait. If no scene of CasualHookups' begins within 15 s of Hook up (10 s after a B press, if that's later), a short notice says so and RealNPCs' own night follows without her saying yes a second time. Her kind is learned as one it won't take (`hookups won't:<model>`) only when B was pressed on her and nothing followed, or after two asks with B never seen pressed (a missed press or a slow one alone proves nothing). Nothing is learned while CasualHookups may be rolling its own chances (it started the game on chances other than 0/100/0, which it may keep till the next start).
  - A watch that saw the two of you close but never a scene, after 60 s, now gives RealNPCs' own night too (if she's still there), so her yes is never left hanging.
- Every branch is logged: `HOOKUPS ask: ... has taken her kind before, so press B`, `HOOKUPS ask: ... not known yet whether CasualHookups takes her kind; press B, and if no scene of its begins within 15 s the night is RealNPCs' own`, `HOOKUPS key pressed on ... N s after Hook up`, `HOOKUPS with ... no sign of its scene N s after Hook up (its key never seen pressed: its prompt may never have shown)`, `HOOKUPS: <model> not learned yet: its key was never seen pressed (once; ...)`, `HOOKUPS: <model> not learned as a kind it won't take: its chances may be its own (...) until the next start`, `HOOKUPS: CasualHookups started this game with chances ...`, `HOOKUPS: CasualHookups takes <model>` / `won't take <model>`, and `HOOKUPS: CasualHookups won't take <name>'s kind (<model>), so her Hook up is RealNPCs' own night` at her yes.
- With RealNPCs not deciding (`[Romance] Romance`, `Trysts` or `CasualHookupsDecides` off), CasualHookups now gets its own chances back. Before, RealNPCs left it alone but its chances stayed at RealNPCs' "always gives in" (0/100/0), so its B Flirt worked on every woman. RealNPCs puts back the three chances from its copy in `RealNPCs\backup\CasualHookups.ini.before-realnpcs`, and sets 0/100/0 again when it decides once more. It only moves between those two sets, so chances you set on the panel stand. Its key stays on B (G is enRAGE's). This was chosen over holding its key on every woman for good: off means the two mods apart, which is CasualHookups with its own dice. CasualHookups may read its settings only when the game starts, so while it started this game on RealNPCs' 0/100/0, its key (never G) is held on every woman until the game restarts; from the next start it is left alone. RealNPCs notes the chances it started with in `RealNPCs\backup\CasualHookups.chances-at-start`, so an Insert reload still knows. Log `HOOKUPS: RealNPCs doesn't decide now, so CasualHookups has its own chances back` / `RealNPCs decides again`, then `HOOKUPS: RealNPCs doesn't decide, but CasualHookups started this game always giving in (0/100/0), so its Flirt (B) is held on every woman until the game restarts` or `... started this game with chances ..., so it's left alone with its own dice`, and `HOOKUPS held (not deciding): ...` on a press.

### Keys, brawls, aim: review fixes
- Flirt on H: a Hook up press that came to nothing (she was in a fight, the lock-on dropped, too far off) no longer makes the next Flirt press, even on another woman, the Hook up ask. Every press is read once.
- A plain two-man fistfight that nobody joined or came to join gets no fate roll after it ends. During it, those two were already spared. A man who goes down after a brawl while its scene still plays its after-fight words is taken out of that scene, and the scene ends ("a man down after the fight").
- The 15 s watch after a fight starts afresh each fight. A quiet moment left over from the last fight ended the next one's watch at once, so the game's own knockouts went unrolled again.
- A fight with even one joiner, or someone on the way, now counts as a brawl for the scene's own cap: it holds within 80 m, and runs to the fight's furthest end (`BrawlLeastSeconds` plus the time people coming may keep it going), so the fight's own rules end it. When the cap does end it, the log says `it ran out of time (N s)`, not `the player left`.
- Aimed at:
  - A walk-up to Arthur is now ended for real: a grudge, a thanks, a lonely one, a relative (not one made for the part), a pickpocket's walk into you. Before, it was only named, and the grudge walker said his line over the hands-up, then walked off. A rescue's thanks ends too.
  - The pickpocket about to run, a theft's thief and a wedding are kept on purpose, and logged as `kept: ...`. They no longer count as a hold let go, or bring the 5 s second look.
  - An onlooker's acts still to come (the hurry after a freeze) and any wander of ours due later go with the onlooking, and their stand-still or anim is cleared.
  - A lawman on a job of ours (a body to clear, a fight to break up) has the job ended. He's handed straight back to the game: events unblocked, KEEP_TASK off, no wander. Before, he was "an encounter of RealNPCs'" and carried on deaf to the gun.
  - A waker on despair's last steps, handed back by the act's end, is now logged as let go, not "left to it".
  - Only the man enRAGE can question is left to it. Before, while enRAGE questioned anyone, everyone else Arthur aimed at was left held for a minute. One of enRAGE's people is still left alone while it questions, and looked at again 3 s later.
  - For 10 s after the aim, nothing of ours takes them up again: a brawl's join, a street scene (the aftermath's tend included), an onlooker's act or a wander. The mind's acts, the ring and a conversation already waited.

### Aimed at, let go to the game
- From Lex in Saint Denis: the first few townsfolk he aimed at didn't react ("stuck from previous scenes possibly"). Later a woman put her hands up and begged, the game's own answer.
- Every frame, whoever Arthur free-aims at, or locks on to with a gun in his hand, is looked at once per person (again after a minute, or 5 s after something of ours was let go). Whatever of RealNPCs' still holds them ends at once, so the game's own reaction takes over. The unarmed lock-on that Talk and Flirt use never counts.
- What is ended:
  - A street scene they're cast in (stopped: "someone in it was aimed at"), or their place in its fight (dropped from the sides).
  - The fight's ring, or their walk over to join.
  - A waking act or hold.
  - A mind's act.
  - An onlooker's act.
  - The Talk menu.
  - A conversation in real voices.
  - Bravery's PCF 44, put back as the game had it for 20 s.
- Walk-ups to Arthur, a rescue's thanks and a wedding are named in the log. A man knocked out in a brawl is left down. RealNPCs' own encounter cast (made or borrowed) is only logged ("left to it").
- As a safety net, an ambient person's events are always unblocked. One of enRAGE's people is unblocked only when a hold of ours had them. Another script's people are never touched. Never during enRAGE's questioning, or for a man it can question at gunpoint: log `AIM: <name> left to enRAGE's questioning`.
- Log `AIM: <name> was held by <what, what>; let go to the game (events unblocked)`, or `AIM: <name>: nothing of ours held them`, so a stuck case shows either way. F7 `TEST aim:` counts them.
- For 10 s after he aims at someone, nothing of ours starts on them: the mind's acts, the fight ring, or a conversation in real voices. A RealVoice talk began on a man at gunpoint at 15:46:03.

### Brawls reach further, gather a crowd, last longer (settings version 164)
- From Lex's Saint Denis brawl (15:35): it grew to six with nine more getting up to join from both sides, but it was over about 15 s after the first blow. The lawman 41 m off found it over, and two men were knocked out by the game's own blows after it ended, before any roll.
- `[Mind] BrawlJoinMetres` (new, 30; 10-60): onlookers within this many metres may join (18 fixed before). The pool is scanned 12 m beyond it. The brawl that comes at you (`BrawlPullsYouIn`) keeps its 18 m: it's the people beside you, not the street.
- A joiner more than 10 m from the man he's going for runs over first (events held on the way), and is in the fight once within 6 m. One getting up off a bench or the bar is taken off it at once if still on it 6 s on (as the Director does for the two who began it), then runs over if the fight is across the street. Each on his way has about 1.6 s a metre plus 4 s (6-30 s). Logs `BRAWL <name> runs over to join in, N m off, for <name>`, `BRAWL <name> was still sat or leaning N s on: pulled out of it to join in`; with DebugLog, `BRAWL <name> never got there`.
- Those on their way count for their side. While anyone is coming, the fight runs at least 6 s more each moment and the first two's bruises don't end it. A side whose last man goes down while one of its own is on the way waits for him (10 s at most), and he leads it when he arrives. Logs `SCENE fight: a man down, the last of his side standing; N of theirs on the way, so it waits for them`, `SCENE fight: one of the fallen side's own comes and leads it now`, `SCENE fight: nobody of the fallen side came in time`.
- Someone picked to join keeps the side he first took for the whole fight. Before, Henrietta Ellison was picked for one side and then the other.
- `[Mind] BrawlLeastSeconds` (new, 45; 15-120): a brawl (two or more joined) goes on at least this long from its first blows, unless a side is all down. How long joiners can keep it going is now at least this plus 15 s (45 s before). A brawl's scene runs past its usual 90 s to its least time plus a minute, and holds while you're within 80 m (50 before).
- The crowd: `[Mind] BrawlCrowdMetres` (new, 40; 10-60): people this close to a fight (20 m fixed before) notice it and walk over to watch and cheer (`[Scenes] FightCrowds`). Up to 6 round two men, up to 16 round a brawl of three or more, on a second ring 7 m out. A busy street fills it, four a look. In a brawl the sat and the leaning get up to come and see. They get 40 s to walk there (10 s before), and a bigger ring cheers more often. The ring belongs to the fight now, not to its two leaders, so it no longer breaks up and re-forms each time a downed leader is replaced. Log `RING N gather round the fight (M in the ring, up to 16, from within 40 m; K get up to come)`.
- The law notices a brawl from `BrawlCrowdMetres` + 5 m (35 m at least; 35 before).
- The game's own knockouts: the Director now drops a fighter at 45% of the health he came in with (30% before), since one blow took a man from over 30% to dead between checks. The first two are checked too once anyone has joined or is coming. They used to be checked only in a brawl of four. For 15 s after a fight ends (or until nobody of it has been in melee for 2 s), anyone of it found knocked down, or dead by fists, gets his fate rolled as in the fight, with the man he was going for as the one on him. Log `BRAWL down (after the fight): <name> is down N s after it ended; his fate is rolled`. F7's brawl line counts them.
- F7: the join line gives the radius, the least seconds, how many ran over, how many never got there, how many are on the way, and the ring now. The ring line gives the biggest ring, the radius and the caps.
- Not done: the fighter flags that make a strong blow knock out instead of kill (PCF 21 with 456). They're untested, and the after-the-fight watch covers those knockouts.

### Hook up is H, after her yes
- Hook up is on H now, the same key as Flirt (Lex: G clashed with enRAGE's Give Money). The lock-on menu's H prompt reads Flirt. Only once a woman has said yes to your flirting, is willing for the night, and her answer has been said, the same prompt is made again as "Hook up". It never shows before a yes, and never on a no.
- Once you've asked (with CasualHookups, its night), the prompt goes back to Flirt, and CasualHookups' own Flirt (moved to B) takes over. If CasualHookups starts nothing and RealNPCs' own night can't be had just then, Hook up shows on H again so you can ask her once more.
- Nothing of RealNPCs' is on G (`INTERACT_OPTION1`) any more: the second prompt is gone. CasualHookups' key move from G to B and RealNPCs never holding G are unchanged.
- The notices say "Hook up (H)". The one for a woman who liked your flirting but won't go further no longer tells you to press Hook up, since it doesn't show for her. Log `FLIRT Hook up pressed: <name>` as before. F7's flirt line says when the prompt is Hook up.
- Reminder: with `[Romance] Romance = false`, neither Flirt nor Hook up shows.

### Talk on Q, rebindable in the game (settings version 164)
- Talk in the lock-on menu of someone RealNPCs knows is on Q, the game's own lock-on "target info" control (`INPUT_INTERACT_LOCKON_TARGET_INFO`; a pad's RB), with that control's own icon beside "Talk". To use another key (e.g. T), rebind that control in the game's Settings > Controls: Talk and its icon follow. A control rebound to T also turns the ticker on the same press (`TickerNextKey` is T), and next to a telegraph clerk opens enRAGE's telegraph too (T is its key).
- `[World] TalkKey` (new, `Q`) sets it, on the settings panel's Hotkeys page. Q means the control itself, whatever key the game gives it. Any other key (e.g. T) is read by RealNPCs itself while the Talk prompt is up: the prompt then reads "Talk (T)" beside the control's own icon (a script can't change the icon), the control's own press does nothing for it, a pad's RB still works, and that key opens Talk in place of whatever it does otherwise (a T set here doesn't turn the ticker while the prompt is up). Only on that path does Talk stand aside on the keyboard next to a station or telegraph clerk (within 3.5 m), because T is enRAGE's telegraph key there (log `TALK stands aside`). `None`: no keyboard key, a pad's RB only.
- An ini that holds `TalkKey = T` keeps T, read directly, until it is set back to Q.
- Log `TALK key: the game's lock-on target-info control (...)` (or `TALK key: T read directly by RealNPCs (TalkKey isn't Q) ...`) at the start and whenever TalkKey changes, and `TALK menu opened on ... (the Talk control (Q unless rebound))`, `(the Talk prompt (RB))` or `(T, read directly)`. F7's Talk line says which.

### The lawman jogs with the body
- He jogs with it now (move speed 2; it was 1), on the walk to the spot, the nav-mesh walk and the straight walk; the final set-down keeps the undertaker's 1. The log's speed line shows whether the game lets a man carrying a body go that fast.
- The lawman who lifts a body now carries it somewhere he can get to: a walkable spot about `[Mind] PickMetres` less 3 m from you (12 m at least; 22 m with 25), on the far side of the body from you, not inside, near the body's level, one by a wall first. A body already lying that far out is carried 6 to 10 m further. Before, the spot was a random bearing he often couldn't reach, so he picked the body up, stood still with it and dropped it. Up to four spots are found, again from where you stand once he has hold of it. If he stands still on one (after 2.5 s: under 0.2 m/s and less than a metre gained), he tries the next; after two have failed he walks straight when the line is clear. Only when every spot fails does he set the body down where he stands, logged `no reachable spot` with how far he got. Every check logs his speed and the metres gained. The old `he stopped carrying it ... sent again` (he was still carrying it) now reads `he stands still with it, N m off (...); trying spot 2 of 4`. The game's safe spot is never inside a building now, for every feature that asks for one.

### Pre-release review fixes
- The lawman's carry: its every-frame part (the body carriable, his reset flags) now runs while your controls are off too (a shop's catalogue, the barber, a supper's or a tryst's dark), and its clocks stand still meanwhile, so he no longer drops the body or gives up because you looked at a catalogue. A body that leaves his hands during the place order more than 4 m from the spot is logged as dropped (`he dropped it on the way, N m short of the spot`), not as set down, and isn't counted as a clearing.
- **AI MODEL (OLLAMA)** and its status now head the first page when the panel shows every setting too (`PanelShowsAll` on), not only the Essentials page. The status is true when Ollama stops: while it's on and answering, Ollama is asked again every 20 s it hasn't answered anything, and as the panel opens, so a closed Ollama reads "on, NOT ANSWERING" within seconds, not after five failed asks.
- The AI model is never turned on by itself while the settings panel is open (the panel's next save would have turned it off again unseen); it waits until you close it. Language found on at a start now counts as your choice (`LanguageChosen = true`), so an off you set by hand later, with the game closed, is never undone.
- A worry kept across enRAGE's take-up: the linked scenes between the two (the debt, the feud, courting), whether someone is spoken for, who is told of a death, and the other side's concern once the worrier has died now find the link (kept under the resident's id) for enRAGE's person too. The resident whose worry they live on with is dropped from the save last of all, so the worry can't change when the save is full. Someone whose own worry Arthur mended under an earlier build keeps that worry.
- Gatherings: after loading an earlier save, the gap since the last gathering no longer counts from a gathering that hasn't happened yet.
- Being watched: a thief by the church on a Sunday waits even with a lawman in sight (a bold one went ahead before).
- `[Mind] OnlookersRunning` is 1-8 (above 8 did nothing more), `TalkModelWaitSeconds` 1-7 (a request to the model gives up at 8 s). On the Mind page `HushFear` and `HushFearSaintDenis` sit under the town-mood switch they belong to, before `MindTempers`.
- The package readme's `[Mind] Language` entry says it is turned on by itself, and has `LanguageChosen`.

### AI MODEL (OLLAMA) at the top of the Essentials page, and found by itself (settings version 163)
- The settings panel's Essentials page opens on one large gold switch, **AI MODEL (OLLAMA)** (`[Mind] Language`), set apart from the rest, ON or OFF on the right, a line on what it does, and its status beside the name: "on, answering", "on, checking...", "on, NOT ANSWERING (is Ollama running? is llama3.1:8b pulled?)" in red, or "off".
- Found by itself (Lex chose this): at each start, off the game thread with a 2.5 s timeout, RealNPCs asks Ollama at `LanguageUrl` `/api/tags` whether it runs and holds `LanguageModel`. If it does, Language is off and you have never chosen, Language is turned on in the ini and a one-time notice says "AI model found: people near you now think in their own words. Turn it off at the top of the settings panel (Alt+R)."
- `[Mind] LanguageChosen` (new, false): set true by itself whenever you change Language, in the panel or in the ini by hand, so a model you turned off stays off. An ini of an earlier version with Language on keeps it on as your choice. A model turned on this way once and off since (in the ini with the game closed) isn't turned on again (the save's `told:ai model`).
- Log `LANGUAGE detect: Ollama at ... holds llama3.1:8b; [Mind] Language turned on (you hadn't chosen), and you were told`, `LANGUAGE detect: no Ollama answering at ... ; [Mind] Language stays off (asked again at the next start)`, `LANGUAGE detect: [Mind] Language is on already ...`, `LANGUAGE chosen: you set [Mind] Language off (in the settings panel) ...`. F7 `TEST language:` says what the detection found.
- The package readme has the "Highly recommended: the free local AI model" section of the README.

### Gatherings of the awake at any hour (`[Encounters]`)
- Lex, 2026-10-31, after seeing one: "make them happen more often and at any time of day". `GatheringHours` (new, `any`; or `night`): with `any`, besides the night's ring (midnight to 03:30 in the wilds, once a night, as it was), a ring may stand by day: in the open out in the wilds, or, with you in a town, just outside its edge (the spot itself must be outside the town; never in the street; never indoors). `night` keeps only the night's.
- `GatheringGapHours` (new, 6): game hours at least from the end of one gathering to the next, so they don't stack. A day ring doesn't use up the night's.
- A ring by day thinks its own thoughts on the ticker (no "same night"). Log `ENCOUNTER gathering by day, at the town's edge: ...` / `by day, in the open`; a refusal says `the last one was N game hours ago (at least 6, [Encounters] GatheringGapHours)`. F7's encounter line shows the hours, the gap and when the last one was.

### Bold thieves try it with the law looking on (`[Scenes] BoldThieves`)
- Lex, 2026-10-31: a bold one should try now and then even with a lawman watching ("less realistic, more exciting"). Being watched made every would-be thief wait. `BoldThieves` (new, 25, 0-100, under `BeingWatched`): that percent of would-be thieves go ahead anyway with the law looking on, one roll a day each; a hothead, a greedy one or a drunk half as likely again (38%). Both thefts: a pickpocket's reason in the street, and the lift on you (the man picked first, then his roll). By the church on a Sunday a thief still always waits. 0 is the old way.
- Log `WATCHED Abe Turner: pickpocket goes ahead, bold, with a lawman 14 m off looking on (25% a day, [Scenes] BoldThieves)`; F7 `TEST watched:` counts them. mindtest `ObservedTest`: at 0 every thief waits, at 100 none does with the law (all by the church), at 25 95 of 400 plain thieves and 147 of 400 hotheads try it.

- The numbers chosen in the NIGHT3 run (its For Lex item 8) are settings now, each beside its feature's switch and greyed while that switch is off; every default is the number as built, so nothing changes until you change one.
- `[Scenes]`, being watched: `WatchedLawMetres` 25, `WatchedRecklessChance` 40, `WatchedChurchMetres` 60. Lonely regulars: `LonelyNeed` 0.65, `LonelyLookSeconds` 45, `LonelyMetres` 18, `LonelyWaitSeconds` 15, `LonelyRestDays` 3. Late thanks: `ThanksAfterHours` 6, `ThanksWithinDays` 7, `ThanksDrinkChance` 15, `ThanksDollarChance` 35, `ThanksFiveChance` 60, `ThanksMendedChance` 90, `ThanksLoanDays` 3.

- `[Mind]`, the bully turning on whoever stepped in: `BullyTurnsHothead` 55, `BullyTurnsProud` 40, `BullyTurnsColdGreedy` 25, `BullyTurnsCoward` 3, `BullyTurnsOthers` 15, `BullyTurnsDrunk` 15 (added), `BullyTurnsMeant` 10 (taken off). Onlookers: `OnlookersRunMetres` 20, `OnlookersRunning` 4. Talk: `TalkModelWaitSeconds` 2.8. The hush: `HushFear` 0.5, `HushFearSaintDenis` 0.8. Strain: `StrainChance` 40.

### A worry kept when enRAGE takes someone up
- From Lex's Talk test (NIGHT3 For Lex 9a): after enRAGE took Hugh Lambert up, he had a different worry (none, then the drink), and a worry Arthur had mended stayed with the resident's id. Now enRAGE's person lives on with the resident's worry: the same kind, stage, ending, people and thoughts, worked out from the resident's id and facts (`Store.WorrySeedOf`, saved; the resident is kept for it), the first take-up deciding. A worry Arthur mended follows to the new id, and one he mends after the take-up counts for both.

(debt (Pruitt), worse, day 4 of 8)`; the `CARRY:` line lists "their worry". mindtest `CarryTest`: before, the two ids' worries differ on 30 of 30 days; after, the same on 30 of 30; the mend follows. `StoreLoadTest` round-trips the new save field.
- Still open from 9: (b) "told you their worry" is per session and by id; (c) a take-up while the menu is open; (d) other people's memories naming the old id.

### The lawman's carry, done the game's undertaker's way
- Lex's log, 2026-10-31: "the lawman lifts Jedediah Archer and carries it 17 m off the street", then a second later "he dropped it on the way". The walk was ordered while the lift was still going (the game says a man is carrying something from the moment he bends), and the order cancelled the lift.
- Now step for step as Rockstar's own undertaker does it: he walks over by the nav mesh until 15 m off, then one sequence walks him up to the body and lifts it; the carry counts only once the body's carrier is him and its carry state is settled (5 or 7); then his tasks are cleared, he puts his gun away, the game's place order takes the body towards the spot, a nav-mesh walk covers the rest, the place order sets it down there, and the clearing is done when the carry ends. The body's carriable flag and his reset flags are kept every frame, each for the stages the undertaker keeps them.
- Every `LAW clears:` step now logs the carry state number and who the body's carrier is; a drop counts only when the body has stopped being his for half a second. The fallbacks stay (set down where he stands after 40 s on the way or 10 s at the set-down, never left holding it, handed back to his rounds after).

### Word of a killing reaches kin slower, and not at all from nobody (settings version 162)
- Lex, 2026-10-31: "Could we slow down the amount of time it takes for news to spread about a killing? ... there was no one around to see the death so the word shouldn't have spread in this case." The telegram that came after the ambush was for an earlier killing, two game days before; the ambushers themselves made no word.
- Slower: a killing someone saw reaches the dead man's kin 2 to 5 game days on (`[Features] KinWordDaysMin` 2, `KinWordDaysMax` 5; it was 12 to 60 game hours).
- No witnesses, no word of you: a record killed with nobody alive within 35 m (the same count the killing's deed keeps), or in self-defence, reaches kin only once the body is found (enRAGE buries it, or a lawman carries it off; then 2 to 5 days from the find), or else 5 to 10 game days on (`UnseenKinWordDaysMin` 5, `UnseenKinWordDaysMax` 10). That word doesn't know it was you: grief, no letter to you, nobody rides after you, and his kin in their own town don't walk up to you over it. Someone who comes upon the body, sees you by it and lives to tell makes it witnessed; everyone who saw it dying before they told makes it unwitnessed.
- The telegram says which death: "Esther Holt got a telegram: her son Lyman is dead. Shot near Bluewater Marsh, two days ago." (how, where, and how long ago).
- Word already on its way in a save is delivered as before (as witnessed). Log `WORD of X's death will reach A and B in N h (witnessed | unwitnessed: long delay, nobody near | unwitnessed: long delay, self-defence | unwitnessed: body found, ...)`, `WORD of X's death: the body was found (a lawman carried the body off | buried); ...`, `WORD of X's death: someone saw you by the body and lived to tell; ...`, `WORD of X's death: nobody who saw it lived to tell; ...`, and the delivery `WORD of X's death reaches Y (mother, Saint Denis) (witnessed | unwitnessed: body found, ... | unwitnessed: long delay): Shot near ...`. `KILLED` lines say how many were near. Not yet seen in game.
- Reviewed: whether anyone saw it is marked when he dies, with `WordOfMouth` on or off, and the rest goes by that mark. His kin in their own town walk up to you over it only once word of the death has reached them and knows it was you (with `WordOfMouth` off, after `KinWordDaysMin` days). Before, his mother could walk up a minute after the killing. Mourners at enRAGE's funeral for a man nobody saw you kill don't point you out. A sweetheart or spouse whose love was killed with nobody to see it grieves without blaming you (log `ROMANCE X grieves Y; nobody saw who killed him.`). If the kill's note was lost to a reload, the killing's deed decides. Word of a death that wasn't yours logs `(witnessed, not by you)` on delivery too.

### Reviewed the same day (the brawls, the braver town and the stalker in black)
- Brawls: a joiner who came to lead a side once its first man went down is let go when the fight ends (he fought on after the scene, off the fist hold). A man the game has only knocked senseless is a down like any other, and his fate is rolled (he was always killed, even at `BrawlFistsFatal` 0). The killing blow is always someone's: with nobody listed on the downed man, the man he went for, else the least beset man of the other side (the ledger had "by their own hand"), and his name is in the book. After a down, a man turns to a man before a woman, and a man of the side leads it before a woman (1899). The fair fight and the friend in it are counted for the last man down too. A wave joining balances against the sides with those who just got up from a seat.
- The brawl Testing row's refusal now counts everyone within 24 m by the street scenes' own rules, in their order (in combat, fleeing, in a conversation, in a scene, nodding to you, no identity, and so on), so the next refusal names what keeps people out. The 14 refusals in Saint Denis were not for want of names: anyone free is named on the spot.
- Braver town: everyone made braver is put back as the game has them when a story mission starts. Bravery 0 also leaves fighters' CA 77 and 78 alone. Aim-assist lock-on counts as aiming (hands up still work). Whoever the mod sends running (a pickpocket, a shooter, a horse thief, a scene's fright) has PCF 44 put back first, for two minutes. A failure stops the rest of that pass at once.
- Settings: an ini missing only a Testing row is written again without running the last version's default moves (`StalkerChance` 20 of your own would have turned 35 again).
- Stalker: with `StalkerBlack` on, the key and the roll say a stranger in black will turn up behind you, out of sight (they said someone near you).

### The stalker in black, and more often (`[Stalkers]`; on the Essentials page)
- Lex, 2026-10-31: "can we dress the stalker in an all black outfit? As well as try and have the stalker appear more often". With `StalkerBlack` (on), the stalker is always a stranger who turns up out of sight behind you, dressed the moment he exists in `StalkerBlackOutfit` of `StalkerBlackModel` (only the outfit preset is put on). Nobody near is turned and none of enRAGE's people, since enRAGE knows its people by their clothes. With it off, everything is as before (someone near, one of enRAGE's people, or a stranger). A revenant still comes back as he was. A model the game doesn't know falls back to the first candidate that it does, said once in the log.
- Which preset is truly black isn't known offline, so there is a new Testing row, "Stalker outfit: show the next candidate" (`TestStalkerOutfitKey`, None). Each press stands a man 4 m in front of you in the next preset of the next candidate model (every preset of one model, then the next: Saint Denis gentlemen, formal servers, riverboat dealers, upper-class men, gunslingers and others; no gang or story models), and takes the previous one away. The log (`STALKER OUTFIT candidate ...`) and the hint give the two ini lines to copy. He goes after 90 s.
- More often: `StalkerEveryMinutes` 15 (was 34) and `StalkerChance` 35 (was 20), about once in 45 minutes of play (it was about once in three hours). Both are on the Essentials page with `StalkerBlack`. An ini holding the old stock values moves to the new ones; values of your own are kept.
- F7 `TEST stalker:` and the startup line name the model and preset; `TEST records:` says a record can't stalk while StalkerBlack is on. Not yet seen in game: which preset is black.

### A braver town (`[World] Bravery`, 85; on the Essentials page)
- Lex, 2026-10-31: "we should make all NPCs much, much more brave". The fleeing seen in Saint Denis was the game's own panic (the mod's own "hurries off" never fired), so the game's own answer is changed, on the brave ones only. Every 2 s, inside the people ledger's own pass, each ambient grown-up within 60 m is asked once, by handle and model, so the odd coward still runs and a handle the game reuses is asked again. The brave get PCF 44 (cower instead of flee): where the game would send them running, they stay where they are. Their own value is read first and put back when they're no longer ours to change: taken by a mission or enRAGE, Bravery lowered, 30 s out of range, or the script stopping. Never a child, a story character, a lawman, another script's person, one of enRAGE's people or the mod's own. Whoever you aim at or lock on to gets the game's own answer back that frame, and keeps it 20 s, so hold-ups and robbery work as before. Nothing blocks events: people still gather to watch a fight and report a killing.
- Fistfights: the fighters get CA 77 (no threat broadcast, as the game's own street fight sets on its fighters) and CA 78 (no randoms fleeing), put back with the rest when the scene ends, so a fight draws a crowd instead of scattering it.
- The mod's own frights, scaled by the same setting: an onlooker hurries off only near the worst fright (it was any strong one), and walks off from someone else's trouble less often (stays to watch). At 85, one runs from a happening at most (it was four), and fewer freeze and then run. The rest back off or stand instead of running: a man backing off in a scene walks a few steps back, an unarmed man a gun is drawn on puts his hands up, the waking's frightened walk away, a wedding broken by a killing stands and stares, the night visitor's bystanders back off, and a scattered gathering walks off. Plot flight (the pickpocket, the horse thief, the stalker, a shooter fleeing his deed) is untouched. 0: everything as before.
- F7 `TEST bravery:` (braver near you now, made braver this session, put back, for your aim, handles reused); log `BRAVERY:` when everyone is put back. Not yet seen in game: whether PCF 44 stops witnesses running for the law, and how a brave robbery victim answers.

### Brawls that draw the street in, and fists that kill or knock out (settings version 161)
- More of a busy street joins: with `[Mind] MindBrawls` on, anyone near the fight may join, not only people the mind has already met. Up to six strangers each look are given an identity on the spot, the way the street scenes do it (never with `[World] OwnLooks` on). People join from 18 m (it was 12). On a street with ten or more watching, one more comes in at each look. The brawl's own pull on the street is stronger (up to 0.15, it was 0.07), and a lawman in sight holds back fewer (0.30 of the chance on the street, it was 0.15).
- The sides balance and the blows spread. Someone with no stake in it leans to the side that's short. A joiner goes for the man he hates unless two are already on him (three for a grudge of -2 or a hothead with no pity); otherwise he goes for whoever has the fewest on him, a man before a woman. A wave is placed one joiner at a time, each against the sides as the last one left them. (On 2026-10-31 every joiner went for one man, and he died.)
- A man going down no longer ends a big brawl. Once two or more have joined, a fighter beaten under 30% of his health, or found dead, is dropped from the sides. A joiner of his side takes the lead if he led it, whoever was on him turns to the least beset man left, and the brawl goes on until a side has nobody standing. Before, a knocked-out man who started it ended the whole scene.
- Killed or knocked out (`[Mind] BrawlFistsFatal`, 50). A man beaten down in a brawl is killed this percent of the time in a plain beating. It is more likely with several on him (+12 for each past the first, at most +25), a drunk or a hothead at him (+10 each), bad blood (+8, +15 for a grudge), or an old man down (+15). It is less likely with a lawman within 30 m (-20), a friend of his in it (-15), or a fair fight, one on one with the sides even (-10). It stays within 5-95%, is seeded by who is in it, and comes out about half and half across a town. Knocked out: down 20-40 s and unhurtable meanwhile, then up and on his way. Killed: booked as a death ("a beating") through the town's own death handling. Before, every fist down lay there for good while the ledger called it "out cold". One of enRAGE's people is only ever knocked out. If the game's own blow kills a man before the roll, that is booked as a death too.
- Guns a little more often (`[Mind] BrawlDrawShare`, 40): a losing man draws at 40% of the quarrel ladder's odds (it was a quarter), and two or more on him is now a reason too. In a brawl of four or more, a drawn gun takes only those two men out of it, and the rest fight on.
- The Testing row "A brawl grows and the law steps in" takes from the nearest 14 free people (it was 8). A refusal now counts what stood in the way: "fewer than two free people within 24 m (N free; everyone within 24 m: ...)", by fighting or fleeing, the game's own scripts' people, busy, and so on.
- Log `BRAWL down: X is knocked out cold by Y (...; down for N s)`, `BRAWL down: X is beaten to death by Y (...)`, `BRAWL comes to:`, `BRAWL draws: the brawl goes on around the two of them`, `SCENE fight: a man down; another of his side leads it now`. F7 `TEST brawl:` adds the downs, and `TEST join:` adds the onlookers given a name. Not yet seen in game.

### Lawmen the game no longer takes back

- A lawman sent to carry a body off the street, or to break up a brawl, was taken back by the game before he got there (14 of 15 bodies "never reached it"; the one lifted was set down 3 s later).
