He's now taken over for the job and handed back after it (another script's lawman is only directed, never let go of for the game to clear), sent by the nav mesh to where the body lies, and watched: stopped for 3 s, he's sent again, twice at most. The carry follows the game's own undertaker: the body is marked carriable and the carrier kept at it every frame, he walks it off, and sets it down with the game's own flags; never left holding it. The log says each step and, when it fails, what state he was in (`LAW clears: ...`); the brawl's lawman says how far off he was when he shouted, or that the fight was over before he got there (`LAW breaks up: ...`).

### Names stay up in a crowd

- In a busy street with a test running, every name over a head could vanish at once: the frame's 48 pieces of text were spent before the names were drawn (the bar, a thought, a test's long words, the subtitles), and the names left the rest to the Testing overlays. Now the nearest eight always get their name (the overlays take what's left), a name goes without its second line when text is short, and a test's words above the ticker are cut to two lines (the whole of them is in the log). When names are held back the log says what spent the frame's text, once a minute: `NAME cards held back: ...`; F7's name card line counts the frames.

### Brawls that grow, and the law that stops them (settings version 160)
- A fistfight can grow into a brawl a wave at a time: one joins at a look while it's a few, two or three once it's four or more, one more in a saloon. Friends and grudges pull people in, and the bigger it gets the less it takes, most of all in a saloon with drink in it; on the street most still watch. Up to `[Mind] BrawlMost` (12) fighting at once. Someone sat at the bar or leaning gets up the game's way before wading in. `[Mind] MindBrawls`, with MindLive and MindJoinFights; off, the old rule stands (three at most, nobody with the law near).
- The law at hand now holds most back rather than everyone: on the street a sixth as many join, in a saloon riot about a third.
- The law breaks it up (`[Mind] BrawlLaw`, with MindLive): a lawman within 35 m who can see a fistfight runs over and shouts to break it up. Each fighter stops or not by their mind (the law-abiding and the self-possessed stop, the angry, the drunk and the one winning less often); those who stop are let off with a word; whoever keeps swinging goes on, gets the lawman's fists and is hogtied, each in turn; with three or more holding out a second lawman comes to help. The usual "word with the winner" after a fight stands aside when the law broke it up. Those still swinging after the word are fair game.
- A brawl may come at you (`[Mind] BrawlPullsYouIn`): in a brawl of four or more within a few steps of you, someone who joined it may turn on you: most likely one you've hit, then one against you, then a drunk hothead; never someone afraid of you or a sober stranger with nothing against you. Whoever does is fair game.
- Rarely, a gun (`[Mind] BrawlDraws`, with LadderShots): an armed man losing a brawl who is drunk, hot-tempered, or proud with bad blood may draw, at a quarter of the quarrel ladder's own odds for the gun (about 1 in 20 of the drunk and armed when they start losing, rolled once a brawl), and may fire by the ladder's odds for the shot. The fists end; a lawman within 50 m comes with his gun. Whoever draws is fair game.
- Testing row "A brawl grows and the law steps in" (`TestBrawlTownKey`): starts a fistfight; for a minute and a half the crowd's joining skips its bar and roll (still a wave a look, still the sex rules), and a lawman anywhere within 70 m comes once five are fighting or 15 s have passed. Log `BRAWL joins`, `BRAWL comes at you`, `BRAWL draws`, `LAW breaks up`; F7 `TEST brawl:`.

### Thoughts that vary, and the waking the model finds
- With `[Mind] Language` on, each thought the model words now goes to one thing their mind is on: their work, someone in sight, a memory, the body, the worry, the place, money, family, faith, the town's talk, a small thing seen, what they'll do later, and only now and then the weather. It takes a shape of its own (fragments, one plain sentence, a question to themselves, a reckoning, a blunt opinion, a saying half-remembered, something looked forward to or dreaded, a grudge or a fond thought). The model is told their last few thoughts and to think of something else, and an answer that opens the same way or is too like one is held. The joined thought-and-choice asks a little more freely (0.85). Sampled against today's situations in game: 157 different openings in 180 thoughts (was 138), the weather in 10% of them (was 23%). `[Mind] LanguageVariety`, on (settings version 159).
- Nobody doubts the world is real without cause: below the second stage of waking, and with nothing strange lived through (a buried man seen walking, the night visitor, a death they woke from), a thought that wonders whether the world is real, a trick or painted on is held and the written line shows. The need that read as "searching for a reason" is put to the model as wanting their days to count for something. "Real" as a plain word ("a real good cup of coffee") still passes.
- The waking found by the model: someone with cause who wonders at the world in a thought of their own is shaken a little more by it ("things are as they seem" chipped 0.04 a thought, at most 0.5), and when that tips the belief they begin to stir, as from any doubt. Log detail `MIND doubt voiced`. `[Mind] ModelWakes`, on.
- Fewer stirring, a little: `AwakeningShare` 3 as built is now 2.25, and an ini at 0.6 goes to 0.45 (a value of your own is kept).
- A joined pick whose thought came without its "Thought:" label still reads its choice (3 in 90 in the sampling).
- Subtitles between other people show only for speakers on screen (the voice under the floor still heard); the default range stays, Lex's ini set to 6 m.

### Remembered after enRAGE takes them up

- Fixed: when enRAGE took up one of RealNPCs' townspeople as its own (a greeting does it), what they knew of you stayed behind: tell someone your name in Talk, greet them, and the next Talk offered the name again. Now your name, your talks, how they feel about you, what they've seen of you and their memories go with them. Their worry is still their own after the change.

### One thing at a time for each person (a review across tonight's features)

- Fixed: the street's chance meetings and quiet talks could take someone already busy with you or with another part of the mod: the person you were talking to in Talk (the list vanished as they turned to chat with a neighbour), a lonely regular waiting for a word, someone come to thank you, or a lawman at work. They take nobody another part has now.
- Fixed: only one person comes up to you at a time, for every kind of walk-up: someone come to thank you, a lonely regular, a friend of a man you wronged, a relative, a pickpocket. Before, a grudge could be said over a Talk conversation, or a relative and a lonely regular arrive together; and a pickpocket no longer starts during a scene, in a hushed town or under a lawman's eye.
- Fixed: a lawman carrying a body off, stepping in on a lash-out or chasing a thief is no longer picked for another job or sent strolling by another part, and nobody is sent strolling out of a fight.
- Fixed: someone the mind had sent on an errand stops it, with no new orders, once Talk, a walk-up or a scene has them; a model's answer that comes back for someone taken up since is dropped (no thought over their talk with you); a mind's own scene needs both people free.
- Fixed: right after you say goodbye in Talk, or someone thanks you or has it out with you, they don't also tip their hat and greet you as you pass. Greeting someone you're in Talk with no longer starts a second conversation over it. A friend's grudge in a hushed town, or with a lawman looking on, stays words.
- Smoother frames: the Talk prompt's check, a thanks walk and a lawman carrying a body off now step four times a second rather than every frame, the body search tests the distance first and rests after a refusal, the mind's own scene options are worked out every 10 s rather than 3, and the hint above the ticker is laid out once rather than every frame.

### The bully turns on whoever stepped in (the run's buffer)

- When someone steps in on a bully and he won't back down, he may now turn on the one who stepped in instead: the man comes up to him, gets a hard shove with a word on it ("You want some too, Cal? Here."), and the bully says his piece to the street ("Anybody else want to play the hero?") and goes; the one he had hold of goes to see the man is all right ("Lord, Cal, are you hurt? That was meant for me."), who answers as his nature is: makes light of it, or calls after him ("That's right. Keep walking."). A hothead more than half the time, the proud now and then, a coward hardly ever; drink makes it likelier, and a man stepping in who looks like he means it makes it less likely; never at a woman who stepped in. The one shoved remembers facing him down (once the shove has landed), and for a moment after a shove the shover is fair game: stopping him is no crime. Part of `[Scenes] Kindnesses`; the stand-up Testing row now takes four turns (the fight goes on, he backs off, they take the other away, he turns on them). Before, he only told them to keep out and went on. Also fixed: the one picked on called a friend who stepped in "mister".

### Being watched (the psychologist's list: people behave better when they're seen)

- When the street's own timing would start trouble for a reason between two people (a pickpocket, a theft, a shove, a fistfight, a standoff, the noon callout) and a lawman within 25 m is looking their way with a clear sight of the one who'd start it, it waits: they glance at the law and think why ("Law's watching. Another day, then.", "Lawman right there. Hands in your own pockets."), and the reason is still theirs once he's gone. A thief always waits, even one the mind chose itself; a hothead or a drunk goes ahead about four times in ten (once a day, not again every minute). On a Sunday morning by the church the same ("Sunday. It can wait till tomorrow."), but the godless don't mind it. A chance meeting that would have come to trouble with the law looking keeps to words. Any other scene a mind picks for itself still decides, and the quarrel ladder keeps its own rule for the law. `[Scenes] BeingWatched` (on; settings version 158). F7 `TEST watched:` counts what waited; log detail `WATCHED`.

### Why a scene starts, as a thought (the game developer's "legible reasons")

- When a street scene starts for a reason between two people, the one whose reason it is thinks why over their head as they go over, so you can follow what led to it: "That money. Hiram hears about it now.", "Ruth lost someone. Somebody ought to say a word.", "Got to tell Abe. Can't keep it in." Someone they know they think of by name; a stranger as "that fellow" or "the lady", and only for what a stranger can be part of (a snap, drink, a coin asked for, news, a flirt). Only when you can see them; a scene the model chose has the model's own thought instead. `[Scenes] ReasonThoughts` (on; settings version 155). F7 `TEST reason thoughts:` counts them.
- Fixed: when the model chose for someone near, a condolence was offered to the grieving one ("goes to offer Ruth sympathy", with Ruth the friend), keeping clear to the one who did the wrong, a debt still owed to the one who owes it (it's the one owed who calls it in), and half of all proposals to the woman (the man kneels); each now goes to the one whose choice it is.

### Personal space (the psychologist's list)

- Walk up to someone and stand right up against them (under a metre; in a saloon, only face to face: shoulder to shoulder at the bar is nothing) for a couple of seconds, both of you still, and they mind it as their nature and their feeling for you say: the plain ask for a little room and step back ("You mind? You're right on top of me."), then turn to keep an eye on you and go on their way; the timid, anyone afraid of you, and the sore but timid step back with a glance; the proud tell you to mind yourself; the hot-tempered and anyone against you stand their ground and tell you to back off (a woman says she'll call the marshal); the cold only look, the greedy mind their purse; a friend makes light of it (a woman, that folks will talk). Spoken lines come with a subtitle and a recorded voice; the thought shows over them. Not someone who walked up to you, not while you're locked on to someone (Talk, or the game's Greet), sitting, riding, aiming or fighting; never the law, anyone sitting or at the bar, a working girl, anyone busy; once a person in five minutes. `[World] PersonalSpace` (on, with `[Scenes] SightReactions`; settings version 154). Testing row "Someone minds you standing so close"; F7 `TEST personal space:`; log `SPACE`.

### Thanks, later (what players love: kindness remembered and repaid)

- **Lonely regulars.** Someone the mind finds lonely who knows you (your name, or a kindness of yours lately) may come over when they see you in town, hoping for a word ("Mr. Morgan! Hoped I'd run into you. Got a minute for a fellow?"), and wait a moment facing you. Stop and Talk with them and they're glad of it; walk on and it stings, and they think so (nothing is held against you). They come only when you're on foot and have stopped, and let it be if you ride or walk on before they reach you; greeting them counts as stopping. Never someone afraid of you or against you, sore at you, wronged by you lately (and not apologised to) or mourning a friend you killed, nor the cold; a woman doesn't walk up to you after dark or in a saloon; the proud never own to being lonely ("A word, if you've a minute. Nothing pressing."); walk past someone and they leave you be for three days. `[Scenes] LonelyRegulars` (on, with `[Mind] MindLive`; settings version 157). Testing row "A lonely regular comes over"; F7 `TEST lonely:`; log `LONELY`.
- Someone you stood a drink, gave money or whose debt you settled through Talk may come over when they next see you in town, some hours or a few days later, and thank you for that very thing in their own words and their own voice ("What you gave me the other day. Bought bread with it, and then some. Thank you."), then go on their way. Not everyone: a drink stood is answered with a drink some day, not a speech, so few come for one ("Next one's on me, mister."); about a third come for a dollar, most for five, nearly all for a debt. They thank you for the biggest thing you did them, and that covers the rest; a warning, a slight, a blow or a theft from you since cancels what came before. The proud who took your eleven dollars as a loan, and said so, pay it back, three days on at the soonest. The cold, anyone sitting, and a woman after dark or indoors give you a nod and keep it to themselves; the greedy don't come; nobody who fears or hates you now. Once for each kindness; never while you're wanted, fighting, aiming, in Talk, or someone else is coming up to you. `[Scenes] LateThanks` (on; settings version 153). Testing row "Someone you helped thanks you" (at once, nothing paid, nothing marked); F7 `TEST thanks:`; log `THANKS`.

### Tempers carry, and honour rules in Lemoyne (the psychologist's lens)

- **Stress that builds.** One trouble is borne; two or more at once (a worry at its worst or gone wrong today, a slight or a beating not answered, a friend's death, a face in the bar or a purse gone; a beating and the shame of it count once) and someone may snap at whoever is there and won't hit back, the harder the more there is: at a timid one whenever it builds, at anyone else now and then. The anger picks a safe target: never someone well-off, and a man doesn't take it out on a strange woman; the timid and the loyal keep it in. The log says what was weighing on them ("Silas has too much at once (grieving Ruth, robbed) and snaps at Ezra"), and the one who snaps thinks it as they go over; what they snap is their worry's own words at its worst, else a plain "Leave me be. I've had a day of it." Part of `[Mind] MindTempers`.
- **Displaced aggression:** someone slighted, spoken to hard or beaten in the last four game hours (their own memories) may take it out on someone near who won't hit back, a timid man above all: one trouble leads to the next ("Hiram was beaten earlier, and takes it out on Amos"). The one who shamed him isn't the one who pays.
- **The culture of honour** (Nisbett and Cohen's Southern "culture of honor"): in Lemoyne's towns and country an insult must be answered, so the quarrel ladder climbs a little faster there (more over nothing, a slight or a woman) and backing down costs more before a crowd. Offline: 74 of 400 quarrels reach blows where honour rules, against 61 elsewhere.
- `[Mind] MindTempers` (on, with MindLive; settings version 152).

### Lawmen clear the dead from the street (roadmap 33; Lex, 2026-10-31)

- A body left lying in a town street for 20 s or more, with no fight or pursuit near and nobody carrying it: the nearest lawman on foot within 50 m walks over, lifts it with the game's own carry, takes it about 20 m off the street (away from you), sets it down and goes back to his rounds; up to three people near turn to watch him. One at a time, each body once, a minute between; never the story's own or another script's. A lawman lifting a body is new to the game's people, so every step is logged (`LAW clears:`), and if the game won't let him lift it the log says so. `[World] LawClearsBodies` (settings version 150). Testing row "A lawman clears a body"; F7 `TEST law clears bodies:`.

### Arthur's own fighting words (roadmap 34)

- Arthur now says one of his own recorded fighting lines (the ones the game rarely plays, which the Cut Dialogue Restoration and Enhancement mod is built on: his taunts at one man, near or far) when someone comes at him, the first time the self-defence watch sees each, and when he catches the pickpocket himself. Nothing is taken from that mod: they're the game's own recordings, played by name. Never over a line already playing on him (that mod's, the game's or RealNPCs'), and 20 s between. `[World] ArthurSpeaks` (settings version 151). Testing row "Arthur's own line"; F7 `TEST arthur:`; log `ARTHUR says`.

### Talk to people: the interaction menu (roadmap 6; Lex chose a new lock-on prompt)

- Locked on to an adult RealNPCs knows, the lock-on menu now also offers **Talk** (Q, or a pad's RB). It opens a short list above the ticker: "What's the news?", "Let me stand you a drink." (in a saloon), "Here, take this" ($1, or $5 once they've told you they're worried about money), "Watch yourself.", "Goodbye." Up and down (the D-pad, or the mouse wheel) to choose, Space or Enter (A) to say it, Backspace (B) to leave (a small hint under the list says so); the list grows to nine with your name, their worry to ask after, a debt to settle and an apology (below). They turn to face you; Arthur's words show, then their answer in their own voice, from who they are and how they already stand with you: someone fond of you, or someone you've been kind to that day (a drink, money), tells you their own worry in their words ("My little one's had a fever three days now."); a poster or a secret only to someone fond of you; anyone else tells you what the town says about you. Someone afraid of you placates you; someone who hates you won't drink with you or take your money and tells you nothing. The proud won't take your money (and take the offer as a slight), the devout won't take a drink, the coward steps back from a warning and the hothead squares up. What it was is written down for their mind (a round or charity as kindness, a warning as a challenge to them: words, not a gunfight), so their gratitude or fear, how they greet you after, what witnesses make of it and what they remember all follow from what exists; enRAGE's own townsfolk also warm to you through its own method. The same thing done to the same person again within the game hour is said but not counted again. Money comes from your pocket when they answer. Nobody busy with anything else (a scene, a talk, another script, enRAGE's own business) is offered it. It closes when you walk off, draw, fight, a mission or a cutscene starts, a scene takes them or the two of you are 5 m apart. `[World] InteractionMenu` (settings version 148). Testing row "Talk to the nearest person you know"; F7 `TEST talk:`.
- **Their answers in their own words** (with `[Mind] Language` on; Lex: the model words more of what people say). What someone answers you in Talk is now said in the local model's words, by their nature and mood ("I ain't one to take charity from nobody. I can earn my own wages.", "My boy's been sick with fever for three days now."); what it means stays the menu's (the drink taken, the money refused, the debt paid), and the written line stands whenever the model is slow (2.8 s from your words) or says something it shouldn't (a modern word, a name it wasn't given, a refusal where the answer accepts, a loan not called one). A drink or money turned down, and a man giving his own name back, keep their written words; the voice that plays is chosen by what the answer means, not by the model's wording; and Talk's answers go ahead of the model's other work. A name the model makes up for you is taken out; yours stays once you've told it. Sampled offline with llama3.1:8b on 20 people and six things said, twice over: 105 of 120 passed the checks, about 170 ms an answer. `[Mind] LanguageTalk` (on; settings version 156). F7 `TEST talk:` counts how many were worded, held and late; log `TALK model`.
- **Ask after their trouble.** Once someone has told you their worry, the list offers to ask after it ("How's the little one?", "How's that debt of yours?", "Any rain out your way?"; never a secret or a poster): they tell you how it stands today in their own words, a few words first by their nature ("If you must know.", "Oh! Well..."), and not the very thought you just saw over their head; once it's over you can still ask how it turned out. Being asked is a kindness to them, once a day. What someone told you holds for that trouble: when a new one comes, they tell you afresh.
- **Say you're sorry.** After a warning, a slight or a blow of yours in the last three days, the list offers "I'm sorry about before." (within the hour, "I didn't mean that."): most take it ("Kind of you to say so. It's forgotten."), the proud this once, the cold say it costs you nothing; a blow or a theft is taken but not forgotten ("I'll take it. I'll not forget it, mind."); someone afraid of you says it's all forgotten and doesn't mean it (it moves them little), someone who hates you says sorry doesn't mend it. Written down for their mind as an apology, once a day. The list now runs to nine.
- **Tell them your name.** While someone doesn't know it, the list offers "The name's Arthur Morgan." From then on they know it, as if they'd asked (their letters, thoughts and words about you use it instead of "that stranger"). They answer as manners had it: "Mr. Morgan" from most, "Morgan" from a man among men; the friendly man gives his own name back ("Hiram Cobb. Pleased to know you, Morgan."), the proud with a flourish, a woman gives a strange man no first name; the cold just say it back, someone afraid of you stammers it, someone who hates you says they know well enough who you are. Someone grateful, sore or shamed who knows your name thinks of you by it as you pass, not as "the stranger" (their spoken greeting stays their voice's own words).
- **Settle a debt** (roadmap 9, the first worry you can change). Once someone has told you of their debt, the list also offers "Let me settle that debt ($11)". From that day their worry has ended well: their thoughts turn to the man who paid ("A man I hardly know put eleven dollars in my hand. Paid Pruitt before I could think better of it."), the one they owed stops calling for it, their money need eases, and it's written down for their mind as help. The proud take it as a loan; someone who hates you won't be in your debt. The day you did it is kept in the save (and, for the proud, that they called it a loan); load a save from before it and it never happened. Telling someone your name is kept too, as it always was when they asked it. Log `WORRY settled by you`.
- **Mend other troubles with money** (roadmap 9: the next worries you can change). Once someone has told you their trouble, the list offers what money can do for it: "Here, for the doctor ($2)" for a fever at home (the doctor wants two dollars to ride out), "Here, see a proper dentist ($3)" for a bad tooth (better than the barber's pliers), "Here, toward that ticket ($5)" for the fare they're saving. As with the debt, the worry has ended well: that day they're still waiting on the doctor or the dentist ("Doctor's coming. Somebody paid him. Lord, let it be in time."), and from the next their thoughts turn to how it came out and the man who paid ("Doctor's paid, and not with my money. Ruth sat up this morning and asked for broth."); asked after it, they tell you to your face ("The doctor's on his way out to us, on your two dollars."); a fever can be helped only before its crisis (by then the doctor has come and it's in God's hands); for the fare, the five dollars toward it takes the gift's place; each has its own answers ("A real dentist? Not the barber's pliers? Mister, you've saved my jaw."), the proud take it to save face (no loan for two dollars), someone who hates you won't take it, except for the sick at home ("For their sake, not yours. ...Give it here."), and days later they may thank you for it ("Eating on both sides again, mister. That was your three dollars."). Kept in the save as a settled debt is (`Store.WorrySettled`; loading a save from between two mends for one person keeps the earlier); nothing new is saved.

### A town on edge (the psychologist's lens: fear spreads beyond those who saw it, and fades)

- **Onlookers hurry off from real danger.** Someone badly frightened by what they saw (a killing of yours, guns drawn) no longer strolls away: a glance at it and off at a run, twenty metres away from it, then on their way; someone rooted to the spot by a killing runs once they can move again. A milder fright still walks off, a friend of one in it stays near, and fear kept off the face still slips away quietly. And fright spreads: when one runs, the others near who were frightened at all and showed it go with them (up to four from one happening), never a friend of one in it. Part of `[Mind] MindOnlookers`.
- **The street hushes after something big** (the game developer's "drama director"). For about six hours after a killing (while the town's fear is at half or more; Saint Denis, a city, takes more: the first two hours or so after one killing), the street's own timing holds back its fights, shoves, drunken trouble, thefts and noon callouts, chance meetings included, and leaves longer between scenes (half as long again); condolences, gossip, words and small talk go on, and a quarrel stays words (the ladder takes none to blows). A scene a mind picks for itself still decides. Part of `[Mind] MindTownMood`. F7 `TEST town mood:` says when the street is hushed and how many scenes it held back.
- A killing, a robbery or a mugging leaves the whole town on edge for about a day, worst in the first hours (0.95 at once, about half after six hours, gone in a day): everyone's safety need there rises, more at night, so people out go home, keep clear and keep watch, and the model is told ("The town: badly frightened since a killing just now"), so their thoughts say why. Two killings frighten more than one, not twice as much. And the same trouble seen again in a town that week moves onlookers less each time (the third brawl draws a look, not a gasp); the one it happens to feels it in full. Worked out from the happenings already kept: nothing new is saved. `[Mind] MindTownMood` (on, with MindLive; settings version 149). F7 `TEST town mood:`.

### The model reaches further, and says how it keeps up (step 3 of the model plan; Lex: "control as many NPCs as possible")

- The model now picks for people up to 25 m away (15 before) and for up to 8 at once (4 before), both settings: `[Mind] PickMetres` and `PicksAtOnce` (settings version 147). Past 15 m each person's choices go to the model half as often, the rest are the mind's own. The model's own queue holds up to 16 (6 before).
- F7 `TEST model:` gives the numbers to raise them by: picks asked (everyday and waking), the late and unreadable shares, answer times (typical, 9 in 10, the slowest, over the last 128), how many wait, how many the queue turned away. Once a minute while picks are asked the log has a `MODEL minute:` line. The mind itself still looks 25 m out, so a `PickMetres` beyond 25 changes nothing yet.

### People start the street's scenes from their own thoughts (step 2 of the model plan; Lex, 2026-10-31)

- When the model picks what someone near does, their own reasons with someone else near are now among the choices, in their words: "goes to have words with Hiram over money", "goes to offer Ruth sympathy", "goes to see Abe about the money between them", "goes to make it up with Clara". Picking one starts that scene through the very path the street's own timing uses (the cast, the quarrel ladder, the walk-over, someone stepping in, a bystander), so nothing is invented: only who chooses changes. Only the one who would start it is offered it; only while the street can take a scene; the same two rest after, as before. Sampled offline: a cooper sore at Hiram over eleven dollars went to have words with him 9 to 11 times in 12, wherever it stood in the list. `[Mind] MindStartsScenes` (on, with LanguagePicks; settings version 146). Testing row "A mind starts a scene (their own reason)". Log `MIND starts:`; F7 `TEST picks:` counts them.

### The model chooses what the waking do (Lex, 2026-10-31: "model decides more"; lash-outs: "model decides freely")

- With `[Mind] Language`, `LanguagePicks` and `MindLive` on, each act of a hold asks the local model, 4 s before it ends, what this person does next, with who they are, how they feel and what's on their mind, and the waking brief for their stage. The list: stand dazed, drift, pace or freeze, look round; from stage 3, with someone near, two of the strange acts at that person by name, preaching, going for them, or going for you (a gun at stage 4 if they carry one); at stage 4 the strip if their roll allows. Whatever it picks is done, lash-outs included, with nothing rolled; the person it named is the one bothered or attacked. No answer by the act's end, or one that names none: the rolls as before. `WakeLashOut` 0 still takes lash-outs off the list. The list is shuffled for every ask: in a fixed order the model gave the same answer 20 times in 20. Asked 20 times each offline (llama3.1:8b, shuffled): a timid woman at stage 3 never turned violent (she waved a hand before a face, stared, stood dazed); a plain man at stage 4 never did; an angry, armed hothead at stage 4 drew a gun 17 times in 20, about half at you. Log `WAKE pick`; F7 `TEST wake hold:` counts the picks. In game (Lex, 2026-10-31): 11 asks in three minutes, 8 answered in 73-166 ms, all quiet choices (an unravelling man offered his fists at you twice drifted off instead). Since: an act that ends early waits up to 1.5 s for an answer asked just before (two were asked 0.01 s before their end), and someone sitting may get up, the game's own way (a seated man had only "sit" and "look round" and looked round four times running).

- **What people think and what they do come from one answer** (Lex, 2026-10-31: "having their inner thoughts reflect the model's inner thoughts for that NPC better"). When the model picks what someone does (the everyday choices and the waking's), it now writes the thought in their head as they decide in the same answer, two lines: `Thought:` then `Choice:`. The thought shows over them as they act, after the same check as every model thought; one that fails it is held back, and the choice stands. Sampled offline (llama3.1:8b): a cooper sore at Hiram, "can't just stand here fumin' like a fool, I got to do somethin' about that Hiram", keeps a wary eye out; a waking hothead, "must get that fake man", goes for the stranger. About 200-400 ms an answer. F7 `TEST picks:` counts the thoughts shown and held. Log `MIND pick thought`.
- **One name on the prompt and over the head** (Lex, 2026-10-31, a screenshot: "Jacob Shelby" over a man's head, "Austin Colby" on the lock-on prompt and in his greeting). When enRAGE takes up one of our people under a name of its own, RealNPCs renamed enRAGE's record back, but enRAGE labels the prompt from a second list of the people it has named, every scan, so its name came back. That entry is renamed too now, and the prompt relabelled through enRAGE's own method. The log's NAME line says "(the prompt too)".
- **The law steps in on a lash-out** (Lex, 2026-10-31: "getting beat up by Hank Crabtree ... in front of a lawman but he is not getting attacked by the law"). The game's lawmen leave one townsman beating another alone. Now a lawman within 30 m who can see someone waking turn on you or anyone else shouts at them ("Break it up! I said break it up!") and goes for them, fists for fists, his gun if they carry one. When it's over and they're alive, he hogties them, the game's own way, and goes back to his rounds. Under `[Scenes] LawChases`. Log `WAKE law`; F7 `TEST wake hold:` counts it.
- **Carrying a body you didn't kill is no crime, for good** (Lex, 2026-10-31: picked up a man who had ended his own life at the fourth stage, wanted for murder). The clear worked but the game charged again every half second while he carried the body. Now the game's own "doesn't count toward wanted" flag is set on someone as their despair begins, and on any body you didn't kill when you pick it up (the log says whether the game took it on the dead). The repeated LAW line is written once and every 20th time after.
- The strange-acts row's words stay on screen until the act they describe ends (up to a minute).
- Every Testing row says what it's doing above the ticker when it starts (Lex, 2026-10-31), and the strange-acts row in detail: which turn of nine, who, what they do and to whom, what to watch for, and what the next press does. Long words wrap over several lines.
- The hang watch (2026-10-31: three game hangs in Saint Denis): if any part of RealNPCs holds the game thread for 2 s, a background thread writes `HANG watch: ... inside <part> > <step>` to the log, again at 8 s and 30 s. The sights steps are marked one by one.

## 0.7.10 (2026-10-31)

A fix: the game froze in busy towns.

- **The game no longer freezes in Saint Denis** (Lex, 2026-10-31: three freezes in Saint Denis, Windows calling each a hang). A watch added to find it caught the game thread stuck in RealNPCs' reading of what Arthur wore (`sights > sample > clothes (the metaped pieces)`, for 2, 8 and 30 s), read every 3 s since 0.7.4 so that people could react to him naked or undressed. It no longer reads clothes at all (nor does `[World] OwnLooks`, which used the same calls): nobody remarks on Arthur undressed for now. Nothing else changes from 0.7.9.

## 0.7.9 (2026-10-31)

A fix: stripping froze the game.

- **Stripping is held back** (Lex, 2026-10-31, in game: the game froze two seconds after a fully awake man was made to strip from the Testing page; the log stops as the clothes came off). Nothing strips now, whatever `WakeStripChance` says, and the Testing row passes over it, until the cause is found and it's seen working. Nothing else changes from 0.7.8.

## 0.7.8 (2026-10-31)

The waking stay strange: from the second stage they no longer slip back into their day between acts, they use their hands and bodies, they bother the people around them, and the worst of them turn on you or on others. Lex tested the hold, the strange acts and a lash-out at him in game; stripping hasn't been seen in game yet.

### The waking stay strange, and get stranger (Lex, 2026-10-31, in game: "they don't do those animations, and it seems they swap back to the game controlling them")

- **Held between acts** (`[WestWorld] WakeHold`, `WakeHoldMinutes` 3): from the second stage, an act that finishes runs straight into the next instead of handing them back to their day: stood dazed (looking at the sky, their hands or nothing, a mutter under the breath), drifting a few slow steps and stopping, pacing, freezing. After the minutes they're let go for a minute. They're let go at once for a fight, a gun drawn or fired, the law after you or a lawman's work, a street scene, or you more than 30 m off. The log says `WAKE hold` when it starts and `WAKE hold ended` with how long and why.
- **Gestures** (`WakeGestures`): the stage clips the Testing page's "Wake stage gestures" row plays are now used in the acts: rubbing the eyes, a hand to the face, pointing at the sky, "is this it", a hand to the head, the pleading hands, and the madman's and the lost man's whole-body clips while they stand. A clip that won't play twice, or carries them more than 1.2 m, is dropped for the session (`WAKE gesture dropped` in the log).
- **Strange with others** (`WakeStrange`): at stages 3 and 4, now and then, with someone near: right up close and staring, a hand waved before the face, following step for step behind them, telling them they aren't real, or preaching to whoever is about. The other answers by their temper: walks off, curses, laughs, or takes fright and hurries away, in their own voice, with a thought of their own. Log `WAKE strange`.
- **Stripping bare** (`WakeStripChance` 5): a few of the fully awake, once each, take every stitch off where they stand and carry on. Never enRAGE's people (it knows them by their clothes). Up to three who see it react as to a naked man, with thoughts of their own about it. Log `WAKE strip` says what came off and whether they ended naked on that model. The game dresses them again when it next makes them.
- **Turning on someone** (`WakeLashOut` 25; Lex, 2026-10-31, after a stage-4 man drew on him: "fantastic ... it should include Arthur as well as other NPCs for them to unleash their panic/rage on"): a quarter of holds at stages 3 and 4 end with them going for you or for someone near, half and half: fists while unravelling, their own gun when awake and carrying one. Hotheads and the proud more often, cowards less. Shouted words first ("You! It's you doing this to us!"), then the game's own fighting. Someone they go for other than you is defended as from a bully: fair game to stop, with the red dot. Log `WAKE lash out`. Settings version 145.
- **A waking person greeted keeps their stage** (Lex, 2026-10-31, in game: awake, greeted, and the stages were gone; Y began them again at 1). With enRAGE installed, a greeting can make one of our residents one of enRAGE's townsfolk, and from then on they were looked up by enRAGE's record, which had no waking. Their stage, the despair roll and the strip now go with them (`WAKE carried` in the log). And an upper-body gesture is no longer dropped because the person walked on during a look round.
- **What the waking say shows as subtitles** (Lex, 2026-10-31: "I didn't hear the voicelines or see the subtitles" for the wave's "Can you see my hand?"). Their shouts, questions, pleas and the strange acts' words went through the queue that only subtitles lines said to you, so none showed. Now the voice plays at once and the words show: the top box for what's said to you (the pleas, a lash-out at you), the box under it for the rest, and the dazed mutters "under her breath". Each line is logged as `WAKE said`, with whether the voice played.
- The quiet scenes beside a street scene, and the chance meetings, no longer take someone in the middle of a waking act (2026-10-31: a quarrel beside the scene took a woman mid-act twice, ending it at once).
- Testing page: "Strange waking acts, the next in turn" on the nearest person unravelling or awake (close up, wave, follow, not real, preach, lash out at you, lash out at another, strip, dazed), then held. F7: `TEST wake hold:`. Settings version 144.

## 0.7.7 (2026-10-31)

The settings panel works on other builds of Script Hook RDR2 .NET V2. Lex checked the panel in game.

### The settings panel opens on other Script Hook .NET builds (a player, 2026-10-30: Alt+R did nothing)

- The player's log showed the panel failing on its first frame ("Could not load type 'RDR2.Native._NAMESPACE30'") and switching itself off, and the bell and waking scenes failing on the look-at call ("Method not found: TASK_LOOK_AT_COORD"). Their Script Hook RDR2 .NET V2 gives the same version number as ours but names those two game functions differently. RealNPCs now calls both by the game's own hash, which doesn't depend on how a build names it.

## 0.7.6 (2026-10-30)

Killing a pickpocket who robbed you is self-defence again, as it was meant to be: no murder charge. Lex tested it in game (a pickpocket in Annesburg, shot after he took $25: no charge, no bounty, the money back).

### A thief shot while running off isn't murder (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game: "I just killed a pickpocket and got wanted for murder")

- In Annesburg a pickpocket took $25 and ran; Arthur shot him. RealNPCs saw it as self-defence and cleared the bounty and the wanted level, but the game's pursuit stayed on ("still wanted (level 0, the law after you)") and the charge was murder. Now the clear wipes the game's record of the crime too (what you owed before is kept), and if the law is still after you on the next check, the law is switched off for a moment (a second and a half, once a fight) to end the pursuit. The log says LAW after the clear, and LAW after the moment off, whether it held.

## 0.7.5 (2026-10-30)

A fix for the settings key (Alt+R), which two players found stopped working a while into the game.

### Alt+R keeps working (two players, 2026-10-30: the settings key stopped working a while into the game)

- Each part of RealNPCs is switched off if it keeps failing, so one fault can't take the rest down. The count of its errors ran all session, so a rare error every so often added up, and after fifteen of them the settings panel was off until the game restarted: the key did nothing. Now a part is switched off only for fifteen errors with no two quiet minutes between them, and the settings key always brings the panel back (the log says so).
- The settings keys work with Shift or Ctrl held too: sprinting, crouching, or a key Windows still thinks is down after Alt+Tab made Alt+R read as Alt+Shift+R and do nothing. Not when that combination is another of RealNPCs' keys. The first time, the log says so.

## 0.7.4 (2026-10-30)

Lex's evening in game after 0.7.3, and the rest of the NPC-life run: what follows a fight, crowds, onlookers who join your own fistfights, quarrels that may end in shots, rival gangs, the night's muggings and drunks carried home, horse thieves, plans over days that shape what people do, people going to the real saloon, store and church, and clothes people notice; the law's fixes (self-defence after the fight, another's trampling, carrying a body you didn't kill); names that keep (lawmen and keepers marked, a greeted stranger's name, names inside big buildings); shouts that carry, two subtitle boxes, the bar's scenes in turn with T; and, with a local language model, thoughts worded, picks of what people near you do, and thoughts said aloud in their own voice. Much of it is new and not yet seen at length in game; each part has its own switch.

### Thoughts said aloud in their own voice (Lex, 2026-10-30; in testing)

- As a thought shows over someone within 12 m whose voice the add-on knows, the local model is shown the thought and the remarks that person's own voice has recorded (by their words where your local word list has them, else by what each is for: grumbling about the weather, a curse under the breath, drunken talk, things going badly...) and picks the one that fits. They say it aloud in their real voice, with its words in the lower subtitle box when they're known. Most thoughts stay unspoken: half are never offered, and the model is asked twice, the line said only when both answers agree (in a trial before the game, a sure fit came back alike each time, an unsure one wandered). One every 20 s at most, each person once in four minutes, never someone speaking or in a scene. The log's MIND aloud line shows the thought, what was offered and what was said; F7 TEST aloud counts them. `[Mind] ThoughtsAloud`, on, with `Language` (settings version 143).
- The more voices transcribed, the better it fits: export voice banks with OpenIV (PEDS_00 to PEDS_10.rpf, one folder a bank into C:\mods\voice-test\banks) and transcribe them there; the words then reach the game through voices\words.txt.

### A working girl's own come-on, wherever she flirts (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game)

- Carrie, a working girl in Valentine's saloon, said her own come-on to Arthur, the game's PLAYER_FOLLOWING recording in her voice. Now, wherever one of the working girls is about to say something flirty (her yes when Arthur flirts, her yes to somewhere quieter, a come-on to a man in a street scene, a sweetheart's hello), that line goes first, now and then: once in ten minutes a woman and once in two minutes in all, so it stays a treat. Only the working girls' voices: in anyone else's the same line is a cold "what you following me for?". In the log, VOICE a working girl's come-on.
- The model's thought over someone's head was logged (and counted on F7) every time it was drawn, twice a second; now once a line.

### Two subtitle boxes: to Arthur, and between others (Lex, 2026-10-30, a screenshot)

- A woman's gossip to another sat in the subtitle box where a line to Arthur goes. Now the game's place is for what's said to Arthur alone; what people say to each other (street scenes, quiet talks, hellos in passing, the doctor's patient, the law chase, the gangs) shows in a smaller, softer box just under it, above the bar, and the two show at once without either replacing the other. A thank-you after you step in and a sorry from someone who walks into you are said to you, so they take the top box.
- No thought in a subtitle: the one that was (a rescuer's, after talking someone down from the edge) goes over their head now. The Testing row's subtitle preview shows both boxes. Roadmap 30 now includes every line heard rather than read, inner thoughts too, in the speaker's own voice.

### The model picks what people near you do (Lex, 2026-10-30; in testing)

- The first step toward the model deciding. When the mind's choice changes for someone within 15 m of you, the best three of the things it would allow them (and a plan's own step, "goes to find Gideon, meaning to get even with him", when the one it's about is near) go to the local model with who they are and how they feel, and it answers with a number. That's what they do. Every option is one the mind already allows, so any pick is safe; a feeling strongly felt still decides by itself. The wait is 2.5 s at most: late, empty or unreadable, the mind's own choice is done, as before. Picks go ahead of thoughts in the model's queue, four at once at most. Measured on llama3.1:8b before the game: 30 to 140 ms an answer, a clean number each time.
- The log's MIND pick line shows what was offered, what the model picked and whether it was the mind's own (the first 40 a session in the main log); F7 TEST picks counts them. `[Mind] LanguagePicks`, on, with `Language` (settings version 142).

### Plans lean the day; someone enRAGE knew goes by its name (Lex, 2026-10-30)

- A plan carried over the days now shapes what its holder does with the day, not only whom they seek out. Someone courting or meaning to thank a person goes to the store more (and once there picks out something to give, said over their head: lemon drops, a tin of tobacco, the good coffee) and strolls in the hope of passing them. Someone paying a debt back keeps to the work and away from the saloon, the cards and the bottle. Someone set on getting even broods over a drink, keeps an eye out, and has less of a friendly word for anyone. Where a thing is proper still decides. The log's MIND acts line says "and a plan" where it leaned the choice; PLAN ... picks out something at the store.
- Without enRAGE: everyone within the name cards' reach (`NameCardsMetres`) is one of ours, with a name, a trade and a town, not only the three nearest strangers: two new people a second, nearest first, and once named they stay so. Those named for the cards alone get the Awakening's roll the first time the mind actually meets them, so the share of the waking is what it was. With enRAGE running, its townsfolk are its own to name, as before.
- With enRAGE: one of our townsfolk who turns out to be someone enRAGE knew before now goes by enRAGE's name for good, in every record of ours (the resident, their ties, worries and plans), so the card, the thoughts and the bar say one name, and the next time they're ours alone it's the same one. In the log, NAME ... ours goes by ... now.

### Carrying a body you didn't kill isn't murder (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game)

- Picking up a man who had ended his own life got Arthur wanted for murder: a witness took the body over his shoulder for his doing. While you carry a dead person you never hurt, and for 90 seconds after you put them down, a murder, corpse or kidnapping charge is put back to how you stood when you picked them up, and the law stands down. Hurt anyone who hadn't come at you in that time and it's the law's call again. Under `[World] SelfDefenceNoBounty`; in the log, LAW: the game charged you ... for carrying.

### Shouts carry; T steps through the scenes nearby (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game)

- What's shouted is now shouted: anger, threats, fright, a cry for the law ("Thief!"), fight talk, cheering and calling out play with the game's own shouted speech settings, which carry much farther than talk, so a quarrel or a cry down the street reaches you. Ordinary talk is as before; a voice that won't shout a line says it as usual. `[Scenes] ShoutsCarry` (settings version 141).
- **`T`** (the ticker key, or the pad's) steps through the scenes on the bar while it shows two or more nearby: the next one, alone, with where it is and "(2 of 3)", held twelve seconds after the last press. Otherwise it's the next line on the ticker, as before.
- A stranger you greet keeps the name over their head. Greeting one of the townsfolk made enRAGE take them up as one of its own, under a name of its making, and the card (and everything after) went by that. Now, when enRAGE takes up someone whose card was showing our name within the minute (at the greet or a moment after), and its someone is new (seen once, or first seen these five minutes), they're given the name they already had; anyone enRAGE knew before keeps the name it gave them. In the log, NAME enRAGE took up.

### The bar shows every scene; no pair repeats itself (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game)

- With more scenes than the bar's width holds, it shows each in turn, four seconds apiece, with where it is and "(2 of 3)", instead of the nearest and "(+N more)"; when they all fit, all at once as before.
- The same reason between the same two isn't played again for three game hours: one woman's worry at its worst made the same scene with the same friend four times in two minutes once scenes came more often.

### More of the street at once (Lex, 2026-10-30)

- `[Scenes] ScenesAtOnce` 4 (was 2, at most 6 now, was 3; an ini still on 2 is moved to 4; settings version 140): the scene and three quiet talks beside it. Quiet talks come again sooner (a quarter to under half the scene gap, was 40-70%), chance meetings every scene gap (was one and a half), and up to ten people act on their own choices at once, one new every 1.5 s (was six, every 3 s). Still one scene at a time that needs the Director whole (blows, the bully, the pickpocket).

### The language layer, measured (Lex, 2026-10-30: Ollama installed, an RTX 5080 at max settings)

- Its first run in game crashed the game (a stack overflow, the CLR's 0xC00000FD): building a request asks for the written line it may not copy, and the written line was worded through the swap, which built the request again, round and round. A request now gets the written line itself, and the swap can never run inside itself. The tests had it off.
- Measured beside the game at max settings: llama3.1:8b (4-bit) loads in about 7 s once a session, then words a thought in 0.3 to 0.4 s (20 to 26 tokens); with it loaded the card held 13.8 GB of its 16.3. A request now asks for no more than 1,024 tokens of context (less memory, ample for one thought), and the first 25 answers and swaps a session go to the main log (`LANGUAGE answer for ... in N ms`, `LANGUAGE shown for ...`), so a play session shows what the model wrote and how fast.

### Plans over days (the NPC-life run, step 6)

- People carry plans about each other over the next few game days, born from what already lies between them (`Core/Plans.cs`): someone wronged lately (a rough-up, a beating, a mugging, a pocket picked) means to get even with whoever did it (4 days); someone helped (tended, taken home, stood up for) means to thank their helper (5); a debtor with a real creditor means to pay them back (4); a sweetheart means to court (3). One of each a pair, two at most a person, kept in the save; one left undone past its days is let go.
- Near you, with the one it's about also near, the holder goes to find them (`PLAN X goes to find Y`); close, the street's reasons lean to the scene the plan wants (a quarrel from the ladder to get even, a new thanks scene, the debt's own meetings, courting), and a scene of that kind between them carries it out (`PLAN done`). Now and then the holder thinks of it over their head ("I'll find Wade. Today, or tomorrow."), and the Show minds card says it ("plan: get even with Wade (3 days left)"); F7 counts them. Made in the game, each game hour, so the Core's tests stand. `[Mind] MindPlans` (on, with MindLive; settings version 139).

### Fewer wake, and slowly (Lex, 2026-10-30: "slightly turn down the amount of people waking up, and very slow how fast they progress")

- `[WestWorld] AwakeningShare` takes a part of a percent now (0.6), and is 3 by default (was 5; an ini still on 5 is moved to 3). `AwakeningDays` is 5 by default (was 1; an ini still on 1 is moved to 5): at least five game days on each of the first three stages, then a better than even chance each day of the next, so from stirring to fully awake takes three or four weeks of game time, not five days. Settings version 138.

### Names say what they are: lawmen and counter keepers (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game: Hampton Pitre's card had no "Lawman")

- The line under a name starts with their trade where it's plain to see, as enRAGE's cards did: "Lawman" (or "Sheriff", "Deputy" by the game's model), and a town's counter keepers, "Store keeper", "Barkeep", "Gunsmith", "Doctor", "Butcher" and the rest; then their temper ("Lawman - Proud"). For someone you haven't met, the trade takes the place of "Stranger".

### Onlookers join your fistfights too (Lex, 2026-10-30)

- Joining in was only for fights between two of the street's people. Now, while you trade blows with someone, those within 12 m are weighed the same way (Core/JoinIn.cs), you on one side and the man you're fighting on the other: a friend of his, or someone against you, may come at you; someone fond of you may take your side and go for him; a stranger seldom, a drunk hothead in a saloon now and then; nobody with the law at hand or who has seen what you do. Fists only, at most three; those who come at you came at you (self-defence holds); when you're done fighting they leave off. The log's `BRAWL joins your fight:` line.

### A crowd rings a fight; a horse thief (the NPC-life run, step 5)

- While two people trade blows in the street, up to six of those within 20 m who aren't of a fearful nature (the mind's anxiety; nobody it doesn't know), free and on their feet, walk over and stand round it about 4.5 m out, facing in, and cheer or jeer it on now and then; close enough that a friend of one may yet join in. They go back to their own ways when it's over. `[Scenes] FightCrowds` (on, with MindLive; settings version 136). The log's `RING` line.
- In a town, with no lawman within 40 m, a man whose mind leans to it (little honesty, an appetite for risk, money short) or one known for thieving walks to a horse standing with nobody on it (never yours), gets up and rides off at a gallop: a red dot, fair game. Its owner, the last who rode it, shouts after him if near ("Horse thief! That's my horse!"). He's marked a horse thief from then on. At most once a town in two game hours. `[Scenes] HorseThieves` (on, with MindLive; settings version 137). The log's `HORSE` lines.

### The night: a mugging, and a drunk taken home (the NPC-life run, step 5)

- In a town at night, out of doors and with no lawman within 50 m, a man whose mind leans to it (little honesty, an appetite for risk, and money short; or a known pickpocket or mugger) may walk up to someone on their own and demand their money, with a gun if he may draw. Whether they hand it over is their nature's: the anxious and the meek do; the bold refuse, and are knocked down for it. He runs, they cry thief, and the robber is marked a mugger from then on; the victim won't forget him. While he's at it he's fair game, as a bully is.
- The mugger runs as a thief on the run, as the street's pickpocket does: a red dot, fair game, a lawman within 70 m gives chase, and you can run him down yourself.
- A thief the law runs down is hogtied by the lawman now, the game's own hogtie (Lex, 2026-10-30: "lawmen hogtying"), not only told to put his hands up; if the hogtie doesn't take in 6 s, hands up as before. The log says which.
- Late at night, someone in their cups goes down in the street, and a friend near gets them up ("Come on. Up you get.") and the two walk off home together, side by side.
- Half the time he's out cold, and the friend carries him off over the shoulder, the game's own carry (Lex, 2026-10-30: "the in game pickup person animation"); the game carries only the limp, so he goes limp first, and if the carry doesn't take in 5 s he's brought round and walked home instead. The log says which.
- Each at most once a town a night. `[Scenes] NightScenes` (on, with MindLive; settings version 135). The log's `NIGHT` lines.

### More chance meetings (the NPC-life run, step 4)

- A chance meeting between two people with no reason of their own needed them standing within 3.4 m of each other already. Now two up to 6 m apart may meet: one walks over to the other first, as the reasons' meetings do. `[Scenes] ChanceMeetMetres` (6; 2-12; settings version 134). The quiet talks beside a scene keep to people already standing together.

### Names in Saint Denis: never left to enRAGE's cards alone (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game: none in the streets or the saloon)

- While enRAGE's own cards are on (its running NameCards above 0, whatever its file says), ours stood aside entirely; enRAGE cards only its own people, so a city of strangers had no names at all. With names always on, ours never stand aside: they card everyone enRAGE doesn't, and leave enRAGE's people to its own card (one a head). The log says when enRAGE's cards come on or go off.

### Names inside big buildings (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game: none in the Bastille saloon, Saint Denis)

- A card was hidden for anyone whose interior wasn't yours, and a big building is several interiors, so a whole room could go without names. With names always on, only the walls' ray decides now. And each time you go in or out of a building the log says what the cards found: `NAME cards indoors (saloon, Saint Denis): 14 people within 25 m, 9 carded; none for 5 the game's own (...)`, so a room that still shows none says why.

### Whoever joins a fight is fair game too (the NPC-life run, step 3)

- Only the man who started a street fight carried the bully's red dot and was fair game. Now anyone who joins in on it (Core/JoinIn.cs), and a man firing on another in a quarrel while the gunplay lasts, is the same: a red dot, the `BULLY` log line, no crime to stop them, and the one they went for thanks you.

### People go to the real saloon, store and church (the NPC-life run, step 2)

- Someone near you who chooses the saloon, the store or church (MindActs) walked off out of the scene, as if home. Now they walk to the real place: the bar (the nearest saloon's, Valentine having two), the store's counter, or the church's door; there they take a seat, the bar rail or a spot nearby for a couple of minutes (the store: a while at the counter), then go their own way.
- The places are learned, not typed in: wherever a town's counter keeper is seen at work within 120 m (the barkeep at the bar, the store keeper at the counter, and the gunsmith, the doctor, the butcher and the rest for later) is where that place is, kept in the save (`Spots`), so it's known next time. The log's `PLACE` line says when one is learned. A town whose place isn't known yet: off out of the scene, as before.

### Rival gangs fight it out (Lex, 2026-10-30: building on enRAGE's gangs attacking each other; staged for now, the MindsEye in time)

- Every 10 s, men of two different gangs within 90 m of you (the O'Driscolls, the Lemoyne Raiders, the Del Lobos, the Laramie, the Murfrees, the Skinners, the Night Folk, Bronte's and the Braithwaites' men, the Grays; the army and the Pinkertons as one side; never the Wapiti, the story's people or men enRAGE has busy) may meet in a fight: 30%, or 70% where enRAGE has reported a war, feud, clash or rivalry between the two in the last two game days. The nearest of each go toward the other, words are shouted ("Raiders! This is our ground!" / "Make us."), a moment's standoff with guns on each other, then every man of both within 50 m fights the nearest of the other side until a side is beaten or gone, or a minute. At most one in five minutes, and two gangs that passed each other by aren't tried again for half a game hour.
- `[Scenes] GangShootouts` (on; settings version 133). The log's `GANG` lines (who met, the words, how many each side lost); F7 counts them.

### A quarrel may end in shots (Lex, 2026-10-30: "fights escalate to having the NPCs use guns on each other"; deadly, true to 1899)

- The quarrel ladder's last step was always said and never played ("the mind damages nobody yet"): the one who'd have fired thought better of it and both put up. With `[Mind] LadderShots` (on, with MindLive; settings version 132) the shot is fired: the one who pushed the draw to it says a last word and goes for the other with his own sidearm; the other fires back if he carries a gun, or runs. It runs until one is down or 15 s, then the shooter runs from it. Whoever falls stays dead, and what follows a death follows (the ledger, mourners, kin, vengeance). Only where the gun may come out at all: both armed, no lawman near, never in a saloon; the ladder's own odds keep it rare. The log's `SCENE shots:` line.

### After a fight (the NPC-life run, step 1; Lex, 2026-10-30: "consequences that chain")

- A street fight near you no longer ends with everyone going back to what they were doing. Once blows have been traded and it's over, the Director says who was left standing (`FightOver`), and a few seconds on a friend of the one who went down walks over and sees to him ("Hold still. Let me look at that."), or, with no friend there, maybe a kind soul among those who saw it. Then a lawman within 50 m walks over to the one left standing and has a word ("Break it up! Who started this?"), answered by the man's nature (a hothead: "He had it coming, deputy."; a coward: "He started it, sir, I swear it!"; a cold one: "Nothing that concerns the law.") and a warning. Each is a scene of its own when the street is free; it gives up after a minute or once you're 45 m off. The fight's hostile tie already brings the two to words again when they next meet.
- `[Scenes] Aftermath` (on, with MindLive; settings version 131). The log's `AFTER the fight:` lines; F7 counts them.

### What you're wearing, or not (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game: standing naked in the saloon and nobody minded)

- People notice when you're naked (nothing over the body or the legs) or undressed (shirtless, or in your drawers), read every 3 s from the game's clothing categories (shirt, coat, vest, dress, cloak, poncho; pants, skirt; the body itself and a hat, boots or a gunbelt never count, and any category not known counts as covering, so a gap can't make a dressed man naked). The log's `SIGHT clothes:` line says what was read each time it changes.
- In 1899 that was a scandal: a woman is shocked (a gasp, a look away, "Lord above! Cover yourself!") and often turns and goes, as the timid and some plain men do; the bold and your friends laugh at it ("Lose your clothes at cards, mister?"); the cold and those against you are disgusted; anyone who has seen what you do is afraid. Naked is noticed before blood, drink or mud, anywhere (a saloon too, and while a street scene plays), by up to three at once and eight times a town a day; undressed is nothing out in the wilds. New thoughts for each.

### Where scenes start, and the bar lists them all (Lex, 2026-10-30)

- The street's reasons start a scene within 24 m of you (was 16), the two up to 9 m apart (was 7); the mind's near ring, whose people act on their choices, reaches 25 m (was 20); chance meetings and the quiet talks start within 13 m (was 22: 60% of it, close enough to hear and read); the night's scenes within 21 m (was 16, 30% more). Scenes still start in view, and still stop when you're 50 m off.
- A scene no longer needs one of its people on screen to start: anywhere within its radius, behind you too, so the street goes on whether you look or not (Lex, 2026-10-30). What costs frame time is how many play at once (`ScenesAtOnce`), not where you look.
- The Nearby bar lists every scene playing within 40 m, nearest first, each with how far and which way ("Town gossip (Ada and Clem) - 5 m east  ·  Two men came to blows (Simeon and Morgan) - 15 m south"), as many as the bar's width (`TickerWidth`) holds, the rest "(+2 more)", so it never runs off the screen.
- A gang shootout starts only between men of the two sides within 50 m of each other, and takes in only those near the meeting (one in Rhodes ended before it began, the Grays 90 m off).

### Self-defence holds after the fight too (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game, Rhodes)

- Four men came at him and he killed them, self-defence by every rule; witnesses were held back for the fight and 90 s after, and two minutes on one of them went to the law and the game called it murder ($15, wanted). For ten minutes after a self-defence killing, a murder or assault charge on him, with nobody innocent hurt since, is put back to how he stood before the fight, bounty and wanted alike. The log says `LAW: a witness took your self-defence killing of ... to the law as murder ...: no longer wanted`.

### The law: charges in words, and a trampling that wasn't yours (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game)

- The log's `LAW` lines name the game's charge ("killing a horse (0xC7261D79)", "trampling (0x45DB39D8)") instead of a number alone: all 58 of the game's crime types (`Core/Crimes.cs`, from femga/rdr3_discoveries).
- A man run down by someone else's two-horse wagon, and the game charged Arthur with trampling ($15, wanted). When the ledger has someone die under hooves or wheels that weren't his (not by his hand, nor his own horse under him within 8 m), a trampling charge on him in the next 30 s is put back to how he stood before it, bounty and wanted alike; the log says `LAW: the game charged you with trampling ..., but another's horse or wagon did it`.

### Pickpockets keep to their trade (Lex, 2026-10-30)

- The Testing page's cast is never sent to pick your pocket (the old man of the cast was, and was shot).
- A man who has picked your pocket stays a pickpocket: one of RealNPCs' kept people from then on, marked, and met (he's had his hand in your pocket), so his name shows with "Pickpocket" under it; a known pickpocket near you is the one who goes next time, before any other man. Roadmap 28, the criminal class, takes it further: pickpockets and worse working other people too.

### Less said about mud (Lex, 2026-10-30: "it's difficult for players to clean their character")

- Filthy is remarked only when you're caked (the dirt level from 0.95, was 0.9; in Saint Denis and Blackwater from 0.75, was 0.6), and once a town a day (was three times).

## 0.7.3 (2026-10-30)

Lex's in-game pass of 2026-10-30 in Valentine, after 0.7.2, and the start of the NPC-life run: several street scenes at once (quiet talks beside the scene), every quarrel from the mind and fights that spread as onlookers join in on a side, people acting on their own everyday choices, names over heads always on within 25 m, the bar saying where a scene is, and fixes from the evening's play (a T pose at the bar, a pickpocket who stood still, brawls from chairs, a wake tag lost behind a thought). Much of it is new and not yet seen at length in game; each part has its own switch.

### Several street scenes at once (the NPC-life run, Lex 2026-10-30: "more frequent NPC-to-NPC scenes, including several at once")

- While a street scene plays, and between scenes, other people elsewhere on the street now have quiet talks of their own: two with a reason to talk first (gossip, small talk, a kindness, the old days), else the two standing closest, at least 10 m from the scene and never anyone already in one. A quiet talk is words only (no blows, no walk-over, nobody stepping in), so the scene keeps everything that needs one owner. Its lines are heard in their own voices, with a subtitle when it's within `ScenesOnTickerMetres` of you; they never wait in line with the scene's. The bar names the nearest when no scene plays.
- `[Scenes] ScenesAtOnce` (2; 1-3; settings version 127; on the Essentials page under The world): the scene and up to this many less one quiet talks. 1: just the scene, as before.
- A chance meeting that came to nothing no longer holds the next one back a minute and a half: the wait starts when one really starts.

### People act on their own choices (the NPC-life run, step 2; Lex, 2026-10-30: "start with the mind acting on choices")

- What each mind chooses to do (needs, nature, mood, who's near: Core/Choosing.cs) was only written to the log as `MIND would:`. Now, when someone near you and in view changes their mind, they do it: take a stroll; sit down to rest, eat, have a drink, join the cards, get on with the work or say a prayer at the nearest of the game's own spots within 15 m (a bench, a porch, a chair, the bar, a work point); head off home or out of the weather, walking off out of the scene; keep well clear of you, or step clear of someone they can't abide; go over to a friend for a word, or to someone standing near to talk (two standing together, a quiet talk may follow); keep a wary eye on you when they've seen you do violence.
- Gently: never someone sat, leaning or at something already, busy in a scene or another of RealNPCs' moments, held by enRAGE or the story, riding, fighting or running; one new act every 3 s at most and six acting at once; each let go to their own ways once done, arrived or after a while. `[Mind] MindActs` (on, with MindLive; settings version 130). The log's `MIND acts:` line says who did what and why; F7 counts them by kind.

### The wake stage tag sits on top of the thought (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game)

- With Show wake stages on, someone's tag ("Stirring (1 of 4)") went from sight while their thought showed over their head. The thought box now sits on the point over the head, the tag on top of it, and the Show minds card above both.

### The pickpocket keeps running; nobody brawls from a chair (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game)

- The man who lifted your money in the saloon stood where he was, twice: he was told to flee once and never looked at again, and indoors the game found him no way out. Now every 2 s, if he's moved less than a metre and isn't down, he's sent running again, turn about away from you and from where you stand; the log says `PICKPOCKET stood still ... sent running again`.
- A brawl between two people sat or leaning never came to blows: the chair kept them even pulled out of it (Bryant and David, 16 s of nothing). A fistfight now never casts someone sat or leaning, and a quarrel the ladder takes to blows between such stays words.

### Names over heads, always on (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game: "the nameplates are sometimes not showing up")

- In a crowded saloon some people had no name: only the ten nearest were carded, a table, a post or a lamp in the way hid a card as a wall did, cards faded out over the last 12 m, and they were drawn last of all, after the Testing page's cards over people, so a full frame dropped them first. `[Display] NameCardsAlways` (on; settings version 129, on the Essentials page): a card over everyone in range, up to sixteen, hidden only by walls, full until the last 4 m, drawn before the Testing overlays. Off: as before.
- The cards leave the Testing overlays their room: drawn first, they had spent the frame's text, and a woman's wake stage tag went the moment her Fondness bar came (Lex, 2026-10-30). With Show wake stages or Show minds on, the names stop short of what those need.
- Names within 25 m (`NameCardsMetres`, was 30; an ini still on 30 is moved to 25, a distance of your own is kept), on the Essentials page too. People you know keep their own distance (`NameCardsKnownMetres`, 75).

### Fights that spread: onlookers join in on a side (Lex, 2026-10-30: "between any 2+ NPCs if their inner thoughts lead to it")

- While two people trade blows, every few seconds the onlookers within 12 m are weighed by their minds (`Core/JoinIn.cs`): a friend of one fighter, or someone with bad blood against the other, may join on a side and go for the one they hate most, else the one who started on that side; a drunk hothead may wade in, a saloon's most of all. Most watch, and a stranger is less likely to join the bigger the crowd. Nobody joins with a lawman within 30 m, nobody sat or leaning, at work, mounted or already busy. A man's hand against a woman at a tenth of his rate (1899, Lex's word), a woman joins at half and only with WomenInFights. At most three join, a single one each look, so it spreads: the friend of the man just set upon may be next. Each joiner fights with their fists for a fight that runs a little longer, their own ways back when it ends, and leaves a tie with the one they went for.
- `[Mind] MindJoinFights` (on, with MindLive; settings version 128). The log's `BRAWL joins:` line says who, for whom, at whom and why; F7's dump counts them. mindtest's JoinInTest holds the numbers (strangers about 1%, a friend about 38%, bad blood about 32%, a drunk in a saloon about 6%, the law 0).

### Every quarrel from the mind (Lex, 2026-10-30: "it can break out between any 2+ NPCs if their inner thoughts lead to it")

- With MindLive, an argument or a fistfight between any two people near you is played from the quarrel ladder: whether it stays words, comes to a shove, to blows or worse is theirs, from their natures, drink, what it's over, who's watching and their history. The ladder had passed people over when its last look at them, up to ten seconds stale, found nobody (Nimrod Hatch, known all morning, was "not known yet"); now it looks again.
- The coin that let a woman into a fight half the time gives way to the ladder when it plays the quarrel; its own 1899 rule stands (a man's hand against a woman at a tenth of his rate, by Lex's word). Without the mind, the coin as before.
- The fistfight test (`TestBrawlKey`) takes any two named people (women too with WomenInFights), not only two men.

### No T pose when a fight starts at the bar (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game)

- A man leaning on the bar or sat when a brawl started was pulled out of it at once after 3 s, and went into a T pose before the fight (Ned Fleming, in the Valentine saloon). He's now asked to get up the game's way, again at 4 and 6 s, and pulled out only past 8 s (logged when it happens), with the punches ordered a moment after; a fight has 16 s to start (was 12).

### The bar says where the scene is (Lex, 2026-10-30)

- "Nearby: two men came to blows (Simeon and Morgan) - 10 m south": how far the street scene is from you, to the nearest 5 m, and which way (eight points of the compass, north up the map).

## 0.7.2 (2026-10-30)

The night run of 2026-10-29 (the mind acting on what people feel) joined to 0.7.1, with Lex's in-game pass of 2026-10-30 that afternoon in Valentine: `[Mind] MindLive` is on for everyone now (settings version 126 switches an ini still on off, once), so onlookers, people stepping in, greetings, help after a fall and talking someone down follow the minds; the settings panel pauses the world and takes the mouse; a hermit's name over their head; a willing woman's voice no longer turns you down; the names' own settings on the panel can be changed again.

### The settings panel: the world paused, and the mouse (Lex, 2026-10-30)

- The world pauses while the panel is open, clock and all (`[Output] PanelTimeScale` 0; it was 0.25, slowed; an ini still on 0.25 is moved to 0 at settings version 125, a speed of your own is kept).
- The mouse works on the panel: the game's cursor shows, pointing at a row chooses it, a left click changes it forward (or does it, or sets its key) and a right click back, a click on the page's name turns the page, and a click on the line under the panel switches the essentials and every setting. The wheel scrolls the list three rows a notch (the chosen row stays while it's in view), and the scrollbar can be clicked or dragged. The game's own cursor controls read nothing outside its menus, so the pointer and buttons are read from Windows. Seen working in game (Lex, 2026-10-30).

### A hermit's name over their head (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game)

- A hermit at their camp had no name over their head: the body is one of RealNPCs' own people, but it was never tied to the town's, which is where the names over heads look. The hermit now shows as anyone does ("Stranger" until you've met).

### Flirting: her voice matches her answer (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game)

- A willing woman could be heard turning you down: flirting again too soon gets a "slow down" ("One at a time."), and when the game turned its voice down while she was busy it was tried again and played seconds later, over her next yes. A new flirt now drops whatever is still waiting from the last one.
- "Slow down" sounds like a tease now, not a cold shoulder (no more IGNORING_YOU for it).

### The settings panel: the names' own settings could be changed again (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game)

- With Names over heads on known or everyone, the settings under it (Strangers' names, the personality line, the distances) stayed greyed out: the panel only took true or a number above 0 as on. A choice is now on unless it says off.

### The flirt keys (Lex, 2026-10-29: "H to flirt, G to engage in the hookup if the NPC is interested")
