- H in the lock-on menu is always Flirt now. Before, the moment she said yes the same prompt turned into Hook up, so a second H asked her before her answer had even been heard. Hook up is its own prompt on G, shown only while she is willing and once her answer has been said. F2 is gone as the flirt key (`FlirtKey` None; an ini still on F2, or on the older F6 or 5, is moved to None at settings version 106; a key of your own stays). The notices say Hook up (G). With CasualHookups installed its own Flirt key still finishes it (B, as RealNPCs set it once). Not yet seen in game.

### The one who steps in walks over first (Lex, 2026-10-29: the woman who stood up to a bully said her piece from 9 m off)

- Someone joining a street scene from a few steps off (the one who stands up to a bully, a friend who talks his man down in a quarrel, a jealous husband or wife) now walks all the way over before the scene goes on, and says it face to face. Before, they had under four seconds of walking and were then turned to face the others wherever they had got to. Someone sitting or leaning is let go of it first; a walker who stops short is sent on again; after a few seconds more than the distance needs (12 at most) the scene goes on from wherever they are, and the log says how far off they stopped. A quarrel's friend walks to his own man, not to whichever of the two happened to be first. Not yet seen in game.

### Who steps in comes from their own minds (roadmap 39; Lex, 2026-10-29: "Ideally we want NPCs to dynamically react based on their inner psychology")

- With `[Mind] MindLive` and the new `[Mind] MindStandUp` (on; settings version 107), who steps into a rough or a brawl between two others is no longer a roll on temper and ties. Each onlooker within 10 m whom RealNPCs knows takes the trouble the way the mind would once it had happened (what it means to them as a friend of either or a stranger, how many are watching, their own nature), 1899's rules say whether it may show (a woman of standing keeps her anger off her face unless she's hot-tempered; a lawman nearby or a church hushes it), and the one most moved to step in does. Offline, with five strangers watching a rough, somebody steps in seven times in ten, the loyal and the hot-tempered half the time each, cowards one time in sixteen, the cold almost never; the bigger the crowd, the less each one does. The pacing roll is gone for these (the rests between stand-ups stay). Women step in too, at half a man's rate for now (Lex, 2026-10-29: women may get into fights, and men may trade blows with them, half as likely until the mind decides it). The bully's answer comes from his own mind: faced by a man, a hothead or a proud man holds his ground and most others weigh it (37% back down in all); shamed by a woman before the street, a little over half back down (54%; a coward most, 86%, the proud 77%, a hothead least, 17%). When the bully holds his ground, the one who stepped in may take the other away instead (a friend of the one picked on six times in ten, a stranger about a third): the two leave together, away from him, while he jeers after them, and it never comes to blows (and the two remember it: taken away from a brawl is someone taking your side); before, they were waved off and the fight went on as if nothing had been said. With `MindStandUp` off, or for anyone the mind doesn't know, the old roll. The one walking over is checked again as they set off (a story mission or enRAGE may have taken them up since). In a quarrel from the ladder, the friend who talks his man down is one whose own mind says to step in (a friend of one of the two does about two times in five, a stranger one in six); a friend whose mind only says watch doesn't, and the quarrel goes its own way. Log: the `KIND stood up:` line says what the mind felt (`the mind: Rosa Wadsworth: pity (strong), urge intervene, shows shown; 2 of the 4 the mind knows would, 7 watching`) and the bully's (`Abe's mind: fear (moderate), urge avoid: backs down`); when nobody's mind moves them, F7 `TEST kindness:` says what the first few felt. Not yet seen in game.

### Lighter: the names over heads

- The names over heads build nothing anew each frame: each card keeps its text, made ready for the screen, and its width until what it says changes, and the look for who is near skips a far body sooner. They draw exactly as before (for everyone, MindLive or not). Not yet measured in game.

### Who's who, among your papers (roadmap 24)

- With the new `[Display] WhosWho` (on; settings version 116), for every player (Lex, 2026-10-30), the papers you carry end with Who's who: the people you have met, the most recent first (forty at most). Each says who they are ("Ruth Avery, a steadfast laundress of Valentine"; someone who never told you their name is only what they look to be), when you first and last saw them, how they take you (fond of you, well disposed, cool, bitter), what has passed between you (spoken, flirted, seen your doings), and the people they are friends or at odds with, named only if you know the name yourself; and, with `[Mind] MindLive`, for anyone near you this session, what they feel about you lately, as their greeting would show it ("Lately very grateful to you.", "Lately afraid of you."). The dead are marked and spoken of in the past. Read only: it is made from what RealNPCs already keeps each time you open your papers, and never kept itself. Not yet seen in game.

### People show what they feel about you as you pass (roadmap 39)

- With `[Mind] MindLive` and `[Mind] MindPassing`, someone who feels something about you from a while back shows it when you pass, the way they show it to each other: grateful, a hat tip or a wave and a greeting in their own voice (one a game day; a nod after that); sore at you, a hard look (a spit at your feet now and then from the cold, the proud and the hot-tempered, never a woman), or, if they fear you too, a sullen look away; afraid of you, a glance and away; shamed before you, eyes down. A feeling kept off the face shows only in their thought. What you did a moment ago is the onlookers' to answer, not this (a quarter of an hour at least). One person every second and a half at most, each once in five minutes. Log (detail) `NOD you:`; counted in F7's `TEST mind acts:`. Not yet seen in game.

### The street's meetings lean by their moods (roadmaps 18 and 30)

- With `[Mind] MindLive` and the new `[Mind] MindMeetings` (on; settings version 115), what passes between two people who meet near you leans by the day each is having: two sore people come to words sooner than to small talk, two glad ones the other way, and someone lonely stops for a word. Only a lean on reasons they already have: nobody quarrels who had no cause, and nobody chats who had no tie or reason to. The `REASON` line says the lean (`the mind: +12 (Abe glad, Ruth glad)`). Not yet seen in game.

### Despair's other side: someone may talk them down (roadmap 3)

- With `[Mind] MindLive` and the new `[Mind] MindRescue` (on; settings version 113) and `RescueChance` (60; version 114), despair is no longer only an ending. When someone awake begins their last words, anyone near whom RealNPCs knows takes it in by their own nature; a friend within 12 m, or a stranger within 8 m moved more than a little, walks over while the last words go on (if they get there in time: `RescueChance`), and says it to their face, in words of the time (a close friend by name; a man to a woman he doesn't know, "ma'am"). A friend is heeded four times in five, a stranger a little over half. Heeded, they come away, and the two keep it: a tie between them and the memory of who stopped them. Not heeded, the despair goes on as before. Nobody steps in if nobody near is moved; the one coming is checked again all the way and gives up if they're taken up or too slow. Offline, a friend nearby is moved to go nine times in ten and a stranger one in three. Log `WAKE despair: ... sees it coming and goes to ...`, `talked ... down`, `wouldn't be talked down`; F7 `TEST rescue:`. Not yet seen in game.

### Who helps after a fall comes from their minds (roadmap 39)

- With `[Mind] MindLive` and the new `[Mind] MindHelps` (on; settings version 112), when someone near you takes a fall, the one who comes is the one most moved by it of those who saw it (not simply the nearest), and how they help comes from what they feel: moved, a hand up; a little moved and not the gentle sort, hauled up gruffly; unmoved, they walk past. A friend still always gives a hand, and a foe still sneers or walks on. Offline, a stranger gives a hand three times in five (one in four of those gruffly), and about three times in four for a woman or an old person down (73%): the loyal nearly always, the hot-tempered a little over a third (38%), the cold one time in five. A foe is never the one who comes. The `KIND fall:` line says what the mind felt. Not yet seen in game.

### Greetings follow how they feel about each other (roadmap 39)

- With `[Mind] MindLive` and the new `[Mind] MindPassing` (on; settings version 111), when two people who know each other pass in the street, the greeting follows what the first one feels about the other lately and the day they're having, not only their tie: sore at a friend over something lately, a curt nod and no word; envious, a nod and no more; afraid of them, eyes away and walk on; shamed by them, eyes down; grateful, a warm word even to someone they barely know; a feeling they keep off their face leaves the greeting as it was, and only their thought says it; the day's mood is read against their own nature, so a sour sort still greets a friend; with nothing felt about the other, a sore day keeps it to a nod and a glad one has a word for anyone they know. Their thought over their head says what they really feel (35 new lines). Enemies pass as before, with the hard look. Log (detail) `NOD the mind:`. Not yet seen in game.

### Known again by their look, standing alone (roadmap 34, phase A's second half; off as it comes)

- `[World] OwnLooks` (off until seen in game; settings version 110): without enRAGE, an ordinary person RealNPCs has kept a record of is known again by the look of the body they were met in (the build and every piece they wear, with its colours), so the townsman you spoke to yesterday is the same man today, with his memories and his ties, instead of a stranger with his face. Only in their own town, the same build and sex; never a town regular (they keep their own clothes), the test cast or a shopkeeper at his counter; nobody when two people RealNPCs keeps wear the same look, or when the one it would be is in another body in sight. With enRAGE running, enRAGE's own knowing by look is used and this never. The look is kept with the person once something happens to them (a new optional field; old saves load as before). Adapted from enRAGE's own way of reading a look, with its author's permission. Log `LOOKS known again by their look:`; F7 `TEST own looks:`. Not yet seen in game.

### The Awakening follows what they lived through (the mind design's step 10, second half)

- With `[Mind] MindLive`, West World and the new `[Mind] MindWakes` (on; settings version 109), the Awakening reads the mind's beliefs. What someone has lived through moves their sense that "things are as they seem" (something uncanny, a killing seen, a buried man walking shake it; a kindness steadies it), and that leans who begins to wake and how soon they wake further: the daily chance to move on a stage, 60% as before, runs from 40% for the steadied to 78% for the badly shaken. The moment something they live through makes that sense give way, someone asleep begins to stir at once ("slowly, then suddenly"), and someone already waking moves on a stage if a day has passed; once a person a session, and never while the Awakening is switched off (`AwakeningShare` 0). And the revived dead are seen: someone RealNPCs saw die who walks near you again (one of enRAGE's people it brought back, or a townsperson it missed; a death by your own hand isn't counted yet) is known by anyone near who saw them die, by their own memory of it; they're frightened (with `[Mind] MindOnlookers` on, most back away from him and a hard fright roots them to the spot), and that alone breaks their sense of things, so they begin to wake. Only people RealNPCs already keeps a record of are moved. Log `AWAKENING from doubt:` and `MIND revived:`; F7 `TEST awakening from doubt:`. Not yet seen in game.

### Onlookers act on what they feel (roadmap 39, part 2)

- With `[Mind] MindLive` and the new `[Mind] MindOnlookers` (on; settings version 108), someone who only watched a quarrel, a fight or something you did does something about it, by what they feel and what 1899 lets them show, instead of only turning their head: the frightened walk off, a man who keeps his fear off his face slips away quietly, the pious and the orderly shake their heads and walk off, a gun or something uncanny roots them to the spot, contempt shakes its head at it, and a keen eye turns the whole body to watch; in a saloon, where a fight was nothing new, a mild fright stays to watch. At most two people act on any one thing, the most moved first, a couple of seconds after it starts; never the law, anyone at a counter, anyone sitting or leaning, in a scene, a waking act or a conversation, nobody past 30 m, and nothing more than 15 seconds late. Only on trouble between people and on what you did: never on a pickpocket at work, so the crowd doesn't give him away. A stranger's mild fright doesn't send them off; they watch it warily. Offline, over crowds of four to ten watching a rough or a brawl, onlookers act in about two scenes in three, one act a scene: turning to watch more than half the time (the commonest thing real crowds do), walking off about a quarter, a head-shake, a disapproving walk-off or a quiet slip away the rest; of those who walk off, more are men than women. Log `ONLOOK` (within the 20 MIND lines a minute); F7 `TEST onlookers:`. Not yet seen in game.

### Women in fights, half as often as men (Lex, 2026-10-29: "we can allow men to trade blows with women in this mod, that is fine for women to get involved in fights, but we could add it being 50% less likely than a man getting in a fight until we have the innermind logic take over")

- `[Scenes] WomenInFights` (on; settings version 107; not behind MindLive, by Lex's word): until now only two men ever came to blows. Now a quarrel that comes to blows, a snap at the worst of a worry, drink and temper in a saloon, two hot tempers who won't give way and a chance meeting's brawl may have a woman in it, at half a man's chance for each woman (a man and a woman half as often as two men, two women a quarter), seeded by the pair so the same quarrel gives the same answer. With the quarrel ladder (MindLive) a woman may take a quarrel from the shove to blows herself at half a man's heat, and a man's blows against her are half as hot (they were a tenth). The ladder's gun rungs are as before, and she never draws one, but with more of his quarrels with her reaching blows, a gun is drawn on a woman about twice as often as before (offline, 36 against 15 in 3,000 quarrels between a drunk armed man and an armed foe). The face into the bar, a drunk calling someone out and the card cheat stay men's (their moves were only ever seen played by men). The words fit: "a man and a woman came to blows" (a quarrel from the ladder says "two people came to blows"), and the barkeep doesn't call them gentlemen. Off: two men only, as before. Not yet seen in game.

## 0.7.1 (2026-10-30)

Built on 0.7.0 during Lex's in-game pass of 2026-10-30, from his tests and a players' request, and seen working in game that day: the name plates (easier to hide, "Stranger" until you've met), self-defence that the law now honours (the pickpocket who robbed you, anyone who comes at you, a bully you step in on), pickpockets on foot and walking straight up, what people say near you as subtitles, Fondness, Reset RealNPCs Memory at a station clerk, the Mod list, and a settings panel that opens on the essentials. None of the night run's mind work is in it (that waits on its own branch).

### Pickpockets (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game)

- The thief walks straight up to you. His walk was given anew several frames in a row every 3 s, and each new one stopped him, so he walked, stopped and walked again, and from 20 m off gave up before he reached you. He is sent on only when he stands with no walk running.
- He gets close enough to take it: the bump counts from 2 m (was 1.3, and the walk stops him short), standing within 4 m he is sent the last step, and a far start gets 1.5 s a metre more before he gives up; the give-up line says how near he came.
- A pickpocket works on foot: a man with his own horse within 25 m is never picked (the game had one ride up to you and off with the cash), one who gets on a horse anyway gives up, and the bump needs you both on foot.
- Nothing else takes him on the way: the pickpocket on his way to you and on the run counts as busy for the rest of RealNPCs (the test cast's stroll had one back at his spot, 7 m off, until he gave up), and he is sent on whenever he comes no nearer for 3 s.
- His dot turns red even when he already had one (the test cast's minute mark is taken off and a red one made; anyone else's dot is turned red).

### The law: self-defence holds (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game)

- Shooting the pickpocket who robbed you made you wanted, and the lawmen near fought you. RealNPCs only looked at the wanted level, which stays 0 while the law is after you, so it never cleared it; and the game's pursuit, once started, comes back however often it is cleared. Now RealNPCs sees the pursuit and puts the bounty back, and while a fight with someone who came at you is on (90 s after your last hit on them) and you've hurt nobody else, it holds witnesses back from calling the law and has lawmen already fighting you let go. Those calls alone didn't stop the game's own witnesses; the next line is what did.
- Killing whoever came at you is no murder, the game's own way: the game's pickpocket script (beat_pickpocket) marks its thief so what's done to him doesn't count toward your wanted level (PED config flag 6), and suppressing the crime never held one back. RealNPCs now marks its pickpocket at once and everyone it sees coming at you, for five minutes. Seen in game: shooting the pickpocket who robbed you is no longer a witnessed murder.
- Stepping in on a bully is defending the man he has hold of (Lex, 2026-10-30): a man slamming another into a bar or wall, or the one who started a street fistfight while they're at it, is fair game then and for a minute after the fight ends, with a red dot on the map while he is, so you can tell who to stop (the log names him: BULLY). The man you stepped in for keeps out of it (no answering the shots for twelve seconds), turns to you and thanks you, where before he could turn his gun on you.
- Bumping into someone on the way no longer spoils it: a stumble within 2 m of you counted as hurting someone who hadn't come at you (the mind's shove, 0.7.0), which ended self-defence for five minutes; the game charges no crime for it. It stays a slight in people's minds, and if they answer it, you started it. A real blow still counts.
- The log's new `LAW` lines say each time you become wanted or your bounty changes, and what RealNPCs saw at the time (a fight in self-defence, the game's charge, a despairing shot near). `LAW stage` lines follow the game's own stages (a witness on the way to tell, witnesses active, the law investigating, wanted, the red circle's radius) and name who near you carries a map mark. Not yet all seen in game.
- A search that dies down while the law can't see you is roadmap 27: the game keeps no score a mod can lower for it, and clearing the wanted level doesn't end a search.
- For testing: three seconds after the test cast arrives, `TESTCAST after arrival` logs what each of them is doing (calm, fleeing, running), whether you have a gun out, the dead within 30 m and the law's stage.

### What people say near you is a subtitle, not a line at the bottom (Lex, 2026-10-30: "There shouldn't be inner thoughts showing at the bottom of the screen")

- The words of a street scene, a hello or goodbye, the doctor's visit, the law chase and the pickpocket's "Pardon me" were drawn on the bar at the bottom, where they read as thoughts (a mourner's "Last night I dreamed he walked in the door"). They now show as they're said in the subtitle box drawn the game's way ("Hermine: \"...\""), within `[Ticker] ScenesOnTickerMetres` of you (12 m, was 30: Lex saw a mourner's line from 26 m off, out of sight; an ini still on 30 is moved to 12 at settings version 120); `[Output] Subtitles = off` hides them, and a line said to you is never covered by one. The bar keeps the news; thoughts stay over heads. Voicing these lines in the speakers' own voices is roadmap 1. Not yet seen in game.

### A simpler settings panel: the essentials, F for everything (Lex, 2026-10-30: "there are so many pages and different settings it's hard to read")

- The panel opens on the essentials: an Essentials page (the world: West World, stalkers, encounters, romance, pickpockets; on the screen: names over heads and the personality line, the bars, the meter, the bar at the bottom and its scene line, thoughts over heads, subtitles; the law: no bounty for self-defence; memory: the reset at a station clerk), the Hotkeys and the Mod list. F, shown at the bottom centre of every page, switches to every setting and the Testing page, and back; `[Output] PanelShowsAll` (off; settings version 123) keeps the choice.

### The Mod list page (Lex, 2026-10-30: "to be able to turn on/off other mods from the settings page")

- A last page on the settings panel lists the other mods: the Script Hook .NET scripts in the scripts folder (enRAGE among them) and the ASI plugins in the game folder, each on or off, with its .ini named as its settings when there is one. Enter, then Enter again, turns one off (its file renamed with ".off" after it, so the game passes it over) or back on; nothing is deleted. A script takes it with the next Insert reload, an ASI plugin only when the game next starts, and the page says which. What modding stands on (Script Hook, its .NET hook, the ASI loader, Lenny's mod loader, the .NET API and Newtonsoft.Json) and RealNPCs itself aren't listed. Not yet seen in game.

### Reset RealNPCs Memory at a station clerk (Lex, 2026-10-30: "the NPCs very quickly come to have very high fear and anger levels with the player")

- At a station agent or clerk, the list with Telegraph, Train Tickets and Pay Bounty also offers Reset RealNPCs Memory: hold Q (a pad's RB) for two seconds and everyone forgets what they know and feel about you, while the world stays (Lex's pick). In RealNPCs: your name to them, the talks, sightings, hearsay, the deeds of yours they saw or heard of, their memories of you, their fondness and courting (sweethearts too), your deeds and the town's talk of them, kin riding in for revenge, word still waiting to spread, and the dead you killed no longer yours. With enRAGE installed: its townsfolk's fear, fondness, grudge and favours, what they saw of you and knowing you at all, and the strangers' grudges building toward a nemesis; enRAGE's nemeses and their hate are left as they are. The people, the regulars, families, diaries already written and the ties between people stay. Copies of RealNPCs' save and of enRAGE's people, world and nemeses go to `RealNPCs\backup` first; if a copy fails, nothing is forgotten. Without enRAGE, RealNPCs' part alone. `[Features] ResetAtClerk` (on; settings version 121); also on the Testing page (Everyone forgets you). The test cast is let go by it (bring them again). Not yet seen in game.

### Fondness, and subtitles under the game's own (Lex, 2026-10-30)

- `[Ticker] TickerShowsScene` (on; settings version 122), on the UI page: while a street scene plays out within 40 m, the bar at the bottom says what is going on and who is in it ("Nearby: Two men came to blows (Gideon and Asa)").
- The Testing page's Tools have Show a test subtitle, RealNPCs' box for six seconds; the log notes where each subtitle is drawn.

- Someone you haven't met is "Stranger" over their head, with no name under or over it (Lex: "have their name show as Stranger until they are greeted or interacted with"). Met is greeting, talking, flirting, any contact, or their speaking up to you; only seeing you do something no longer counts. enRAGE's nemeses and the game's own named people (sheriffs, the story cast) keep their names. `[Display] NameCardsStrangerNames` (off; settings version 124), on the Essentials page: on, a stranger's name shows with "Stranger" under it, as before.

- The middle bar under people is "Fondness" (was "Liking"): how fond of you they are, beside their fear and anger.
- RealNPCs' subtitles sat on the game's own subtitle line and the two overlapped. Ours now start just under the game's line and grow down toward the news bar.

### Names over heads: easier to hide, or the name alone (players' request, 2026-10-30)

- The names over heads could already be turned off (`[Display] NameCards = off`, Names over heads on the settings panel), but the panel page holding it was called "Bars and meter"; it is now "UI (Nameplates, bars, etc)", and holds every setting for what is on the screen: the names over heads, the bars and the meter, the ticker and the thoughts over heads, the subtitles and notices, and the panel's own (Lex, 2026-10-30; the ini keeps its sections; the rest of [Output] is "Files and the log"). With enRAGE installed, the cards over heads are its own (ours stand aside for them), so turning ours off changed nothing (Lex, 2026-10-30, in game): off on the panel now sets enRAGE's `[Menu] NameCards` to 0 too, after the usual copy of NemesisMod.ini (turning ours back on leaves enRAGE's off). New: `[Display] NameCardsSecondLine` (on; settings version 117), Personality line under the names on the same page: off shows the name alone, without the temper, trade, "Stranger" or gang under it. Not yet seen in game.

## 0.7.0 (2026-10-29)

Built in the night run of 2026-10-28 (Lex's brief), the day after it and the in-game pass that evening, on the 0.6.3 release. RealNPCs now stands alone (no enRAGE needed, compatible with it); most of what follows was seen working in game on 2026-10-29. The mind stays in shadow: everything new that would change what people think, say or do sits behind `[Mind] MindLive` (off), and with it off every path runs as it did in 0.6.3, except the two players' reports below: live changes Lex asked for during the run, one commit to take back.

### Names over heads (Lex, 2026-10-29: "enRAGE did a really good job of placing NPC names over the NPC head that looked really good and our mod wasn't doing this")

- People RealNPCs knows carry their name over their head in the game's title type, the way enRAGE drew its cards: the name, a smaller line under it (a temper, a trade, "Stranger", or a nemesis's hate: Wary, Sore, Vengeful, Murderous, coloured gold to red), no box, steadied as they walk, hidden behind walls and in other rooms, fading out near the edge of the range and smaller with distance. Who gets one: enRAGE's records and townsfolk when it runs, and RealNPCs' own people always: the town regulars, anyone who has thought over their head, spoken with you or taken part in a street scene. `[Display] NameCards` = off | known | everyone (everyone adds what a stranger looks like, "A townsman", in grey; names over every passer-by wait for RealNPCs' own naming by look), `NameCardsMetres` (30, strangers) and `NameCardsKnownMetres` (75, people you know). While enRAGE runs with its own cards on, ours stay off unless `NameCardsBesideEnrage`. Adapted from enRAGE's card drawing with its author's permission. Settings version 97; F7 `TEST name cards:`.
- The game's own named people have their names too (Lex, the same afternoon: the sheriff of Valentine had none): the story characters (Sheriff Malloy, Sheriff Leigh Gray, Dutch, the Braithwaites and the Grays, some three hundred), the named bounty targets, and the uniques the game names by their job and town ("Valentine deputy", "Rhodes clerk"), from a table of our own adapted from enRAGE's. The line under the name is what the model says of them ("Valentine sheriff"), their temper, or "Wanted" for a bounty; enRAGE's own notes on them stay its.

### Standing alone, step 1 (Lex, 2026-10-29: RealNPCs is to need enRAGE less and less, and one day not at all)

- RealNPCs now loads with enRAGE absent. Before, with `NemesisMod.dll` gone from `scripts`, Script Hook .NET couldn't construct the add-on at all (`ScriptHookRDRDotNet.log`: `Could not load file or assembly 'NemesisMod'`), because its code named enRAGE's types throughout. Now enRAGE's people are held behind thin handles of our own and every read or write of them goes through one file, compiled only when it's reached; the game's date and the model names, which came from enRAGE's code, are our own (adapted from it, with credit). With enRAGE installed nothing changes: the same values are read and written the same way. Without it the add-on starts, logs that enRAGE isn't running and waits for it, as it did when enRAGE was merely slow to load; running without enRAGE was the next step, below.
- RealNPCs runs without enRAGE (step 2, the same day). With no `NemesisMod.dll` in `scripts` it starts at once standing alone, and when enRAGE is installed but never comes up it starts after the two minutes it always gave it; the log's Ready line and the first notice say so and list what waits for enRAGE (its nemeses and their records, its townsfolk's names, the feeling bars, the grudge meter, interrogation, revives, avengers, its newspaper items, its settings page). Everything of RealNPCs' own runs: the townsfolk and regulars, ties, memories, street scenes, the mind in shadow, letters and diaries, the ledger, stalkers, the test cast. The towns and regions come from the game's own map zones through a table of our own, so the ticker, the thoughts and the scenes know where they are without enRAGE. Word of mouth and diary entries bound for one of enRAGE's people wait, and are not lost, until enRAGE is back; the dead and the romances on enRAGE's roster are left alone meanwhile, so installing enRAGE later finds its people as RealNPCs last saw them. The readmes now say the add-on works best with enRAGE and runs without it. The town regulars wear their own clothes standing alone (the first run in game, 2026-10-29 14:32, left them undressed: the dressing waited on asking enRAGE whom it had looked at, and with no enRAGE the answer is nobody); and the F7 notice counts RealNPCs' own townsfolk and regulars where it said "0 people in the world" (the count was enRAGE's roster); and a town regular whose body streams out of the world stays away three minutes before another body may be theirs (standing alone every body may be cast, and one regular came back 36 times in an hour, hopping from body to body).

### Two players' reports (Lex, 2026-10-28)

- The dead tell nothing. A man interrogated and then killed went on "talking about how he was questioned" for hours in the RealNPCs box, and his kin came after you for it, as if he were alive: enRAGE's people sync had missed the death (the player rode off a second later), so on its books he lived, and the word of mouth he had set going was still in flight. A rumour or a letter is now dropped at delivery when its teller is dead on RealNPCs' own ledger as well as on enRAGE's books (`WORD dropped:` in the log), and a telling or a diary entry due from such a man never starts; a man enRAGE itself presumed dead after that and means to bring back is left to enRAGE, as before. His kin's grief and any avenger were real: he died by your hand. What this doesn't reach: enRAGE still has him alive on its roster, so it may send him back into the world as a nemesis, walking and thinking; mending that means booking his death into enRAGE when RealNPCs' ledger saw it, a write Lex decides on.
- Avengers come one at a time. `[World] AvengersAtOnce` (2) caps how many of RealNPCs' avengers are riding, arrived, or still alive on enRAGE's roster, and `AvengerGapHours` (48) keeps that many game hours between one arrival and the next; past the cap the relative grieves at home (`AVENGER held:` in the log). Settings version 91. The "somebody wants revenge every 30 seconds" notices are mostly enRAGE's own: it tells every kin and friend on its roster of a death you caused, each with a grudge notice, and the angry and the loyal among them set out to avenge it; a gang's men are each other's friends, so a camp cleared is a dozen notices. Those are enRAGE's to settle: it shows them only at its message level 3, so `Messages = 2` under `[Menu]` in NemesisMod.ini (or `GrudgeMessages = false` there, when no Messages line is set) turns them off.
- Show minds is off as it comes (Lex, 2026-10-28; on since 0.6.3). The Testing page's Switches and Ctrl+F2 turn the cards on; an ini that already says on keeps it, since the setting is read as it stands. The 0.6.3 migration that switched old inis on is gone with it.
- Kin on the street, made to arrive (Lex, from play: "there would be no npc with that name around at all"). The "X is walking toward you" notice is gone: it named a relative of someone you know the moment a passer-by was sent your way, on a body nothing marked, and the walk order mostly ran out before they reached you (eight of eight in one session). Now the body is a passer-by in view within 18 m whom RealNPCs may direct and who carries no name of their own, the walk order is sent again while they aren't getting nearer, and they give up after 30 seconds; one relative a town a game day and the same one once a week, never in the first two minutes after a start or a load. They're named when they reach you and speak. F7 `TEST kin:` says how many reached you; the Testing page's People group has a row for it. The morning after: in Valentine every passer-by was in one of the game's walking scenarios and the walk-up found nobody; now someone in one is let go of it first and sent over, and a refusal says what everyone in range was doing. Later the same morning: in a well-played town enRAGE has named most walkers by their looks, so the street often has nobody free; now the relative is made out of sight behind you instead and let go to the game once they have spoken (`[World] CastRelativesSpawn`, on), and the relative is chosen to fit the sex the street can supply.
- "Him. I've spoken to them before" on a first meeting: enRAGE knows its townsfolk by their look and passes "met" between bodies that share one, so a stranger in another man's clothes was "met" the moment he was seen. A townsperson met by look alone now says only that the face seems known ("Seen him before, or someone the spit of him."); the spoken lines wait for a talk of your own. And a town regular counts as met again only after six game hours away: before, a new body for the same regular in the same visit, or a look a few minutes later, counted as another meeting, so "I know that face" came in the first hour.

### Small things from the in-game pass (2026-10-29)

- The street is livelier: at most one street scene a minute (`[Scenes] DramaGapSeconds` 60, was 90; an ini on 90 is moved at version 101) and a chance meeting between two people with no reason of their own, where the songs, tall tales, haggling, small talk and gossip come from, waits one and a half times that instead of three (Lex: "these NPC interactions happening pretty frequently to make the world feel lively").
- A name card says "Stranger" under the name until you have greeted, talked with, flirted with or otherwise dealt with the person, or they saw you do something; the temper and trade come after (Lex: "it should show Stranger until the main character has greeted or interacted with the NPC").
- A thief lifts your money: anywhere there are people (a town, a camp, a road), a man may walk into you, beg your pardon and take a little of your cash (a fifth, $1 to $25), then run. Catch him (lasso, hogtie, tackle) and you get it back; a lawman near may chase him down and hand it back; if he gets away it's gone (Lex, after the game's own pickpocket bumped him in a gun store). The thief shows as a red dot on the map, as the game's own pickpockets do, and he's fair game: catching him, beating him or worse brings no bounty (a street pickpocket too). `[Scenes] Lifts`; the Testing page's Encounters row "A man bumps into you and lifts your money"; F7 `TEST lift:`.
- Despair by the gun flows quicker: its clips play half again as fast and the shot comes a moment sooner in the last one (Lex: "the animation took a really long time to play through ... he fired the gun slightly late").
- Name cards show the game's gangs, as enRAGE's did: a gang member's second line starts with his gang ("Lemoyne Raider - Stranger"), and one we have no name for is carded by it ("A Lemoyne Raider"): the O'Driscolls, Lemoyne Raiders, Murfrees, Skinner Brothers, Laramies, Del Lobos, Night Folk, Bronte's men, the Grays and the Braithwaites' men, the Wapiti, soldiers and Pinkertons, by the word in their model's name (seen in game: two Raiders in the Valentine saloon).
- Pickpockets work the streets by day about three times as often as before (Lex: "an exciting feature to show case").
- A lawman runs a pickpocket down: when one of RealNPCs' pickpockets runs and a lawman is near, the lawman gives chase at a run, brings him down within arm's reach and holds him while the one robbed thanks the deputy (Lex, after watching the game's own police tackle a man in Saint Denis). `[Scenes] LawChases`; F7 `TEST law chase:`; the Street scenes row for the pickpocket starts one.
- The world feeling unreal is for the awake only: people who haven't begun to wake no longer think or mutter that something is wrong with the world (Lex: "West World type thoughts don't show for non-awake NPCs"); the awake have four times as many thoughts at each stage (30 a stage, from 8), and an odd look at you ("Doesn't blink enough.") was reworded so it doesn't read as uncanny. The doctor's patient walks home at an ordinary pace after the visit.
- The test cast come 10 m in front of you when brought from the Testing page (were out of sight behind you; Lex). Coming back by themselves, they still come out of sight.
- People walk briskly on their errands (to a scene, to the doctor, to a talk, to a camp seat, home again): `[Scenes] ErrandPace` 2 (1 an ordinary walk, 3 a run), about double the old pace (Lex). The stalker and the awakened keep their own pace.
- The readmes say RealNPCs no longer needs enRAGE and stays compatible with it (Lex).
- People who know each other say so: friends always greet each other in their own voices as they pass, acquaintances most of the time; the other answers, and as they go on they part with a word each ("Watch the road home." "And you."). The words show on the ticker within 30 m, in RealNPCs' own words, never a transcript of the recording (Lex: "NPCs who know each other use their voicelines to say Hi and Goodbye").
- The doctor sees people for their own complaints now (Lex: "instead of bringing their child in, NPCs can go to the doctor and try to interact"): someone near you walks to the doctor for a bad tooth, a back, a cut gone bad, a cough, ribs after a fight, nerves, a stomach, failing eyes, or a child's fever when that is their worry, by their age, trade and what has happened to them; at the doctor they say what's wrong and he answers, both aloud in their own voices and on the ticker, and they walk home thinking it over. Saint Denis's doctor counts now, beside Valentine's and Rhodes's. The Testing row "Someone walks to the doctor and tells him what ails them" takes the nearest named person.
- A street scene within 30 m is followed on the ticker: each line shows on the bar as it is said, with the speaker's name (`[Ticker] ScenesOnTicker`, `ScenesOnTickerMetres`). Lex, from the pass: with the game's own subtitles on, RealNPCs' subtitle box stays off, so a woman stepping in on a fight nine metres off went unseen.
- The settings panel opens with Alt+R, and Alt+F as the second key (it was Ctrl+F7 and Alt+F; an ini still on the old keys is moved, settings version 102; a key of your own is kept). The test cast tell a member knocked out in a brawl from one dead, as the ledger does, so the bully comes back after a fistfight instead of being replaced. The log says why a talk told nothing on (`MIND told nothing: ...`), for the memory check.

### The mind sees your shoves (2026-10-29, from the in-game pass)

- A shove of yours now counts: the game raises no damage or crime event for one, so the mind had never seen the player shove anybody, and no "assault" deed had ever been noted. Now a shove is read from the one thing the game shows of it, the shoved one going into a ragdoll right beside you (nothing else marks it: no event, no melee target, no flag), and it makes a light deed, `shove`, with the victim known: he feels the shove, whoever saw it feels the slight, word of it goes round lightly ("shoved a townsman out of his way"), and the man you shoved counts as someone you started on if he fights back. `SHOVE a townsman (Abe Cobb), 6 near` in the log. Never during a fight you are already in.

### Fixes from a review of the day's work

- A hand-edited controller binding of `+` alone stopped RealNPCs starting (nothing in the log); it's refused with a reason now, and no bad setting can stop the start.
- The test cast fit their parts for the tests but not for the mind (a cached person kept the age, faith and temper from before the fitting), and a member whose build never streamed in was kept in the save beside the new one for good.
- Show minds in a crowd allocated memory every frame while placing the cards; the mind's resident lookup could take a stranger for a bound resident for a few seconds after a handle was reused.
- The supper invitation: a fault before the money step no longer tells you your pockets were turned out, and a host nobody came to wanders off instead of standing rooted.
- A lawman standing by hushes anger and contempt on people's faces (the rule was written but never read); F7's supper, test cast and controller lines can't cost the lines after them.
- A real-voice talk cut short was remembered as small talk had; only one that ran its course is.

### Manners of 1899

- A friend is someone you've had two scenes with, or warmth enough; one hand up or one round bought no longer makes a man who grieves you for three weeks, drinks over you, condoles and takes up your grudges.
- A respectable woman doesn't drink herself drunk in a saloon, stand the house a round, or spit; the saloon's own women may. A woman who steps in on a bully shames him, and he answers her as a lady.
- Strangers aren't on first-name terms: in a scene between people with no tie and no town in common, it's "mister" and "ma'am".
- People think of a named person as he or she, not they; "You're welcome", "Take care" and "I'll be seeing you" are gone; a friend's condolence doesn't set a place at the table.
- The old men have twelve talks and the sweethearts eight quarrels, so no two pairs repeat word for word.

### Strangers at a campfire

- At night out of the towns, two or three men sit round a fire off the road and one calls you over: sit a spell on foot and he tells a tale over coffee, the others chiming in; ride on and he mutters; a gun on them and they beg off. Now and then a traveller walks in from the dark and is made room for. `[Encounters] Campfires` (on), `CampfireChance` (15); the Testing row `campfire`.

### The voice below the floor (West World)

- Late at night in a saloon, now and then, a muffled plea comes up through the boards behind the bar. The barkeep says it's the pipes; his thoughts say otherwise. Stand over the boards and it stops. Next morning the town has a word of it. Nothing is ever found. `[Encounters] VoiceBelow` (on, with West World); the Testing row `below`.

### The doctor's patient

- By day in a town, someone whose child has the fever walks to the doctor's and back, thinking it over on the way (hope going, the doctor's word inside, what's left of it coming home). `[Scenes] DoctorsPatient` (on); the Testing row `patient`.

### The quarrel ladder, played (with [Mind] MindLive)

- With `[Mind] MindLive` on, an argument or a fight between two people the mind knows no longer runs on a fixed script: the quarrel ladder (words, a shove, blows, a gun drawn) decides how far it climbs and how it comes down, from the two natures and the surroundings: the hour and the place, drink, the guns on their belts, the law in sight, a friend of either close enough to step in (he does, as the scene's third), the crowd watching, their history and who was wronged, and what it's over (a debt is money, a feud honour; a saloon's drink; the street's a look over nothing). It ends by an apology, a joke, backing down, walking off, a friend's word, a drink stood in a saloon, a man down, or the law looking on. Guns come out only where they did before (both armed, no law near, out of the saloon); the shot is never fired: the standoff breaks off and the log says the ladder called for it. `[Mind] QuarrelLadder` (on, under MindLive); off, or with MindLive off, exactly the fixed scenes. The log's `LADDER` line gives the whole run; F7 `TEST ladder:`. The Testing page's Street scenes row "A quarrel from the ladder (turns: words, blows)" fires one on demand (an argument from words, then a brawl from the blows), needing only `[Mind] MindShadow`.

### The language layer, the game half (2026-10-29, after Lex approved the GPU setup)

- `[Mind] Language` (off, in testing; with MindLive): the mind's own thoughts worded by a local language model running on your own PC through Ollama (`LanguageUrl` http://127.0.0.1:11434, `LanguageModel` llama3.1:8b; nothing leaves the machine). When a feeling is kept for someone near you the request for them is asked at once on a background thread, every answer goes through the check (the period, the names, the length in words and characters, the frame, no copy of the written line or of the brief's own examples), and a line that passes is kept by its situation, so when the thought comes the model's line shows if one is ready and the written line otherwise, which is then asked for. The Testing row "Ask the local model for the nearest known person's thought" (People) asks whatever the setting, the answer to the log. F7 `TEST language:` (asked, answered and how fast, held by the check and why, kept, shown from the model or the written line, the server's state). Off, nothing runs.

### Thoughts that vary (Lex, 2026-10-29)

- "I see a lot of the same types of thoughts": in the log the sameness was the same frame worn by different people ("Next word from X gets answered with my hands" four times over, "X. We've spoken." six) and worries 42% of everything shown. Two rules now, at the one place every thought passes: no two people near you think the same frame (the line with its names stripped) within an hour, whoever thinks it; and a kind of thought that has had the last few turns gives way to the others (the weather, the place, the trade, the small day), so a town doesn't read as one worried chorus. The thoughts that come before the choice (the world feeling unreal, what one of enRAGE's townsfolk feels about you) keep the same rule. `[Mind] ThoughtVariety` (on); off, the old choice. Offline, two hours in a town of twenty: frames back within the hour 16 to 0, distinct lines 61 to 72. F7 `TEST thought variety:`.

### The mind in shadow (continued)

- `[Mind] MindLevels` (off, in testing): the mind's work spread by distance and a budget a frame: the people in view every look, the town around every 10-30 s, everyone saved elsewhere once a game day (counted only: `MIND away:`), and the town around has its choices worked out too, without a line; off, exactly as before. F7 `TEST mind levels:`.
- What each person believes (still only reported): nine beliefs a person, from who they are and what they've lived through (the world as it seems, the law for folk like me, a decent place, that stranger dangerous or kind or a liar, an honest man, I can look after myself, somebody here), moved a little by each thing they feel and flipped only past a threshold, or on one event for a death seen or a buried man walking past; with `[Mind] MindLive` on, a memory that goes against a firm belief is read the way the believer needs it (his own blows deserved, your kindness a trick, your violence provoked, a death the work of outsiders) by those who rewrite rather than own it; the log says when one flips (`MIND believes:`) and where it disagrees with the Awakening or with how they regard you. Nothing acts on it yet. F7 `TEST mind beliefs:`.
- The language layer (no model yet): the request built from a person's whole state, a cache key per person and situation, and the fallback rule (a late or failed answer gives the written line); fifty saved requests for the day a local model is wired; the Testing row `mind:request` writes the request the model would get for the nearest known person to the log, from the mind's own state (nothing is asked of any model).
- With `[Mind] MindLive` on, the one spoken to in a street scene answers with their body from their own mind: the line as an event, felt by their nature, shown by the manners of 1899 (a proud woman mocked in the street keeps it off her face; a hothead threatened squares up; a cold man takes a cheer with nothing) through the same gestures as before; a neighbour's word is taken as a neighbour's, and a feeling the face hides holds the body still a moment. Off, exactly as before.
- The Testing page's test cast, once made and kept in the save, come back by themselves out of sight when you come to their town (`[Testing] TestCastReturns`; off as it comes since the morning after, when Lex found them turning up at every reload: the cast come only on the Testing row's key, and an ini that said on is set off once).
- What each person would do now (still only reported): once a minute, every person near you weighs what's open to them here and now (work, a meal, rest, home, the saloon of an evening, the store, church on Sunday, a word with a friend in sight, stepping clear of someone they can't abide, keeping clear of you...) against their needs, their nature, their mood and any feeling still running, and the log says what they'd do and why when it changes (`MIND would:`), with "but enRAGE has him" where enRAGE has the person. A thing just done serves its need less for a while, so nobody shows off all afternoon. Nobody is moved by it yet. F7 `TEST mind choices:`; the mind card says `would:`.
- A debt called in and a brush-off reach the mind in shadow: the debtor feels it called in (shame, guilt or fear by his nature) and the one brushed off takes the slight, now the scene says who did what (the creditor, the one who turned away); before, both mapped to nothing.
- The man you assault appraises it himself: an assault's victim (the person you hurt last, when RealNPCs already knows him) is on the deed and its record, so his `MIND` line shows his own anger or fear; being robbed raises someone's wariness as well as their money worry. An execution on the road is a killing seen for whoever watched it (and a rescue for the captive you saved); the night visitor is the uncanny for anyone who saw it. Good news shared is felt by the one who has it and by a friend glad with him; the man who stands a round feels the room's regard; the one left lying after a fall feels the fall before the street; the giver of a coin and the man who sends a beggar off feel the asking (pity or contempt, by their nature).
- Memory that behaves like memory (behind `[Mind] MindLive`): when two people part after a talk, one tells the other the freshest thing worth telling, and the hearer keeps it as something heard from them, less sure (a hearer below the teller, and the teller surer with each telling), and told things go first when the twelve a person are full; a nod with a word in passing tells it too, one way, from the one who greets, some of the time and half-heard. What was heard drifts the way memory does: the unnamed doer becomes someone the hearer already dislikes, a slight told on grows into a quarrel or a fight, two men become three; what someone saw or did never changes who did it, and what you did never drifts (the town's rumours grow those). A memory long past keeps its kind and feeling and loses strangers' names, never a friend's or the dead's. Places hold memories: standing where something happened brings it back ("Right here. This is where it happened."). Thoughts from what was heard name the teller ("Abe says Earl and Hollis came to fists over something."). Nothing new is saved for it but a few optional fields on the memories already saved; an old save loads as before. Off (as it comes), memories are exactly as in 0.6.3.
- Thoughts from feelings, filled out (behind `[Mind] MindLive`): twelve lines for each feeling and whom it's about (was three), in different forms (an observation, a judgment, self-talk, a question, a plan, a regret, one thing bringing back another); when the face hides the feeling the thought says the mask too ("Smile. Let him think it didn't land."); a pressing need in the words of the trade and temper where it tells (a farmhand's hunger isn't a clerk's; a coward's fear for himself isn't a bold man's), ten lines each and four more a trade or temper; the mask said eight ways a feeling instead of four; he or she of a named friend, never he for a woman. 698 lines in all, every one checked for period words.
- Memories worded more ways (with MindLive on or off): every pool of memory lines has at least four (1,012 lines, from 522; 243 pools had two), so the same brawl, slight, round or death isn't thought of in the same two sentences all week; a coward's and a hothead's lines still never meet. The weather, the place they're standing in (the barber's, the bath house, the bank, the station...) and the people in sight have four thoughts or more each as well (53 new); what people say they do for a living when you talk to them has four answers a trade instead of two (forty trades, 80 new), and a friend taking up a wrong names it four ways; the small thoughts that fill a quiet day (the hour, an ache, how they think they look, the age pressing in, the face they keep) and what they make of you on sight have half again as many lines (42 new); every worry's crisis, its good and bad endings and the weeks after have three thoughts each instead of one or two (96 new over the fourteen kinds); the pools that had a single line (what a person says of a debt paid or a mule taken, a neighbour made up with or not, a yes or a no; the town's talk of a dead sweetheart; a keeper's view of the man who shot him; the mood a sentence is said in) have three (58 new); the weather out of doors and every shop a person stands in have six thoughts each (48 new); what a townsperson makes of you on sight, armed, mounted or back in town yet again, and the aches of their own body, a few more each (18 new). The morning after the run, another 152 lines in 103 pools where people still repeated themselves: bystanders' remarks, the twelve towns' own lines, the nods and looks between people who know each other, the posts' keepers, the drinkers, the exchanges (the owed, the neighbour and the courted now have four each).

## 0.6.3

Built on 2026-10-26, overnight and through the day and night of 2026-10-27 (Lex's briefs), with play-tests between. Seen working in game by the release: the execution on the road, the Testing page and its test cast, the street scenes (the brawl with real punches, someone standing up to a bully, the fall and a hand up, the lost child, the old days, the preacher, the round's words), the mind cards, despair's farewell, the stalker, the awake running at a wall, the wake stages over heads. Not yet seen in game: the controller buttons, the supper invitation (off by default), the callout, the Sabbath, the bell, the night visitor's and the traveller's newest changes, and from 2026-10-26 the "Hook up" prompt, the CasualHookups fallback and the body-watch witness.

### The mind underneath, in shadow (being tested)

- A mind under every person near you, run beside the game and only reported while it's tested (`[Mind] MindShadow`, on; `[Mind] MindLive`, off, lets it reach the screen later; settings version 80). First, traits: each person has fourteen, worked out from who they are and never saved: five broad ones with two sides each (curiosity and imagination, order and self-control, sociability and assertiveness, compassion and trust, anxiety and anger), honesty, piety, respect for the law and an appetite for risk. Their temper still stands (a hothead reads as a hothead), their faith counts, a trade and the years pull a little (people grow steadier and warier of risk with age), and enRAGE's own people bring the ten traits enRAGE gave them. The log says each person's once (`MIND traits: Earl Adair (resident, Proud): outspoken and boastful (reads as Proud); ...`), F7 sums it up (`TEST mind shadow:`), and the Show minds card has a last line, "mind: ...". For someone enRAGE gave no temper, the same line says which RealNPCs would give ("share (dry run)"); nothing is written to enRAGE. A person stays the same person: their traits come from who they are, not a fresh roll each time. It reads only people RealNPCs already knows, never makes anyone up, and saves nothing.

- Needs and worries next (still only reported): each person near you has nine needs worked out from the hour, the weather, where they are, their trade and money, what happened to them lately and who they are (hunger, rest, warmth and dry, money for the week, safety, company, standing, meaning, and a vice's pull for those with one), and up to three live concerns (the worry they carry, someone else's worry they're part of, an enRAGE record's goals). The `MIND traits` line ends with them (`needs: hungry and tired (...); worried about the bank's note (the crisis)`), as do F7 and the Show minds card.

- And what they feel (still only reported): when something happens near you that RealNPCs records (a street scene, something you did, a death), each person in it or who saw it, whom the mind knows, judges it for themselves: how bad it is for them, whose doing it was and whether it was meant, whether they can stand up to it, who saw, and whom it happened to. That names one feeling (fear, anger, shame, guilt, grief, pride, gratitude, envy, pity, contempt, relief or joy), how strong, and what it makes them want to do, and their mood follows it for a few hours. A hothead shoved at the bar is angry and wants to have it out; a timid clerk is afraid and wants away; in a big crowd at a fight, somebody nearly always steps in, though each one is less likely to. The log has a `MIND` line for each (`MIND Earl Adair (Proud): anger (strong) at you, urge confront <- scene:rough ...; mood sore and keyed up`), F7 and the Show minds card say how each person near feels. With `[Mind] MindLive` on, the sore or glad moods the rest of RealNPCs uses come from this mind instead of the old rule.

- And what shows of it (still only reported): the rules of 1899 decide how much of a feeling reaches the face. A man hides fear before other men (a timid one can't quite, and in a saloon he blusters), a woman of standing keeps her anger off her face in the street, nobody shows anger in church or before the law, drink loosens it all, and the coldest keep almost everything in. The thought always says what's really felt. Each `MIND` line says what shows and the first thing the game is proven able to do to show it (a gesture, a scenario, a recorded line), from a new catalogue of what has really played in game.

- With `[Mind] MindLive` on, what people feel comes first in their thoughts: someone you shoved a minute ago thinks of you, in words of how they really feel about it (even when their face hides it), and a body's pressing want (hunger, tiredness, rain down the collar when it rains) speaks up now and then. A friend's feelings name the friend; a stranger who shares the name never does. The same line never comes twice in a game day. Off (the default), thoughts are exactly as before.

### The supper invitation

- At dusk on the roads, out of the towns, a too-friendly man may wave you down and ask you to supper at his place, just off the road (`[Encounters] SupperInvitation`, off until it's been tested: turn it on to meet him; `SupperChance`, 12% a roll, half past five to nine in the evening, once a day; settings version 82). His thought over his head gives him away. Ride on, draw on him or antagonize him and he lets you go. Greet him, or stop by him a moment, and he leads you off the road; follow him there on foot and the screen goes dark. You come round in a ditch at first light, a little way off, your head splitting and most of your cash gone (never more than $250). That can't be undone, so the encounter is rare and has its own switch, and his thought (the ticker must be on) gives him away first. Its Testing row takes no money, moves no clock and saves nothing.

### The street preacher

- A devout townsperson may preach in the street on any day now, between nine and six, not only on Sunday afternoons (Lex). It's a tenth as likely on a weekday as on a Sunday afternoon, still once a town a day at most, and the same person no more than every two days. On a weekday the sermon doesn't mention the Sabbath.
- The one who preaches is the devout first, then a churchgoer who would, nearest first, and the first of them with someone near enough to hear; on the Testing page anyone may, but in the same order, so the test cast's devout preacher is the one when he's about (Lex: a test took whoever was nearest, and kept refusing on one man nobody stood near).

### Despair's voice

- Someone who can't bear it no longer sounds like a hostage (Lex: pleading to be untied, crying for help, "leave me alone"), nor like someone saying random things. The game has no lines for this, and each voice records its own words, so for now they say only a farewell as it begins (in their own voice, by their nature), and the rest of the act is quiet, their thoughts over their head. With poison, the pain and the moment of wanting it back are voiced too. The waking act before it never has them begging to be untied. Lines chosen for each voice from what it really says come later.

### The settings panel

- The world slows while the settings panel is open, as it does while the weapon wheel or the satchel is open (`[Output] PanelSlowsTime`, on; `PanelTimeScale`, 0.25: a quarter speed; settings version 84). Never during a story mission.

### Show minds

- Ctrl+F2 turns the cards over people near you off and on for the session (`[Testing] TestShowMindsKey`; an ini with no key for it gets Ctrl+F2; settings version 83). The settings panel's Testing page has the switch that's saved. On as it comes.
- In a crowd the cards no longer pile up (Lex's screenshot, four over the bar): the nearest person has the full card, everyone else one line (name, temper, mood), and a card that would cross another lifts itself above it, nearer people placed first.
- The card sits clear above the thought over the same head (it could cover the thought's first line): it keeps room for a two-line thought always, so it doesn't jump as thoughts come and go, and rises above a longer one while it shows.

### The test cast

- The Testing page has "Bring the test cast here": eight people the tests are often short of come and stroll about a spot out of your sight, 14-26 m behind you (further out if that's all there is, and in a narrow street with nowhere hidden, in the open near you). They are a woman of a mother's age (the lost child), two old men (the old days), a devout man and a churchgoing woman (the street preacher and the one who answers him), and three men for a bully: a hothead, the man he picks on, and a loyal man who stands up to him. Each is a person like any other, with a name, a temper and a mind, and is kept in the save for testing: the same people come each time, in their own clothes, and anyone who died is replaced by someone new. They live as ordinary people, not only for the tests: they take part in anything a townsperson can, make friends and enemies, and may court and marry. enRAGE may take one of them for a townsperson of its own who looks the same; RealNPCs always knows them by their own names. They're never taken for a town regular, someone's worry, an execution or a gathering, and never dropped from the save to make room. A grey mark on the map shows each one for a minute. They go when you're 250 m off, when a mission starts, when a save loads and when RealNPCs stops (bring them again after a reload); the old days, preacher and lost child tests say so when they refuse for want of someone and the cast isn't about; "Let the test cast go" takes them away now (they stay in the save). Log `TESTCAST`; F7 `TEST test cast:` (who's here, what each is free for, and whether enRAGE has taken any of them for one of its townsfolk). Settings version 85 (the two Testing keys). Seen in game (2026-10-28): all eight came and fit their parts. Not built: the cast coming back by themselves when you return to their town.

### Seen in game, left for after the release

- The round bought: the call and the cheers are voiced, but nothing moves (no glass raised, no turn to the bar; the cheer gestures weren't seen). Acting it out is roadmap 33.

### A livelier street

- Brawls between strangers: two hot-blooded men in each other's way (a hothead or a proud man, and another such or a cold one) come to blows now and then, 35% of their meetings in a saloon and 15% in the street, by day or night (before, a reason like a worry always came first, so the chance meeting's brawl seldom got a turn; Lex found no fights in half an hour). Drink and temper in a saloon come to blows at any hour, not only at night (Lex: tavern fights by day too).
- Fights between strangers reach ordinary men too: two hot-blooded men at the full chance, one hot man at half, two ordinary men at a quarter (never a coward), all scaled by the weight below. Without that, at the highest weight in a thin town there were no fights: two hot men together were rare, so the weight had no pair to work on.
- `[Scenes] TroubleWeight` (1 as built; 0-5; on the Street scenes page): how often people come to blows or quarrel. 2 is twice as often, 0.5 half, 0 never by chance. It scales the brawl's and the argument's share of a chance meeting, hot blood between strangers, drink and temper, a hothead's run-in with a stranger, and a snap that comes to blows. Over 1 it also shortens everyone's rest between scenes (two minutes, fifteen after a fight, divided by the weight), since in a thin town everyone near has just been in one. Later (roadmap 30) the minds will decide this themselves.

- People deal with each other around you more often (Lex: brawls are exciting, and not far off the time). A street scene can come every minute and a half instead of every two and a half (`[World] DramaGapSeconds` 90; an ini still on the old 150 gets the new one, one of your own is kept; settings version 87). Brawls come up twice as often when two men meet by chance; a hothead at the worst of his trouble comes to blows more often, and so does drink and temper in a saloon at night. Someone stands up to a bully more often (a friend nearly always, a loyal or proud stranger half the time), with less rest between. Falls, rounds bought and grudges taken up come about twice as often. The rare moments keep their own pace (the lost child, the preacher, the noon callout, the Sabbath, the bell).

### Subtitles

- The game draws its own plain white subtitle at the bottom for any recorded line while its Subtitles option is on ("I don't want no trouble, mister"), and a mod can't stop or move it, so two sets showed at once. `[Output] Subtitles` is now a choice: **game** (the default): RealNPCs' boxed subtitles only while the game's option is off, so one set shows at a time; **ours**: RealNPCs' always (switch the game's Subtitles off to see only these); **both**; **off**. An ini's old "on" reads as game. Settings version 88. The `SUBTITLES:` log line and F7 say which set shows.

### Controllers

- RealNPCs' keys on a controller (Lex): hold one button and press another. By default RB with D-pad up opens the settings panel (its Testing page fires everything with A), RB with D-pad left is the F7 status, and RB with D-pad right your letters; the ticker's next line has none. Set them from the controller on the settings panel's Hotkeys page (A, then hold one button and press another; B keeps it, Y leaves it with none), or in `[Controller]` in the ini, where PlayStation names work too (R1+Up). Only in play, never in the game's own menus or the pause menu, and the held button must be down a moment first, so a quick tap of RB for cover doesn't count. The letters turn with the D-pad and close with B. `PadKeys` switches it all off. Log `PAD`; F7 `TEST controller:` (which buttons the game answered, the last one used). Settings version 86. Not yet seen in game.

### Fights in street scenes

- Two men sitting or leaning when a scene's fight came got up and never swung, and the scene went on to "That's for the weights" as if they had (Lex, testing someone standing up to a bully). The fight now waits for both to be on their feet (one still in his seat after 3 seconds is brought out of it at once), gives the order again every 2 seconds, and its time starts only when they're at it. If they never come to blows within 12 seconds the scene stops there, rather than carrying on as if they had. They're made to fight with their fists the way enRAGE makes its own people (fight unarmed, always fight, never run from it), and given their own ways back when the scene ends: without that, two townsmen told to fight stood in "combat" and never swung (Lex's second try). Seen in game: the punches fly. The Testing page's "Someone stands up to a bully" takes turns: the fight goes on the first time, the bully backs off the next (a plain bully backs off three times in four, so it seldom showed the fight). Log `SCENE fight: at it N s after the word`, or `SCENE stopped ...: they never came to blows`.

### Groundwork for choosing what to do (nothing in game yet)

- How people will choose what to do next when nothing has just happened to them: what it does for their most pressing needs, how it suits who they are and their mood, less the effort and risk and what they did a moment ago, with a little chance for the impulsive; nothing improper for the place and hour (no cards on a Sunday morning, no drink in church); a strong feeling goes ahead of any of it.

### Groundwork for memory (nothing in game yet)

- How people will remember what they saw and pass it on: a memory fades by how much it mattered, fast at first and then slower and slower (a scuffle seen is gone in about three days; a killing seen is still there, faintly, a year on, and longer if people keep talking about it), and when friends meet the strongest news is told on, a little less sure and with names lost at each telling, until "Earl Platt knocked Hollis down" becomes "somebody knocked Hollis down".

### Groundwork for quarrels that go further (nothing in game yet)

- How a quarrel climbs or comes down, in code only: words, a shove, blows, a gun drawn, a shot, with the hotter of the two pushing up by his nature, his drink, what it's over, the crowd and the history between them, and the cooler bringing it down with an apology, a joke, a drink, by backing down or walking off, or a friend stepping in; the law at hand and a town's gun rule hold the law-abiding back from a gun; nobody draws what he doesn't carry. Over twenty thousand quarrels two in three end in words, one in seven in a fight, a gun comes out in under one of a hundred and a shot in one of four hundred; drunk armed men with history in a saloon at night come to a shot one time in twenty, as they did. Nobody draws on an unarmed man lightly, a woman's quarrel stops at the shove, the one wronged pushes harder, and the law in sight breaks up a fight.

### Groundwork for the language layer (nothing in game yet)

- What a local language model would be asked about a person (their nature, mood, what they feel and about whom, what shows on their face, their worry, a memory, the place and the hour) and the checks every answer must pass before anyone would see it: short, plain, no modern words, no names it wasn't given, never breaking the frame. Nothing loads a model or connects anywhere; the written lines stay as they are.

### Lines

- Every line in RealNPCs (about 5,600) checked for words too modern for 1899, for the same line turning up in two places, and for overused openings. Reworded: the two sarcastic "Must be nice"s, a grieving thought that repeated a memory line, a remark that repeated a plea, and a courtship reply that repeated your own flirt.

### Fixes from a review of the overnight work

- Your save is never written over with an empty one at load. If something else had the save file open when the game loaded (OneDrive syncing Documents, a virus scanner, an editor), RealNPCs took it for a damaged file, started fresh and saved that over your real file twenty seconds later. Now it waits a moment, then leaves the file alone and saves nothing for that session (the log and F7 say so); a damaged file is kept as a dated copy, and the copy a crash leaves behind mid-save is recovered.
- One of enRAGE's people who died by RealNPCs' doing can no longer be written dead into enRAGE after you load a save: anything still waiting when a loading screen comes, or the clock jumps, is left to enRAGE.
- Despair: lassoing or hogtying someone in their last moments stops it; shooting them yourself is your doing, not "by their own hand" (nor in enRAGE's history of a record); the shot heard is fired at the ground by their feet, so no stray round flies past; someone let be at the last moment keeps the orders enRAGE just gave them; a survivor puts their gun away. Four of the calm-before-the-end lines read as relief and are reworded, and the one they're closest to is a real friend (one hand up no longer makes someone that).
- People know only what they could: a friend takes up a wrong once they saw it or two hours have passed, and standing up to a bully is no wrong to avenge; a friend offers sympathy only for a death they've heard of; the town talks of the night visitor's body only if someone besides you saw it.
- A mother asking after her lost child is asking after her youngest, never the child the fever took, and not every week. A drinker mourning a child, a son or a black-edged letter mourns; it's no longer "bad luck". A coward never calls a man out at noon.
- Smaller: greetings in passing had who greeted whom backwards; falls never happen under an awning, so nobody is helped "off the boards"; the traveller no longer hails "You, on the horse!" when you're on foot; the old men's dance of '68 is thirty years back in 1899; shutting down stops a grudge walk, drunk voices and a waking voice; the street scenes' search for people costs less.


### After the first play-test (2026-10-27)

- Every thought shows over the thinker's head (Lex, 2026-10-27), never in a notice or the "Around town" box: F7's look at the nearest of enRAGE's people (it showed as a notice at the top left), a waking person's thoughts as they act, and the thoughts of people who know you (about what you did to them, what they heard, your name). Those are all about you: someone in view is asked first and thinks it over their head; someone far off still shows in the "Around town" box, as before (Lex: far-off thoughts there only when they're about the main character).
- Despair acted out with the game's own clips (Lex: both gun endings; "Poison, yes"). The last words are said sobbing. The gun is put in the hand just before (it used to be drawn the game's way, and someone with no holster could lose it), then one of the two arcs the game has: Jamie's from the story ("I don't want to live", the gun to his head, the shaking, the shot) or a hideout leader's (the wait, the gun up, the shot), the shot timed to a point of the last clip (`WAKE despair: ...'s shot at N% of ...`, to be tuned). A new way, poison, true to 1899 (laudanum and arsenic were the common way for those without a gun, women most of all): a long drink and the fall, or slowly (sick, burning, gone). Who uses which is the person's own: the armed their gun, gun keepers mostly a gun, then poison (women about half the time, men a third), then a wall if one is near, else the knife; the poisoning half and half, by the person. The hideout leader's arc was taken out after Lex saw it twice (he lay down, got up and lay down again before the shot, and the gun wasn't really in his hand: its set animates a prop pistol of its own), so the gun is Jamie's arc every time. A clip that won't play falls back to the way before. A Testing row, "Despair now, the next way in turn", takes the nearest free person RealNPCs knows (3-12 m off; never one of enRAGE's records, a story character or a counter's keeper) straight to it, cycling Jamie's arc, the drink, the slow poison, the knife and the wall. The content note says poison now. After Lex's first two tries: the clips are watched every 50 ms, so the shot comes at its point (it came about 0.45 s late) and the next clip follows straight on (there was a gap of 0.4 s); the first despair of a session sobs too (the set is started as soon as it's in, `WAKE despair: X sobbing, once its set was in`); they die at the shot itself (the bullet never hurt the one firing it, so death came with the limp 1.5 s later and the fall lagged the sound; Lex heard it twice), logged `WAKE despair: X dead at the shot, the trigger pulled`; then 30% of the clip came too late (Lex, once the fall came with it), so the shot goes at the clip's own shot event when it names one (looked up as the clip starts, and watched for as it plays), else at 15%; before that, the log said what the shot did 0.3 s on (`WAKE despair: ...'s shot, 0.3 s on: health N -> N, dead by the gun | still alive: no bullet struck them`), since no gunshot was heard.
- What the despairing say and think (Lex: "more distress dialogue that would match up with a 4/4 awake NPC from a behavioral psychologist perspective"). Before, everyone had the one line, "I'm sorry. I can't be this any more.", and the act was silent. Now 122 lines in eight beats, in the order the study of suicidal crisis describes, worded for someone who knows they're made and kept in a loop: the last words (a farewell, an apology, a warning off), the pain and the trap (every road bends back to this street), being a weight nobody would miss (and the dread of only being put back), the plea (wanting to die and to be stopped at once: said to you when you're within 12 m, a cry you can't answer; when nobody's near, a lonelier one), the calm at the end (the present only, small things seen sharp, still afraid), and for poison what it does to them and a flash of wanting it back.
