Each fits their temperament (the bold, the fearful, the cold, the rest), some name the living person they're closest to, nobody's words are anyone else's in a session, and most beats are voiced with the game's lines for fright, a mutter, a plea or a moan. They come at points of the clips (Jamie's arc: the pain, the weight, the plea, then the calm as the shaking goes on, still up when the shot comes) and show over the person's head, each until the next (the ticker's 12 s turns were too slow for an act of 16); said to you, they're subtitled too when the game plays the voice. Log `WAKE despair: X (beat[, to you]): line`; F7 `TEST despair:` counts them.
- A Testing row, "Gunshots at the ground, six ways", for despair's silent shot: on the nearest free person 2-12 m off, with a revolver in their hand for the run, six shots at the ground a step and a half in front of them (on the side away from you), numbered on screen a few seconds apart: their own gun's trigger, a bullet from their hand (as despair fires it), a bullet from a metre beside them, the same owned by nobody, the trigger with the gun aimed first, and the game's shoot task. Nobody is hurt. Best out of town: people near may run, and the law may come. Lex heard 1, 5 and 6, never 2, 3 or 4: a bullet made in the air is silent, a gun fired by its own trigger is heard. So despair's shot now pulls the gun's own trigger at the head (the old bullet still follows, so it kills even if the trigger's misses), and so does an execution's last bullet when the gunman's fight hasn't killed him in 3 s.
- Two new Testing rows for the despair endings and the stages' body language: "Despair animations, one by one" (25: the game's own suicide arcs from the story, Jamie's and a hideout leader's and the gunslinger's, with a revolver in hand that is never fired; four collapses; grief before the act; a poisoning, only to see) and "Wake stage gestures, one by one" (25, by stage: rubbing the eyes, looking up at the sky, "is this it", the madman's "he's watching" and "I think I'm crazy", beckoning, pleading with open hands). Each plays on the nearest free person, stood up and facing you, numbered at the bottom of the screen, and the log says whether it started, how long it ran and how far it moved them (`WAKE anim #N ...`). All 23 of their sets are in Story Mode (`WAKE anim sets:` in the log); which look right decides what goes into despair and the stages next. The person is borrowed for the run (the game gives them no orders of its own; Lex saw a man go back to his day between clips) and held standing between clips, and a clip that doesn't start is tried once more, with why in the log. The voice probe passes over lines already heard this session, and a second probe ends the first with its summary. Heard in game 2026-10-27: every one of the 32 lines plays out of its context (a Valentine labourer 32 of 32, a saloon girl 30, her voice having no plain hello or "better get going"); a waking moment whose lines a voice hasn't got now gives way to another of the stage, once, instead of a turn in silence.
- The Testing page's keys work like the rest of the panel (Lex: Enter closed the panel instead of setting a key): Enter on a row sets its key, Space fires it (a gamepad's A fires it, since keys are set with the keyboard), Delete leaves it without one, and Left/Right only say so. A key another test already has moves to the new row and leaves that one without a key (it was refused, "[ is taken: the execution"); a test left on its default key counts as having it. One of enRAGE's keys, or one RealNPCs can't do without (F7, the flirt key, the reader key...), is still refused, naming whose it is.
- Up to five encounters at once (Lex; `[Encounters] EncountersAtOnce`, 5, 1-8; settings version 79). The crying woman, an execution, a hermit taken up with you, a gathering, the night visitor, a traveller and a stalker share the count; before, any one of them kept every other away, the stalker included. Each roll (every `EncounterEveryMinutes`) tries the kinds not already out, from a different one each time, and starts one at most, so they come one by one; one of each kind for now. The quiet after a big moment no longer holds encounters back; each kind keeps its own rest (once a night, once a day) and its chance. F7 `TEST encounters:` lists all that are out and the count.
- The waking speak (Lex: odd lines from the game itself, "drastic step ups" from stage to stage; `[WestWorld] WakeVoices`, on; settings version 78). Each stage says the game's own recorded lines in the wrong places, more often and nearer to you each step, with a thought over the head for what they make of it: stirring, a word to nobody now and then ("What a world." to the empty street) and explaining it away; questioning, the same line two or three times in the very same recording, a sense of being watched when you look their way, saying they're leaving and not going; unravelling, their whole stock of words in a rush, a plea to be untied with nothing tying them, the sky told off; awake, lines chosen on purpose and said to you ("It was you, wasn't it?"), which are now the awake act's pleas, ending with a goodbye when despair can't follow. One waking voice at a time; voice, a look and a thought only, so the look-only and enRAGE's people take part too. The Testing page's "Wake voices: every line, one by one" plays each line on the nearest person and logs which the game plays out of its context. The wake scan counts only living people toward the eight nearest (horses and bodies used up the slots), and a look-only act says why (`WAKE act: ... [passive: a mission entity ...]`). F7 `TEST wake voices:`, log `WAKE voice`. The log also lists which of 23 candidate animation sets for the despair endings and the stages' body language this game has (`WAKE anim sets:`), for the next step.
- The stalker key works near a hermit's camp again (Lex pressed ] in Valentine and nobody came): a hermit sitting by his fire out of sight, up to 150 m off, counted as an encounter going on, and stalkers wait for those. Now he stands in the way only while he's taken up with you (watching you come, warning you off, talking, running) or you're within 40 m of his fire.
- Show minds is on as it comes (Lex, 2026-10-27): the small card over named people within 11 m of you (it was 15), with their temper, mood, worry, ties and what is on their mind. An ini that still has it off (the old default) is switched on once; settings version 77. Turn it off on the Testing page or with `[Testing] ShowMinds = false`.
- Wake stages over heads (Testing page, `[Testing] ShowWakeStages`, off as it comes): a small tag over each person within 25 m who has begun to wake, "Stirring (1 of 4)" to "Awake (4 of 4)", coloured from pale to red, with what holds them back (riding, can't be directed now, keeps a counter, had their chance). A thought over the same person sits on top of it. F7 `TEST wake stages:`. Settings version 76. Seen in game: Lex, 2026-10-27.
- Thoughts above heads never fall back to the bar at the bottom any more (Lex saw them there again): a thought whose thinker is out of view is passed over after a moment and the next one comes, and a scene speaker's own line, shown when the recording's words are known, goes over their head too. The bar keeps the news. The wake key says when the one woken is riding (they only look at you until they're on foot).
- The wake key (Y) wakes the person you're locked on to, whoever they are; before, it did so only for enRAGE's records and otherwise woke whoever was nearest. Someone who can't be woken (a child, one of the story's own) is named with the reason.
- F7 marks enRAGE's records within 100 m on the map for a minute, each labelled free or why not, and names the nearest free one (for testing a record's despair or a record as the stalker). Log `RECORDS marked`.
- Flirt in the lock-on menu no longer shows on men while same-sex romance is off (`[Romance] SameSexRomance`): none of them could say yes. Seen by Lex in game, 2026-10-27.
- The frame-time meter no longer reports loading the save as a slow frame (it settles for two seconds after the save loads), and its slow-frame line names the part that really cost the most (it named the last feature to run).
- The save file is about 40% smaller (Lex's went from 1,613 KB to 916 KB): empty, zero and false values are no longer written. Nothing is lost: the file reads back the same, and an older RealNPCs reads the new file as it read the old one. Saving takes a fifth less time.
- No more big stutter once a minute. Lex's log showed one frame of 15-24 ms about once a minute: RealNPCs saving its memory of people. A save now waits for a moment when nobody can see a slow frame (the screen faded out, a load), and saves during play only after a minute with no such moment. Even then the file is written on another thread, so the frame pays only for turning the memory into text (6.6 ms in game, from 15-24). A 36-second pause in Lex's game left no trace in the log and no save, so the pause menu looks to stop RealNPCs altogether; in case some menu keeps it running, a game clock that has stood still for half a second counts as a quiet moment too ("the game clock stopped" in the save's log line). The first save's one-off cost (about 50 ms) is paid while the save loads. F7 `TEST save:` says when the last save happened and what it cost; the log's `SAVE written:` line shows the first of each kind. The frame-time meter no longer warns about slow frames nobody could see. It also times the parts of RealNPCs' main step that ran outside any feature, each on its own ("observe enRAGE's people (step)", "tick04 (step)" and so on): after the save had its own slot, a 15 ms frame every few seconds was still left "outside any feature", and the next F7 will name it. Much of that was .NET compiling RealNPCs' code the first time each part ran in a session (offline: the day's first round of work took 56 ms, 0.6-2 ms after; rumours 4.8 ms, 1.4 after); RealNPCs now compiles its engine ahead on another thread right after the save loads (F7 `TEST perf:` says when it's done; log `PREJIT:`).

### Built overnight (2026-10-27)

- More to think after tonight's kindnesses and unkindnesses: a round bought, a debt paid in the street, a friend's sympathy, good news shared, an apology, a hand up after a fall (yours too), a fall walked past, standing up to a bully and being faced down each have more lines, and lines of their own for the days after (before, some borrowed courting words).
- Ride past a lost traveller (or run him off) and now and then he never arrives: a day or so later the town talks of a stranger on foot who went missing on the road. Not yet seen in game.
- The bell at three (West World): at three in the morning in a town with a church, the people who have begun to wake stop and look toward the church at a bell only they hear, each thinking of it by how far they've come; anyone beside them who isn't waking wonders what they're staring at. You hear nothing. On the Testing page: "The bell at three". Log `BELL`; F7 `TEST bell:`. Not yet seen in game.
- A robbery on the road: about one traveller in five is being held up by two of the region's outlaws when you come on him, hands up, with two guns on him. They warn you to keep going. Step in (a shot, a gun on one of them, or walking right up) and they fight you (self-defence); drive them off and he thanks you. Leave it and they take his money and go, and he tells you so. Either way the nearest town hears of it a few hours later. The Testing page's traveller row now takes turns: lost, snakebit, robbed. Log `TRAVELLER held up by 2 road agents`, `TRAVELLER: you stepped in`. Not yet seen in game.
- A card cheat caught in a saloon is now, half the time when it doesn't come to blows, thrown out: the man he cheated tells him to go, and he walks out, and once he's outside his thought shows over his head. (Before, the cheat always ended by telling the other man to leave.) Not yet seen in game.
- A snakebite on the trail: about one traveller in three on the roads is sitting by the road holding his leg and calls for help instead of asking the way. Greet him, or stop by him a moment, and you see to the bite: he gets up, thanks you and limps off toward town, and a few hours later the town hears a stranger stopped for him. Ride on (or run him off) and he stays where he sat, and half the time the town later hears he was found dead by the road. The Testing page's traveller row now takes turns: lost, then snakebit. Log `TRAVELLER ... snakebit`. Not yet seen in game.
- The Sabbath: on a Sunday morning by the church, now and then one of the devout kneels a while with a prayer of their own (their thought over their head), and anyone lapsed or godless nearby who sees it feels watched. On the Testing page: "Someone kneels by the church". Log `SABBATH`; F7 `TEST sabbath:`. Not yet seen in game.
- A fourteenth worry, the gambler and the rent: a card player or a roughneck (now and then a greedy or drifting working man) has lost at the table and owes his landlady the rent. It runs a week or so like the others: he thinks of it, snaps at whoever speaks to him at its worst, and at the end has paid her or lost his room, and says so. Money trouble like the debts: it can drive him to pick a pocket or ask a stranger for a coin. Because a man's worry is picked from the ones that fit him, card players and roughnecks who were mid-worry may find it changed to this one after the update. Not yet seen in game.
- Condolences in the street: someone who had word of a friend's death in the last three days, meeting a friend of their own, is offered sympathy, and answers by their own nature (thanks, a wish to be let be, or a sharp word). Once for each loss. On the Testing page: "Condolences to someone grieving". Log `CONDOLE`; F7 `TEST condolences:`. Not yet seen in game.
- By day in a town, now and then a woman in the street (of an age and a trade to have one) has lost sight of her child, the same child her other worries are about. She asks whoever is near (a kind soul offers to look, the cold say no, the sharp tell her to watch the child better) or you, if you're close (subtitled), and goes on calling the child's name. An hour or so later the town has word where the child turned up. Nobody is spawned. On the Testing page: "A mother asks around for her lost child". Log `LOST CHILD`; F7 `TEST lost child:`. Not yet seen in game.
- A debt that ends well is paid in the street where folks can see: the money counted out, the one who paid asking for it to be said aloud, and the one owed telling the street they're square. The two think better of each other, and the town talks of it. Not yet seen in game.
- Sweethearts fall out now and then (one of them hot-tempered or having a sore day): kept waiting on a Sunday, overlooked at the dance, the last to hear of a move to the city. The next time they meet, the cooler head comes with a small gift (peppermint sticks, a ribbon, a peach pie, a tin of tobacco) and says sorry where the street can see it, and the other takes it or doesn't by their own nature. Taken, they're sweethearts again and the town talks of it. On the Testing page: "Sweethearts have words" and "Making it up with a gift". Log `ROMANCE ... words | made it up | a gift turned down`; F7 in `TEST romance:`. Not yet seen in game.
- The evening after a wedding at the church, the round bought in that town's saloon is a toast to the newlyweds by name (never by the couple themselves or the Greedy), and the thanks drink to them. Log `KIND round: ... (wedding; ...)`. Not yet seen in game.
- Two old men who meet by chance now and then fall to arguing about the old days: the worst winter, what coffee cost, the town before the railroad, a ridge they remember differently, the old sheriff, who danced with whose wife. Neither gives an inch and it ends in a laugh. On a Sunday afternoon one of the devout may preach in the street, and whoever is near answers by their own faith: an amen, a word, unease, or a jeer. On the Testing page: "Two old men argue about the old days" and "A street preacher". Log `OLD DAYS`, `PREACHER`; F7 `TEST preacher and old days:`. Not yet seen in game.
- More to think after a scene: what two people think of each other after a quarrel, a fistfight, a kindness, a face into the bar, a pocket caught, a fall helped up or walked past, someone stepping in, a debt or a courtship now has 30 lines a kind instead of two or three, and what a passer-by thinks just after seeing a fight, a theft, a drunk, a standoff, a flirt or a quarrel has 13 to 15 instead of one to three (559 new lines).
- A stranded traveller asking the way (`[Encounters] Travellers`, on, `TravellerChance` 20; settings version 75). By day on a road out in the country, a man on foot waves you down and asks the way to the nearest town (said to you, subtitled). Greet him, or stop by him a moment, and he works it out, turns and points, thanks you and walks off that way; antagonize him and he backs off; ride past and he mutters and goes on alone. Once a day at most. On the Testing page: "A traveller asking the way". Log `TRAVELLER`; F7 `TEST traveller:`. Not yet seen in game.
- The night visitor (`[Encounters] NightVisitor`, on, `NightVisitorChance` 18; settings version 74; roadmap 16). After midnight in a town, out of doors and with no lawman near, a pale figure crouched over a body somewhere out of sight. Come near and it rises and stares; look away and it's gone; a little later it stands behind you, and once you've seen it and looked away again, it's gone for good. It can't be hurt and never fights: go for it and it's simply gone. Anyone out late who comes upon it first runs. Next morning the town talks of a body nobody can account for, and the body is gone. Once a night at most, and it counts as one of the night's big moments. On the Testing page: "The night visitor". Log `VISITOR`; F7 `TEST visitor:`. Not yet seen in game.
- The noon callout (part of `[Scenes] Standoffs`). Between half past eleven and half past twelve in a town street, a man calls out another he has bad blood with (past their first quarrel, or a hothead stung by a fresh slight). They square up across the street (guns drawn only when both carry one, no lawman is near and it's not a saloon; otherwise they stare it out), someone watching may call out, and the cooler head backs down. Nobody fires. Once a day at most. On the Testing page: "A man calls another out at noon". Log `CALLOUT`; F7 `TEST callout:`. Not yet seen in game.
- Grudges that travel (`[Scenes] Grudges`, on; settings version 73; roadmap 20). A friend of someone wronged in the last three days (a face into the bar, a pocket picked, a beating, a scorn, being stepped round in the street, being faced down) comes upon the one who did it and has it out with them: the sharp answer back (two hotheaded men come to blows), the cold walk off, the kind say they're sorry. And someone whose friend you killed, who has had word of it, may come up to you in town and say it to your face (the hotheaded and the proud; subtitled), or only stare and think it; once for each death, and never violent from our side. On the Testing page: "A friend takes up a wrong" and "Someone grieving one you killed". Log `GRUDGE`; F7 `TEST grudges:`. Not yet seen in game.
- The drinker after bad news (`[Scenes] Vices`, on; settings version 72; roadmap 19, the first vice). In a saloon from noon on, someone whose trouble ended badly today, or who has just heard a friend has died, drinks it away: a man takes up the drinker's stance where he stands, they sound drunk a little later, and their thought shows over their head (grief names the dead). A friend beside them may come and say they're sorry. They're drunk for the rest of the night, so a drunk's challenge or a pickpocket may find them. On the Testing page: "Someone drinks away bad news". Log `VICE drink:`; F7 `TEST vices:`. Not yet seen in game.
- A round bought (part of `[Scenes] Kindnesses`; roadmap 21). Of an evening in a saloon, someone with cause to be glad (a worry that ended well today, a glad mood, or an outgoing man with money; never the greedy) stands the house a drink: the room looks over and cheers, one of them thanks them, and if you're at the bar you're stood one too (said to you, subtitled), and they think a little better of you. A warm tie and a memory with each of the people treated; the town talks of it. Once a saloon a night at most. On the Testing page: "Someone stands the house a drink". Log `KIND round:`; F7 `TEST kindness:`. Not yet seen in game.
- Standing up to a bully (part of `[Scenes] Kindnesses`; roadmap 21). When a man grabs another to put his face into the bar, or two square up for a fistfight, someone named watching may step in before the blow: a friend of the one picked on most of the time, a steady stranger (Loyal or Proud, never a Coward) now and then. The bully backs off (it ends as words, and the one picked on thanks them) or tells them to keep out and goes on; a hothead backs off less often. Ties and memories for all three: a friend made, and the bully's grudge. On the Testing page: "Someone stands up to a bully". Log `KIND stood up:`; F7 `TEST kindness:`. Not yet built: the bully turning on the one who stepped in. Also: when you help someone up after the one who saw them fall had already walked past, that walking past is still remembered. Not yet seen in game.
- A fall and a helping hand (`[Scenes] Kindnesses`, on; settings version 71; roadmap 21, the first small kindness). Now and then someone walking near you, out of doors on level ground, trips and goes down. The one nearest who saw it decides: a friend always helps them up, an enemy never does (and may say so), a kind stranger stops, the cold mostly walk on, the sharp haul them up gruffly or walk on. The old don't tumble: they sit down hard. It leaves a tie between the two (warm or cold) and memories for both and for anyone watching, and the town may talk of it. Before anyone reaches them you can give them a hand up yourself: walk up while they're down and they thank you (said to you, with a subtitle), think better of you and remember it, the more so if nobody else stopped. Rare: about one a town in twenty minutes at most. On the Testing page: "Someone takes a fall". Log `KIND fall:`; F7 `TEST kindness:`. Not yet seen in game.
- People notice how you look (`[Scenes] SightReactions`, on; settings version 70; roadmap 17d). Bloodied (you lost a good part of your health in one go in the last two game hours and haven't been in the water since), drunk, filthy (the game's dirt level; its scale isn't proven yet, so it's logged) or soaked (in water up to your middle in the last half game hour, and not raining): up to two people within 10 m who can see you look, react with their upper body and think it over their heads, by what they think of you and their nature. Those who are afraid of you, or saw you kill lately, glance and look away; those against you are cold or disgusted; the timid are afraid of blood and drink, the hard size you up, the cold look past you, the kind are concerned, and others are amused or disgusted. Some say something to you in their own recorded voice, with a subtitle, only where the feeling has words for it (the afraid and the cold say nothing). Being drunk in a saloon is nothing to remark on, nor is trail dirt outside the city unless you're caked in it. Rare: a minute between reactions, three a state in a town a day, and each person reacts to each state at most once a game day. A man walking off bloodied from a street fight gets one onlooker's look and thought too. On the Testing page: "Someone reacts to how you look" (looking ordinary, each state in turn). Log `SIGHT`; F7 `TEST sights:` (how you look and the readings behind it). Not yet seen in game.
- People who know each other acknowledge it as they pass (`[Scenes] Acknowledge`, on; settings version 69; roadmap 17, first step). Two people with a tie between them coming toward each other near you, one of them walking, and one in your view: friends nod, tip a hat or wave, and one often says hello in their own recorded voice (good morning or good evening by the hour); enemies give each other a hard look and say nothing (the cold, the proud and the hotheaded may spit as the other goes by); sweethearts look a beat too long; people who only know each other nod. One of them thinks something of it, in their own temper and true to what they did, over their head while you can see them. Looks and upper-body gestures only: nobody is stopped or turned from where they were going. A pair once in 45 game minutes, and 20 seconds between any two. On the Testing page: "Two who know each other acknowledge it" (the nearest pair with a tie within 10 m of each other). Log `NOD`; F7 `TEST passing:`. Not yet seen in game.
- Pacing: one place now decides whether something may happen, so the world stays ordinary most of the time and an encounter still surprises after hours of play. Each kind of moment has its own rarity and rests (since the last of its kind, at that place, for those people), and the big ones (the encounters) share a budget of two a night with a quiet stretch of about twenty real minutes after each. The crying woman, executions and gatherings keep their own chances and hours, and now also wait out that quiet. A test from the Testing page skips it and says so. F7: `TEST pacing:`.
- enRAGE's own people (its nemeses, gang men and named folk) are fully in. Their thoughts weigh what RealNPCs saw them live through with enRAGE's own record of them (what they feel about you and its memories of you, what they've heard, their goals, their mood), so what they think agrees with enRAGE (`[Mind] RecordMinds`). One of them may be the stalker while enRAGE has no use for them, one who hates you likeliest, handed back the moment enRAGE has, after 20 minutes at most (`[Stalkers] StalkerRecords`). When one of them dies of despair, or at the hands of the man he came to blows with in a street fight RealNPCs started, enRAGE's record of them says so in plain words, and despair can reach them again (`[Mind] RecordFates`). RealNPCs waits for enRAGE to deal with the death itself (it may decide he lived: then nothing is written), and marks the death only if enRAGE still hasn't after 30 seconds of play. Only people enRAGE has no hold on (no role of its own but a nemesis going about, no plan of theirs due) may be the stalker or despair; one handed back because enRAGE has just given him orders keeps them. Their clothes are never changed: enRAGE knows its people by their look. Settings version 68.
- What happened, and who saw it: RealNPCs keeps one plain record of every street scene, every deed of yours, every death it knows of, and every crying woman, execution and gathering: who did what to whom, where, when, how it came out, and who witnessed it (in clear sight within 30 m, or within earshot: 60 m for a shot, 25 m for a shout or a fight). Up to 400 are kept, and loading an older save drops the ones after it. The log gives each one (`EVENT ...`, with the debug log on), F7 the last five (`TEST events:`), and the log at load gives the save's size and what is in it (`SAVE ...`). It is what people's memories will be built from next.
- The Testing page has a row for every street scene now: an argument, a fistfight, small talk, gossip, a flirt, a courtship (the answer turns yes, maybe, no each time), a proposal, a drunk calling a man out, a purse lifted, a pickpocket caught, begging, a friend tending the hurt, someone keeping clear of the one who wronged them, a standoff, a song, a tall tale, haggling, a card cheat, two who are waking, and each of the exchanges (a snap at the worst of a worry, good and bad news, an old quarrel, sweethearts, a debt called in, a feud, a courting worry). The nearest named pair that fits plays it now, built exactly as a reason's scene would be; a test notes no tie or memory. Also: a plea at gunpoint (fire it, then aim at someone), a word as you ride into town, your waiting letters handed over (or a test one), a stranger's strange remark, a glitch, and disbelief over someone's head. **Show minds** (`[Testing] ShowMinds`, or its row) puts a small card over the named people within 15 m: who they are and their temper, their mood and worry, their ties and memories, and what is on their mind now. **Replay** fires the last test again with the same seed. Settings version 67.
- The Testing page lists everything RealNPCs can make happen on demand, in groups (encounters, people, street scenes, tools), one row each: Enter fires it (the panel closes, and it fires a second later), Right sets a key for it, Delete leaves it without one. Its keys are in a new `[Testing]` section (`TestKeys` switches them all off; settings version 66): the stalker (`]`), waking someone (`Y`), the execution (`[`) and the crying woman, hermit and gathering keys moved there with their values kept; the talk test (Shift+F7), the rough test (Shift+F9) and the font test (Shift+F2), which were fixed in the code, are settings there now, with those as their defaults. The panel refuses a key RealNPCs or enRAGE already uses, and says whose it is. Every fire logs `TEST fire <id>: started (...; skipped: ...)` or `refused: <why>`; a test skips the hour, place, chance and once-a-day rules, never the safety ones (no story missions, nobody while you have no control). F7: `TEST testing:`.
- A frame-time meter (`[Output] PerfMeter`, on; settings version 65). Every part of RealNPCs is timed each frame, its own time only. F7 writes the ten costliest and the whole add-on's cost (`TEST perf:`). The log says when RealNPCs averages over 1 ms a frame for 10 seconds, or one frame takes over 4 ms (`PERF: over budget`, `PERF: peak`), at most once a minute each, naming what cost most.

### Built 2026-10-26

- Despair with a gun or a knife has an act now (Lex saw two people simply drop). They say their last words looking down at their hands. With a gun they draw it the way anyone draws, aim it at the ground in front of them for a moment, and the shot comes from the gun hand. With a knife the blade set is asked for early, and after the fall they writhe on the ground for a few seconds before going still. The game has no animation of a gun to one's own head, so this is pieced together from what it has. Despair is again never for enRAGE's own records (nemeses, gang men): harm to them waits for a way that tells enRAGE. (That way came the next night: a record's death is booked in enRAGE's own history. See "Built overnight" above.)
- People think about what is on their minds (`[Mind] Minds`, on; settings version 64; Lex's brief, roadmap 4 and 5, first slice). Before, a thought came from a dice roll over kinds of thought, and about half had no cause at all. Now each person keeps memories of what they lived through, and the thought that comes is whatever pulls hardest: a face put into the bar they saw an hour ago, the brawl they were in yesterday, the worry they're living through, the rain, someone in sight, you. Memories fade with time and with being thought about, come back when someone in them walks by, and weigh differently on each temperament: a coward and a hothead who saw the same fight never think the same line about it, and the man whose face went into the bar thinks of it differently from the one who did it. Nobody thinks the same line twice in a day. Strangers who saw something are known by how they looked ("a farmhand"), neighbours by name; up to three passers-by who saw a fight are kept so they can remember it. So far: scenes between people (fights, quarrels, slights, thefts, drunks, kindness, courting, talk), what they saw you do (a killing, a robbery, looting, stopping a thief, breaking up a fight, mercy at gunpoint: the timid never forget it, the cold shrug), deaths (a friend's grief for weeks, once word reaches them an hour or a few on; a foe's uneasy relief; a death seen with their own eyes), your flirting (a yes stays sweet, a no stays sore, a no from fear of you is its own thing, and it comes back to her when you walk by), and being saved from an execution. enRAGE's own people keep memories too, and one that weighs on them is what they think on the ticker. Anyone who saw it counts as a witness, the fleeing and lawmen included. Not yet: the weather turning. With the debug log on, every thought is logged with what it came from (`THOUGHT name: "line" <- source (pull)`); F7 writes `TEST mind:`. `Minds = false` gives the old mix.
- The settings panel no longer crashes on an action row (the memory wipe since it was added, and the Testing page's): the help line read an ini entry action rows don't have, and the panel switched itself off until a reload.
- A Testing page in the settings panel (Ctrl+F7 or Alt+F; Lex): keys to make each encounter happen near you now, out of sight, at any hour: the stalker (`]`), an execution (`[`), and the crying woman, a hermit's camp and a gathering of the awake (`CryingWomanKey`, `HermitKey`, `GatheringKey`, no key as they come; set one with Enter, Delete leaves it without). The same five things, and the memory wipe, are rows you can run from the page with Enter twice. A staged one doesn't use up that night's own; a hermit's camp still never goes in a town, since camps stay. Settings version 63.
- An execution on the road (`[Encounters]` `Executions`, on, `ExecutionChance` 20; settings version 61; Lex, after the game's own event with Zeke Townsend). Away from the towns, once a day at most, one of the nearest town's own regulars kneels at gunpoint, a mark on your map. Why he's there comes from his own life: the debt he's been worrying over (the creditor himself and a hired man, or men he sent, saying the same name and the same eleven dollars as his thoughts), the family he's feuding with, or talking to the law (the region's outlaws: the Lemoyne Raiders in Lemoyne, the Murfree Brood in Roanoke, the O'Driscolls elsewhere). He calls to you, they warn you off, and his thought shows over his head. Shoot or rush them and they fight you (no crime: they were holding a man at gunpoint). Save him and he's fonder of you for good, thinks of it for days, and word gets around of the stranger who saved him. Wait, or ride on, and they shoot him: his death is noted by their hand, his kin hear of it (as grief, never as a grudge against you), and the nearest town hears once he's found. If he vanishes before anyone sees him die, nothing comes of it. They give you 25 seconds once you've seen them (12 once they've warned you), with a last warning said to you a few seconds before; then he tries to get up and the gunman really fires (Lex's second test: the old way, a bullet from nowhere and health 0, showed no shot and looked like a loop). If you never come near, nothing comes of it. To see it now: press [ (`ExecutionKey`; settings version 62), or the settings panel's Testing page, "Stage an execution nearby". Seen working in game, the rescue and the shooting both. CONTENT NOTE: a man can be shot dead in front of you; `Executions = false` turns it off.
- News takes time, and needs someone to carry it (from a player's report: notices from strangers that "a stranger killed my friend", people angry within seconds about a camp of O'Driscolls killed out in the wilds, and a diary on one of them swearing revenge for a man killed 30 seconds before):
  - The corner's notices about people (their thoughts of you, their diaries, word reaching them, letters home, news of their lives) now come only from people you've met: talked to, seen a few times, or courted. Before, anyone with a memory of you counted. What strangers feel still happens; it just stays out of your corner. Several people grieving the same death make one notice a day, and a death's telegram home is one notice, not two.
  - Diary entries are written that evening, at least three game hours after what happened, and only if the writer is still alive.
  - Someone who knows what you did tells others only once they've lived another game hour, so a witness you kill straight after tells nobody.
  - A killing is talked of once someone who saw it has lived an hour to tell it. If everyone who saw it is dead too, or nobody saw it, nobody talks of it until the body would be found (6 to 18 game hours on), and then nobody knows who did it: the rumour says "somebody", the paper says "Nobody saw who did it", and nobody says it to your face.
  - Someone who comes upon the body within two minutes, while you're still within 30 m and in their sight, saw you by it: the killing is seen after all, and talked of an hour on if they live (Lex killed a man out of sight and someone walked up to look 5-10 seconds later).
  - A game hour is about two real minutes, so these are hours in the world, not long waits. enRAGE's own news of a killing reaches the dead man's friends 3 to 48 game hours on whether or not anyone saw; that part is enRAGE's.
- Greet her first (Lex): a woman you greeted (R) in the last three minutes is 15 points likelier to take to your flirting, and flirting cold (H without a greeting) is 15 points less likely. A yes to the flirting is no longer a yes to the night: that has its own chance, better after a greeting (the saloon's women 100 or 80 percent, single women 85 or 55, married ones 70 or 40). Once she says yes, Flirt in the lock-on menu reads "Hook up". If the night's answer is no, she says so when you ask, and a cold approach hears about its manners. CasualHookups' own "Flirt" on B keeps its name: that's its text, which RealNPCs can't change.
- Fixed: after a woman said yes and you asked her somewhere quieter, the screen could stay black with your controls off and the game's spinner turning in the corner. RealNPCs paused itself whenever the screen was dark, so it never faded back in, and its 12-second safety net was paused with it. The night now runs to its end in the dark.
- Flirting has more of the game's own voice: several recorded lines for each answer and each reason (a yes for your money, a no from a married woman, a frightened no, a maybe from the shy), tried in a different order for each woman and every few minutes, so the same woman doesn't always answer with the same recording. Arthur's own line tries the game's second-greeting compliments first. The log says which line played (`FLIRT voice:`), to learn which ones Arthur's voice and each woman's have.
- RealNPCs and CasualHookups (another mod) together (`[Romance]` `CasualHookupsDecides`, on; settings version 60). With CasualHookups.asi in the game folder, a woman's yes to your flirting leaves her willing for five minutes, and CasualHookups' own Flirt takes it from there instead of RealNPCs' fade to black. On a woman who hasn't said yes, its key is held. The first time, RealNPCs moves its Flirt from G, which enRAGE's own lock-on prompts (Interrogate, Give Her a Few Dollars) also use, to B, has her clothes come back after (enRAGE knows its people by their look), and sets it to always give in (0/100/0); a copy of the old file goes to `RealNPCs\backup` first. G is never held, so if its key goes back on G, CasualHookups rolls its own dice again. B is also the satchel key on foot; the first test will show whether pressing it while locked on opens the satchel too. If CasualHookups reads its settings only when the game starts, this counts from the next start. A night through CasualHookups counts as one here, and her thought after shows over her head. Not yet seen in game: whether holding the key stops its prompt (the log says, `HOOKUPS`).
- When a woman says yes and CasualHookups starts nothing after its Flirt (it told Lex "not interested" about Saint Denis' working girls, even set to always give in), she says yes again in her own voice and RealNPCs gives you its own night 10 seconds later, so what she said and what happens agree. RealNPCs remembers which kinds of women CasualHookups turns down (starting with those), and from then on their yes goes straight to its own night, with CasualHookups' key held on them so it can't say otherwise.
- CasualHookups' settings are on the settings panel's Romance page, under "CasualHookups (another mod)": its key (B or G), its three chances (kept adding up to 100), the position it starts in, and what happens after. F7 adds `TEST casual hookups:`.
- RealNPCs keeps its own record of who has died: every death of someone it knows near you (its own people, enRAGE's townsfolk and enRAGE's people), with when, where, how and by whose hand. It's the first step of the NPC mind design, and what people think and say about the dead now asks it first. It follows enRAGE's rule for the knocked out (out cold from fists, a gun butt or being incapacitated isn't dead), which RealNPCs didn't before: a resident knocked out in a brawl used to be counted dead.
- When enRAGE misses the death of one of its townsfolk, RealNPCs tells it (`[Mind]` `ShareWithEnrage`, on; settings version 58). enRAGE looks for deaths every 2 seconds while the body is there; Pope McCrae shot himself a second before the area unloaded, so it never saw, and would have brought him back as if nothing happened. Now he'd be marked dead 4 seconds later. If you load an older save that undoes the death, it's taken back again (enRAGE's own save points usually do this already).
- Loading an older save brings back residents who died after it, as it already did for everything else that hadn't happened yet.
- F7's `TEST ledger:` line; `LEDGER` in the log. It also follows anyone near you who's knocked out: when they go down, and whether they get up again, die where they lie, or are gone before they come to (`LEDGER out cold`, `came to`, `died where they lay`), to learn what the game does with them.
- Women answer your flirting by chance now, not by a flat rule (Lex: Arthur kills so many in self-defence that fear had every woman saying no). The saloon's women always go along; a single woman likely (`WillingSingle`, 70 percent), a married or spoken-for one less so (`WillingTaken`, 20); what she thinks of you, her nature and her day tip the odds (being afraid of you takes about 15 points off instead of being a no). Only having spurned you, being pestered again a moment later, or not being drawn to you at all is a flat no. The same woman answers the same way all day. Men answer as before. The log gives the chance: `FLIRT ... chance 72% (single) -> yes`.
- For testing: the settings panel (Ctrl+F7 or Alt+F), on its Testing page, has "Wipe every NPC's memory". Enter asks first; a second Enter forgets everything RealNPCs keeps of the people in this world for the character you're playing (who they are, what they know and feel about you, their ties and deaths); any other key keeps it all. A copy of the save goes to `RealNPCs\backup`, and enRAGE's own memory is untouched. `WIPE` in the log.
- A night together, off screen (`[Romance]` `Trysts`, on; settings version 59): when a woman says yes, flirt with her again within two minutes to ask her somewhere quieter. The screen goes dark, an hour passes, and nothing is shown; her thought after shows over her head. A first version, to be widened (roadmap).
- enRAGE's own people could despair too for part of the day, when enRAGE wasn't busy with them (Lex lifted the last limit on them, with enRAGE's author's permission); that night it was turned off for its full records again (see the despair act above). enRAGE's townsfolk still can. Counter keepers never do.
- Despair has last words now, in the person's own voice: one of ten of the game's lines that fit (disbelief, a goodbye, trailing off, leaving, things going badly, not knowing, fear, wanting to be let be), a different first choice for each person.
- The fully awake say the game's own line of disbelief, with a thought of their own over their head, when they first come to you and when they see someone die near them (Lex heard a bystander say it at a death).
- More fights in the street and the saloon, each for a reason you can see (Lex saw none all day: of 38 scenes in the log, 2 were fights). Two hot-tempered men (hotheads or proud) with bad blood come to blows at their first quarrel, and so does a hot one with drink in him; a quarrel picks up again after 45 game minutes instead of two hours. In a saloon at night, a hot-tempered man with drink in him, or facing a drunk, starts a fistfight about one night in three, or roughs the other up; a coward only gets words. A hot man at the worst of his week who snaps at another hot man now and then gets a fist back. The two who fought sit out 15 minutes before their next scene, so one drunk doesn't take on the whole room.
- Walking over for a scene: whoever isn't sitting or leaning walks to the other (a man at the bar was too often left waiting for), and across a bar or a table is close enough to begin.
- Every write RealNPCs makes into enRAGE is listed (CLAUDE.md, "Writing to enRAGE"). One that set a field by name now says so in the log if an enRAGE update renames it, instead of quietly doing nothing, and the past someone tells you is saved by enRAGE at once. The readmes now say accurately what RealNPCs changes in enRAGE.
- No licence file, on purpose: all rights reserved, with a notice at the end of both readmes.

## 0.6.2

- Trouble between people, played out in front of you, each time for a reason (street scenes; none of it seen in game yet, and each can be switched off on its own, `[Scenes]`, settings version 53):
  - Rough handling (`RoughHandling`): when two men who hate each other come to blows, and one of them is hot-tempered, proud or cold, he may grab the other and smash his face into the nearest table, bar, piano or wall instead, with the game's own move for it, picked to fit what's there. A hothead with drink in him now and then does it to a stranger in his way in a saloon. With nothing to hand it stays a fistfight. The victim swings back, walks off promising more, or runs, by his own nature. Those watching gasp.
  - Pickpockets among the townsfolk (`NpcThieves`): someone short of money (a debt, their savings, the bank, the drink) or light-fingered by nature (greedy, a drifter, dishonest) robs a well-dressed or drunk mark in passing, mostly unnoticed. Sometimes the mark finds it gone a moment later, and sometimes he catches the hand at once and the thief runs (catch him for the town's thanks). Never the loyal or the honest.
  - Drunks who call men out (`DrunkChallenges`): a drunk in a saloon at night picks a man, squares up with the game's own drunken moves, and falls on his face before a punch is thrown. He comes to a while later.
  - Begging: an honest person in the same money trouble asks a well-dressed stranger for a coin instead, and is given one or sent off, by the stranger's own nature.
  - Keeping clear: someone timid who was roughed up or robbed crosses the street to avoid the one who did it.
  - A small kindness: a friend of someone just roughed up or robbed goes to them, sees to the damage or offers a meal, and the two are closer for it.
  - Afterwards the two are tied by it, and know who did what: the one who did it and the one it was done to think about it differently, a lifted purse is a mystery to its owner, and the town talks about the face in the bar or the thief caught at it. Those who keep diaries write the day's scene down, from their side of it.
- People react to what's done and said to them (`[Scenes]` `Reactions`, on by default; settings version 53; not seen in game yet). In a street scene, a moment into each line, the one it's said to looks at the speaker and takes it by their own nature: a hothead or a proud man bristles at a threat or a jeer, a coward flinches, the cold shake their heads, the kind look down when they're mocked, a greeting or a kind word gets a nod, a drunk's ramble a puzzled look, and the everyday now and then a shrug. A man whose face has just gone into the bar reels as he cries out (the game's own street-fight hurt move where the game has it, else a wince). Someone leaning on the bar or sitting only turns their head, and nobody reacts when it's their own turn to speak.
- What's going on around them is on people's minds. A scene that just played out in front of them, the other people in sight, the weather if they're out in it, and the place they're in now come up in their thoughts. Who they're looking at is seen through their own nature: the poor envy the well-dressed, the kind pity the drunk, the timid keep an eye on a hothead, the proud sneer, the greedy size people up. Anyone known lately for putting a man's face into the bar, or for being caught with a hand in a coat, is thought of as that. And now and then a small pleasure or an old memory: the smell of bread, the first coffee, a tune stuck in the head.
- Self-defence lasts as long as the fight. It used to run out a minute and a half after your last punch or shot, even if the man who started it was still in the fight, drawing a gun on you or held at your gunpoint; whatever the game charged after that stood. Now it lasts until he's out of it, and a minute and a half after that.
- The ticker is clearer. It now shows only the thoughts of people you can see (on screen, in plain view), one at a time with a short pause between them (`TickerGapSeconds`, 3), and a small gold mark appears over the person whose thought it is (`ThoughtMarker`). With nobody in view thinking, the bar goes away instead of filling up with old news and a world summary. The ticker key on a thought moves to the next thought.
- The news has a box of its own: "Around town", in the top right under the "Sore at you" panel. Rumours, life updates, gang and faction news, diaries and what people elsewhere think of you show there one at a time, each for a few seconds (longer for a longer one, and paused while a menu is open), then the box goes away. `NewsTo` and `WarNewsTo` take a new choice, `corner`, now the default; an ini still on the old defaults (`ticker`, `right`) is moved over and the log says so. Settings version 38. Seen in game.
- No more white text. RealNPCs drew its subtitles and notices on the game's objective line, the white text at the bottom of the screen, and it showed words the voice wasn't saying (or no voice at all). Now it draws none:
  - Subtitles look like the game's own now: white serif text in a dark box, centred just above the ticker, where the game shows its own (Lex: "move our mod dialogue to this existing subtitle"). The game gives mods no way to put text into its own subtitles, so RealNPCs draws them the same way, and the plain white objective line isn't used for them any more. A test key, Shift+F2, shows the line in twelve of the game's fonts for 20 seconds, numbered, to find the face the game's subtitles use (`FONT test` in the log); line 1, `$body`, was closest, and the subtitles use it.
  - Subtitles only for what's said to you, and only while the voice is playing. What people say to Arthur shows on the game's subtitle line while their recorded voice plays: the recording's own words when the word list knows them, else what they mean. A line whose voice doesn't play gets none, and talk you only overhear (street scenes, conversations between others, mutters) never does. The game's own Subtitles option has to be on too. `[Output]` `Subtitles` is `on` (the default; `matched` becomes it) or `off`; settings version 47. Each one is logged (`SUBTITLE Name: "..." (the recorded words | the written line)`). The game gives mods no way into its own dialogue subtitles: a test played one greeting eight ways (to you, to another person, to nobody, with each of the speech call's flags, and the game's scripted-speech way) and the game subtitled none of them, so its subtitle line it is.
    - Notices (the start-up line, F7's status, hints with the ticker off, a test key's answer, news sent to the game feed) are the game's own updates at the top left, or the "Around town" box if the game won't take them.
  - Gang and faction news goes to the game's own updates at the top left by default (`WarNewsTo = feed`; an ini on the old `corner` or `right` is moved to it, and `corner` or `ticker` can still be chosen). The log line is `WORLD NEWS (feed)`.
  - `NewsTo` keeps `corner`, `ticker` and `gamefeed`; `subtitles` and `enrage` (both white text) become `corner`. `NoticesAsSubtitles` and `OverheardTalk` (its captions were white text) are gone. Settings version 46.
  - enRAGE's territory changes (a gang gaining or losing its hold on a district) come up as top-left updates too (`[Output]` `TerritoryNews`, on; settings version 51), where `WarNewsTo` says. RealNPCs reads them from enRAGE's own news list every two seconds and skips the ones enRAGE keeps quiet (catching the world up after a load). enRAGE still prints its own white copy while its `[Menu] Messages` is 3; set it to 2 (speech and deaths) or 4 (deaths only) in NemesisMod.ini, or on the enRAGE page of the settings panel, to stop that. The log line is `TERRITORY (feed): ...`.
  - The notice that an avenger has come west for you is a top-left update now; it used to go through enRAGE's notice, as white text.
  - Not changed: the rest of enRAGE's own white text (its messages, `[Menu] Messages`, and world news, `[World] WorldNews`, in NemesisMod.ini) is drawn by enRAGE itself.
- White subtitles were a setting before this (`[Output]` `Subtitles`): `matched` (the default) shows one only when the words of the speaker's recorded voice are known (`voices\words.txt`), so the subtitle says what you hear; `on` shows every line, as before; `off` shows none. The voice plays either way. In every mode, a street-scene line, an awake person's words or a host's muttering gets a subtitle only while the speaker is on screen and in plain view. Lines labelled as heard from out of sight ("somewhere in the dark", "somewhere behind you") keep theirs. On `matched`, what's said to you keeps its subtitle all the same: what people tell you when you greet them, remarks as you pass, insults, flirting, pleas at gunpoint, and the night encounters' words to you. Only overheard talk (street scenes, two strangers flirting, weddings, mourners, a host muttering) needs its real words, and with the word list as short as it is, that means few of those subtitles for now. Settings version 39.
- When enRAGE has given someone a name (its name card), RealNPCs uses that name too, on the ticker and in street scenes. Before, a passer-by enRAGE called Jeb Cooley could think on the ticker as "Milo Fenton".
- The gold mark over the person thinking is bigger and sits a little lower, so enRAGE's name card doesn't cover it.
- CONTENT NOTE. Despair no longer needs a gun on them (`AwakeningDespair`; Lex: in Valentine none of the fully awake did it). Anyone fully awake who can't bear it now does it the way that fits them: with a gun if they carry one, or if they're the kind who keeps one (a farmer, a trapper, a hard man, a card player, the old, a gentleman; each with the gun that fits: the plain Cattleman, the LeMat for a hard man, the Volcanic for a card player, the Schofield for a gentleman, a soldier or the law, the small double-action for a lady), else by running at a wall when there's one near, going limp against it, else with a knife. Nothing is put in their hand until the last moment, and anything given is taken back if it doesn't happen. RealNPCs' own residents and enRAGE's townsfolk (Lex, after Carl Lander knelt twice: enRAGE marks a townsperson dead however they die, and blames you only when the game names you as the cause, which it doesn't here); never enRAGE's own people (its nemeses and gang men), never a counter keeper, never with you within 3 m or aiming at them. Everyone gets a fresh roll, once each: before, an unarmed person who rolled it knelt instead and their one chance was spent. The log says why it isn't rolled for someone (`WAKE despair: not for Jeb Humphries (one of enRAGE's townsfolk: only RealNPCs' residents)`), the roll, the way and how it went; F7's `TEST despair:` lists the fully awake within 30 m, which way each is from where you're looking ("11 m behind you to the left") and what would happen; F7 also marks them on the map for a minute with their name and what they'd do, and names the nearest who could on screen. Settings version 56 rewrites the ini's note for `AwakeningDespair` (values kept). Seen in game 2026-10-26: the knife (Henry Crandall, Esther Lowry) and a gun (Pope McCrae, one of enRAGE's townsfolk, with a small double-action); the wall not yet.
- Thoughts above their heads (`[Ticker]` `ThoughtsOverHeads`, on, and `ThoughtsOverHeadScale`, 0.24; settings version 57; Lex: "move the subtitles to above the NPCs head in a smaller font"). The thought of the person whose turn it is shows over them in small type, in a dark box, instead of on the bar at the bottom; the news, the summary, and a thought whose thinker has gone off screen stay on the bar. People count as near within 20 m now instead of 30 (`TickerNearbyMetres`; an ini still on 30 is moved to 20, a value of your own is kept). F7's `TEST thoughts:` counts them. Seen in game (Lex: "looking much better").
- People flirt with each other and court (`[Romance]` `NpcFlirting`, on; settings version 55; Lex: "allow them to flirt with each other and develop relationships"). Two people near you with nothing between them yet: one, free and drawn to the other, makes eyes at them now and then ("This seat taken, Belinda? It is now."), and the other answers the way people answer you, by what they make of the one asking and who they are: their nature, their faith, their troubles and their day, set against how the one asking looks (dressed like money or not) and whether they've lately been known for a face in the bar or a hand in a coat. A yes or a not-sure starts a romance the two remember: when they meet again it goes on to sweet words, and in time one asks the other. A no is a brush-off. Nobody spoken for flirts (a sweetheart, someone they're courting, one of enRAGE's own affairs), only grown-ups no more than fifteen years apart, and only a man and a woman unless `SameSexRomance` is on, which now lets same-sex sweethearts carry on courting too. Since what people say to each other has no subtitle, you see a courtship through their thoughts on the ticker, if they're in view: the one asking as they work up to it ("Don't stammer. Whatever you do, don't stammer."), the other as they answer ("Kind eyes on Earl. Never noticed before."). A yes or a maybe keeps the two (RealNPCs' own people are saved then), so the romance is remembered: their thoughts and the town's talk follow it, as they already did for sweethearts. The log says `ROMANCE Earl (Drifter) makes eyes at Belinda (Proud): no, beneath`; F7's `TEST romance:` counts them.
- enRAGE's own people join in (`[Scenes]` `EnragePeopleJoinIn`, on; settings version 54; Lex: "relax all rules relating to using enRAGE full records"). Its nemeses, gang men and the people it has named used to be left to look and think only. Now they take part like anyone else, under their own names: in street scenes and the trouble between people, in reactions (bristling, flinching, a head shake), in conversations in real voices, and in the waking acts. Not while enRAGE has them busy: in a fight, on one of its jobs or patrols, hunting you, riding, or while it questions someone. They're still never re-dressed (enRAGE knows its people by their look), never driven to despair, and never made into stalkers. One rule now decides, for every feature, who RealNPCs may direct: never the story's own people, children, or anyone during a story mission. F7's `TEST records:` lists enRAGE's people near you and why any of them is left out.
- Whether someone takes to your flirting comes from what they think of you, the same judgement their thoughts about you on the ticker come from, so the two always agree: smitten says yes; someone you did a kindness, or who's fond of you, warms to it; someone who's only met you, or a stranger, is unsure ("Is that so?"); someone who saw or heard what you did, holds a grudge, is afraid of you or was scorned by you says no. Their own day counts too: a bad turn today makes them sour, a good one sweeter, a worry at its worst means not today, and someone spoken for (a sweetheart, or courting) turns you down. Who they are weighs as much (Lex: "a lot of personal influence"), from the same profile their own thoughts come from: a hothead likes a man with a name for trouble and the timid are frightened by it; the proud want a man of means and the greedy look at what's in your pocket (your cash); the loyal warm to the good the town says you did and cool at the bad; a drifter likes a man who doesn't stay put; the devout won't have a known killer; the outgoing are readier, the shy hang back with a stranger; a widow is lonelier, a drinker in a saloon looser, someone with a sense of humour more playful; money worries make your money matter, a feud makes them short-tempered, a secret or trouble with the law keeps their head down, and someone waiting on a letter is waiting on somebody else. Their answer says what weighed most ("A man with money in his pocket. Well, now.", "I'm a churchgoing woman, mister, and I know what you've done.", "Sweet talk don't pay the rent, mister."). Every reply has a voice that fits it now: a "maybe" with no clear tone of its own (Dorothea Shipley's "Hm. We'll see.") used to go unheard. No more dice: the same person in the same state answers the same way. It holds for enRAGE's people, enRAGE's townsfolk, and RealNPCs' own regulars (who used to answer by chance). The log line says it all: `FLIRT Rena Huff (townsfolk; thinks of you: met, Drifter, sore today -> no (sore); ...)`.
- Flirting answers the moment you ask. Pressing Flirt (H in the lock-on menu, or F2) did nothing while anyone else's line was waiting to be said, and with people talking nearby that was most of the time. Now your flirt and the reply go ahead of everything waiting. If the game turns the reply's voice down for a moment (the person busy), it's tried again, up to four times, as conversations already do. Arthur's own lines get no subtitle (only what's said to him does), here and in conversations. The log says why a flirt was ignored (`FLIRT ignored: ...`), and when a line to you could not be voiced (`VOICE: ...`).
- Flirt is in the game's lock-on menu (`[Romance]` `FlirtPrompt`, on; settings version 52). Locked on to someone you could flirt with (a grown-up, within 8 m, not fighting, not one of the story's own people), the menu with Greet and Antagonize also offers Flirt, on H (D-pad down on a pad). A menu entry can only show one of the game's own controls, and none is on F2, so it's H there; F2 still works. Pressing it flirts as F2 does. The log says `FLIRT prompt pressed: name`. Flirting also leaves the story's own people alone now.
- The flirt key is F2 now (`FlirtKey`; settings version 50). It was F6, which is the game's camera, and for one version 5, which takes out the melee item from the item wheel. An ini on either is moved to F2; a key of your own is kept.
- The settings key is easier to find. Alt+F11 only opens the RealNPCs panel on enRAGE 0.9.5.3; on other enRAGE versions it opens enRAGE's own, and some players couldn't find a way in. Now, about 15 seconds after loading, a notice says so and names the keys that always work (`SettingsKey`, Ctrl+F7, and the new `SettingsKey2`, Alt+F). When Alt+F11 does work, the keys are mentioned once, the first time. The log says which case applied (`SETTINGS key:`). Either key also closes the panel again (before, only Backspace, Escape or Alt+F11 did). The READMEs list Ctrl+F7 first. Settings version 44.
- The settings panel (Ctrl+F7 or Alt+F): the mouse wheel moves up and down the list, one row a notch, as the Up and Down keys do.
- Conversations in their real voices (work in progress). About every two minutes (`[Scenes]` `RealVoiceTalk`, `RealVoiceTalkSeconds` 120), two people standing together near you, at least one in plain view, have a word in their real recorded voices. They talk about the weather when it's worth a word (a fine day by daylight; rain, storm or snow), ask how the other is getting on, pass on a piece of news and react to it, or say hello. Each then rests five minutes and the pair fifteen, and the two remember that they talked. Settings version 41. Seen working in game: "Sure is a fine day" and its answer between two Valentine townsfolk, each in their own voice, and two pieces of gossip with their matching reactions.
- Those conversations now fit the moment. When a band of riders goes by within 40 m of you (three or more riding together), two people standing near you remark on them straight away, without waiting for their turn (at most every three minutes). Now and then someone tells a story about the town they're in, if their voice has one (the farmhands at Emerald Ranch do). Sweethearts (a couple whose street scenes went warmly, or someone courting the other) pay each other compliments. And the game's crude remarks are in: a man's to a woman in a saloon of an evening, which she cuts short, and between sweethearts late at night, which she takes well. Each is answered with the game's own reply to it where it has one. Content note: these are the game's own recorded lines, as crude as the game makes them. `[Scenes]` `CrudeTalk` (on by default) leaves them out. Settings version 42. Heard in game in the Valentine and Rhodes saloons at night: a townsman's remark to one of the saloon girls, and her telling him to cut it out. The riders, the town stories and the sweethearts' lines haven't been seen yet.
- The test key (Shift+F7) now tries whatever the moment allows first, without leaving it to chance, and prefers a pair the moment suits (sweethearts, or a man and a woman in a saloon of an evening). The log lists everyone near you and why anyone can't take part.
- A test key for rough handling: Shift+F9 plays the whole scene between two men near you: words, then one grabs the other and smashes his face on whatever is at hand (a bar, a table, a piano, or a wall; it looks for a surface by the man and picks the angle of the move that drives him into it), his cry, and his answer, which turns with each press from swinging back, to walking off promising more, to running. It leaves no grudge between them. Later it happens on its own, between people who have a reason. F7 now also lists who is at the bar near you.
- Fewer conversations stop after the first line. The game sometimes turns a line down for a moment though the voice has it (in a busy saloon especially), and that used to end the conversation, often before the answer. Now the line is tried again, up to five times over about three seconds.
- A test key, Shift+F7, has the two people closest to each other near you trade the game's own paired lines at once. The second walks over first if they're apart; one asks how the other is getting on and the other answers, each in their real recorded voice, the answer only once the first has finished. New setting `OverheardTalk` (none, the default, or captions): with captions, a line whose words aren't known gets a short note in RealNPCs' own words. A voice the word list knows shows its real words. Settings version 40. Not yet seen working in game.

## 0.6.1

- Built for enRAGE 0.9.5.3. Everything RealNPCs reads from enRAGE is still there. enRAGE's new settings (such as TownsfolkReactions) show on its pages in the RealNPCs panel (Alt+F11). Played on: every hook found, no errors on either side.
- Standing still no longer holds up enRAGE's funerals or someone's evening at the saloon. Since enRAGE 0.9.5.3, its calm (which RealNPCs asks for while you stand still) also makes planned events wait, so a funeral could wait for as long as you stood at the graveside. Now, while one of those is due near you, RealNPCs doesn't ask for the calm and it goes ahead. Gang plans and ambushes are still held.
- Aiming at one of enRAGE's outlaws (a gang man, or a man in an outfit's colours) no longer has him plead with his hands up. Since enRAGE 0.9.5.3 he stands his ground unless he's scared of you, and his crew draws on you, so RealNPCs leaves him to enRAGE. Ordinary townsfolk still plead as before.
- Bars under people near you show how they feel about you: their fear of you, how much they like you, and their anger at you, as enRAGE keeps them. The nearest three in plain view within 15 m who feel something get them. A meter in the top right, "Sore at you", lists the strangers whose grudge against you is building, with a mark where they'd become a nemesis, and the nemesis who's after you now. Both are on by default and each can be turned off (`[Display]`: `FeelingBars`, `FeelingBarsMetres`, `GrudgeMeter`; `FeelingBarsShowEmpty`, off, also shows the empty part of each bar; settings version 37). They hide under menus and while enRAGE's own debug overlay is up. Seen in game.

- Each town keeps its own people (`[World]`: `Regulars`, `RegularsPerTown` 30, `RegularsDress`; settings version 32). When the game puts an ordinary person in the street where you can't see them, they become one of the town's regulars. It's either someone met there before, of the same build and back in their own clothes, or, while the town has room, someone new with an outfit of their own. Their names, worries and lives go on between visits, and the ones you've met know you again ("Him again. Same hat."). Nobody changes clothes in front of you: only people who haven't been on screen since they appeared, 20 m or more off and out of sight, are dressed. Nobody enRAGE has already named or fingerprinted is touched, since enRAGE knows people by their look. With `RegularsDress` off they keep their names and lives but not their looks. Seen in game in Valentine; whether a regular's face always comes back with their outfit isn't confirmed yet.
- Up to three ordinary people near you now get turns on the ticker, not just the nearest one.
- Street scenes happen for a reason, and where you can see them. Instead of the two people standing closest to each other, it's the two near you with the strongest reason to have something to do with each other:
  - a quarrel between them goes on, and between two hot-tempered men it comes to blows;
  - sweethearts flirt, and after long enough one asks (never at night);
  - someone who owes money gets it called in;
  - someone at the worst of a worry is short-tempered, and someone whose trouble ended well is easy company;
  - two people who are waking talk strangely;
  - the town talks over what you did.
  If they're a few steps apart, one turns and the other walks over. At least one of them must be on your screen and in plain view. A pair's story moves on each time they meet instead of repeating. Anyone who has just had a scene sits out two minutes, and a pair who fought rests a full day. With nobody near having a reason, a chance meeting still happens now and then, at a third of the pace, also only where you can see it.
- Short exchanges written for what people are going through, so what you see matches what they've been thinking:
  - Someone at the worst of a worry snaps when spoken to, and says what's wrong, with the same names their thoughts use: "Not now. Ada's burning up, and the doctor wants two dollars to ride out."
  - Someone whose trouble just ended tells it, good or bad: "Fever broke! Ada sat up and asked for eggs."
  - The other person answers from their own temper and troubles: kindly ("Lord. I'm sorry, Elwood. If there's anything at all."), coldly ("Everybody's got troubles."), or sharply when they're hot-tempered or at the worst of their own ("No call to bite my head off.").
  - A quarrel picks up again and can be made up, which mends things between them, or it ends with a dare.
  - Sweethearts greet each other.
  - How it went is remembered between them and shows in their thoughts ("Asked after my trouble. Meant it, too."), and the town talks about quarrels, courting and the ones who made it up.
  - Scenes close to you count for more when picking.
- Worries have real people at the other end. When someone with a name who can be met again owes money, is feuding with a neighbour or is courting, the other person is one of their town's regulars: a man for a debt or a feud, someone of the other sex and a fitting age for courting, and someone they already have history with first. Their thoughts name that person ("Owe Whitlow eleven dollars..."), and that person thinks about it from their side ("Clara keeps finding reasons to pass my door. I've noticed. I let it go on."), through to how it ends. When the two are near each other, that comes before anything else, and the second one walks over (up to 18 m) and nothing is said until they're face to face:
  - The man owed calls in the same eleven dollars, and the answer moves with the worry: "soon", then "a week, just a week", then "Saturday, I swear it". At the end it's "Here. Eleven dollars. Count it.", or bitterness over the mule.
  - Neighbours go at it over the fence post, then the dog in the hens, then a threat, and at the end they shake on it or stop talking for good.
  - A courtship goes from a nervous good morning, to an offer to carry something, to "there's a thing I've been meaning to ask". The other one warms to it only if it's going to end in a yes, and at the end it's a yes or a kind no.
- People notice. When a scene plays, those standing nearby turn their heads to look. Sometimes one of them with a name calls out once, from what's between them and the two in it and from their own temper:
  - A friend of one takes their side: "Don't let Simon talk to you like that, Ezra."
  - A friend of both tries to calm it: "Leave it, the pair of you. Not in the street."
  - Someone who dislikes both eggs it on.
  - The cold want it gone: "Somebody fetch the sheriff before this gets stupid."
  - A fight draws a cheer or a cry for help.
  - A courtship draws a smile ("About time, the pair of you.") or a tut ("In broad daylight. Lord.").
  - Bad news draws a "Poor soul."
- Someone reading the paper aloud no longer answers good news ("...sent both men home without a shot fired") as if it were a killing. They no longer say "Thank God it wasn't here" about something that happened in the town they're standing in.
- People make the gestures that go with what they say: pointing and head-shaking in a quarrel, a wave or a hat tip in greeting, laughing at a joke.
- Street scenes use the names the people really go by: enRAGE's name for its townsfolk, a resident's or regular's own name for anyone else. The name over each line and the names inside the lines now agree; before, "Cole" could say "You've been staring, Hank" to someone labelled "Ada". When the fight in a scene goes the other way from the script, the names in the lines still to come swap with the people.
- Street scenes leave a trace. When two people who can be met again (town regulars, enRAGE's townsfolk) quarrel, come to blows, argue over money, flirt or just talk, it's remembered between them: how warm or hostile, how many times. For four days they think about each other by name ("Jaw still aches. Simon hits like a mule kicks."), and for two days the rest of their town talks about it ("Simon Walsh and Ezra Whitlow came to blows. Over nothing, they say."). The ties are seen being made in game (the log's TIE lines); the thoughts about them aren't confirmed on screen yet.
- When one of the two has died since, the other's thoughts turn to that: guilt after a quarrel or a fight ("Last thing I ever gave Abner was trouble. Now Abner's dead, and I can't take it back."), grief for a friend or sweetheart. Before, someone could still be thinking "Abner hits like a mule kicks" about a man who died just after the fight. The town talks about the death instead of the fight. A worry over a debt, a feud or a courtship whose other person has died is set aside: no more thoughts or scenes about it.
- F7 shows the town regulars around you on the map for a minute (grey marks with their names), and names the nearest one on screen with how far and which way.
- Rumours on the ticker keep a name's capital: "Heard Arthur Morgan broke up a fight", not "Heard arthur Morgan".

- Ordinary people have lives of their own now (everyone the add-on gives a name to, and the people at the counters; enRAGE's own people and anyone already waking think as before). Each one is going through something: a debt to a man in town, a sick child, a letter that doesn't come, a dry well, a son gone to the mines or the army, a courtship, a feud over a fence, the bank's note, the drink, money saved for a ticket west, a bad tooth, a secret, a poster that looks too much like them. Which troubles fit depends on their trade and age. Each one runs about eight game days in their thoughts: it begins, gets worse, comes to a head, and ends well or badly, with the same named people all through. Then something else begins, and for a day or two the last one is still on their mind. The people at the counters show it best: visit the gunsmith over a few days and watch his week go by.
- Thoughts also follow the day: the meal that's late, the small hours, payday and bath night on Saturday, Sunday collars, wash day. They follow the year: 1899 with the century about to turn, or the epilogue's 1907, with motorcars and telephones. And they follow what you did in town: for three days after a killing, a robbery or a stolen horse, people there think about it (nobody names you).
- Three more thoughts for each kind of person (farmhands, townsfolk, the old, gentry, labourers, card players, rough sorts, trappers, working girls, soldiers, lawmen and everyone else). About 270 new lines in all, every one checked against enRAGE's text. Seen in game.
- The ticker gives more room to what people are thinking:
  - The person right beside you (within about 3.5 m) gets every other turn, with a new thought about every 30 seconds. Others get one about every minute and a half; before, the same thought came back for about 20 real minutes.
  - enRAGE's townsfolk have lives of their own too. When they don't feel strongly about you, they mostly think about their own worries and their day, instead of everyone thinking "Him. We've spoken." Strong feelings (a grudge, fear, a sweetheart, a favour, something they saw you do) still come first most of the time.
  - Ordinary people and counter keepers still wonder what's real (at your `RealityChance`), but town rumours and talk about you now take about a third of what's left, not most of it.
  - The nearest ordinary passer-by gets a turn even when people with names are near.
- A townsperson thinking back on something they said to you no longer comes out as "Thought him should know"; it's "Thought he should know".
- F7 also logs `TEST worries` (what the people near you are going through) and `TEST near` (who the ticker counts as near, and what each would think right now).
- CONTENT NOTE: a sick child doesn't always get better. About a third of those worries end with a burial.

- The stalker's odd acts, from the 0.6.0 log. 22 of the 28 emotes were Online ones that Story Mode doesn't have, so he never made them. In their place are the gestures townsfolk make as they talk: a hat tip, a hand up for quiet, a slow head-shake, spitting, a nod, laughing at nothing, a salute and more, 21 in all for men and women. They are played on the upper body only, so he should stay standing; if the game doesn't report them playing that way, they are tried over the whole body, and the log says which. The three Online emotes Story Mode does have are kept (the bow, pointing and laughing, the jig). At load the log says which animation sets the game has (`STALKER acts:`, also in the F7 dump); in game, all four gesture sets are there. Seen playing in game.
